# Changelog

All notable changes to coding.sh are listed here. The format follows
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and versions will
follow [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/) from the first npm release.

Each release opens with a short summary. The list under it has one line per change,
grouped by area, with links to the docs for the details.

## [0.1.8] - Unreleased

### Added

- **Compare.** `compare --signals` reads each signal within each arm too: `--json` adds `byArm` with each arm's AUROC, pairs, tasks and CI, the table prints one line per arm, and a signal whose arms' CIs sit on opposite sides of 0.5 is `differs-by-arm` instead of `no-signal`. [Eval methods](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/eval-methods.md)
- **Work.** `coding.sh status` says how old the index is (`Index  updated 4m ago`). When no ingest has finished for 15 minutes and none runs, `work` judges waits and stalls as of the last ingest and says so in its header, so a stopped ingest no longer looks like a fleet of stalled agents. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#an-old-index)
- **Tool evals.** Each arm summary counts silent failures, failed trials with no rejected call, failed call or retry, and target calls per success. The arm table prints both when an arm has them. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#6-metrics)
- **Capture.** The Claude Code status line shows how full the context window is (`ctx 62%`, `ctx 87% full` from 85%) and the Claude apps gateway spend limit (`spend 63%`). `quota --json` lists a `spend` row with `usedUsd`, `limitUsd` and `period`, and `quota wait --window spend` waits on it. [Agents](https://coding.sh/docs/agents#status-line)
- **Evals.** Gemini judges, grades and monitors (`"provider": "gemini"`, `monitor add --provider gemini`) with `GEMINI_API_KEY`. `coding.sh monitor add <name> --http <URL>` scores each new session with your own service: a POST signed with a secret printed once, retried on 5xx, 408, 425, 429 or a network error; a 2xx `{"value"}` answer becomes the score. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#monitors)
- **Work.** Each `coding.sh work` item has a stage (planning, executing, review, approval or done), a kind (research or change) and a line of evidence. The header counts items per stage, and `--stage approval` lists every item in that stage, hidden ones too. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#stages-and-kinds)
- **Work.** Each work item names the pull requests its sessions opened, merged, closed or linked, read from their own tool calls: `work` prints "PR #12 merged" on the item's line, `work show` lists each with its URL, and `work --json`, the MCP `work` tool and the Work page carry them. Sessions get `stats.prs`. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#work-items)
- **OTel.** The OTel receiver keeps the trace tags an SDK sends on a span or its resource: each tag becomes a `tag:<name>` session flag, so `--where 'flag:tag:prod'` finds the session. At most 20 tags of 64 characters each. [OpenTelemetry](https://coding.sh/docs/otel#public-path)
- **MCP.** A `work` tool pages the queue `coding.sh work --json` prints, with the index's age, which `status` now carries too. `session_current` finds a running subagent or worktree delegate, `sessions_list` counts the child sessions it leaves out (`hiddenChildren`), and a `session_get` tail cursor past 20,000 events keeps its place while the session grows. [MCP](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp)
- **Evals.** `coding.sh compare` prints claim rate, claim precision and silent failures per arm: how often the final answer says the work is done, how often the verifier agrees, and how often it claimed success on a failed run. The rows and `claims` in `--json` appear only when some arm claims. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab)
- **Cost.** Plan usage for the 5-hour and weekly windows: the Claude Code status line ends with `5h 62% 7d 41%` when Claude Code sends it, `coding.sh quota` prints each agent's windows with the reset time, `quota wait` blocks until a window has room, and the MCP `status` tool includes the same rows. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli/quota)
- **Cost.** `coding.sh usage --by mcp`, `--by skill` and `--by subagent` show what each MCP server, skill tag and subagent type costs; an MCP server's cost is its result tokens at the session model's input price. Every `--json` row has `"basis": "attributed"`. [Track cost](https://coding.sh/docs/cost#cost-by-mcp-server-skill-or-subagent)
- **Cost.** `coding.sh budget set day|week|month <usd>` sets a spend budget for every session, one agent or one repo, and `budget status` shows the estimated spend, limit, percent and reset. The status line adds `budget 82%` past 80%, and `proxy --enforce-budget` answers 429 to a call over a budget. [Track cost](https://coding.sh/docs/cost#set-a-budget)
- **Cost.** `coding.sh budget set day 5 --where 'user:alice'` gives the sessions a query matches a budget of their own, with their subagents' cost; `budget status --json` adds `where`. The status line counts only day budgets on session keys, and `proxy --enforce-budget` never applies them. [Track cost](https://coding.sh/docs/cost#set-a-budget)
- **Cost.** `usage --cache`, `show` and the MCP `usage` tool split the estimated cost into cache reads, cache writes, uncached input and output, with each part's share; `usage --json` adds `estimatedCostByTokenType`. Reported and gateway-billed costs are not split and are named apart. [Track cost](https://coding.sh/docs/cost#cost-by-token-type)
- **Alerts.** `coding.sh alert add` sets a threshold on failure rate, cost, sessions or one flag over the last hour, day or week. Each change of state sends a signed JSON POST to a webhook or the same JSON to a command. `alert check` runs from cron, and `coding.sh serve` runs it every minute. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli/alert)
- **Alerts.** `coding.sh alert add --measure score:<name>` alerts on the mean of a numeric or boolean session score over the window, and `--under` with `--warn-under` fires when the value falls below the threshold, so a falling eval score notifies. The webhook body adds `direction: "under"` for these alerts. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli/alert)
- **Scores.** `coding.sh monitor add <name> --judge rubric.md` or `--run "<command>"` scores each session once after it ends, in `coding.sh ingest`, the viewer and `coding.sh serve`, with `--where`, `--sample` and a daily judge spend limit. The MCP tool `score_add_batch` writes up to 100 scores, all or none. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli/monitor)
- **Scores.** `coding.sh review create <queue> --where <query> --config <name>` makes a queue of sessions to score by hand, with `--sample` and `--assign`. `review next`, `done`, `skip`, `status` and `list` work through it, and `/api/review` does the same in `coding.sh ui` and `serve`. Each answer is a human score. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli/review)
- **Sessions.** `stats.compactionRefetches` counts read-only calls that fetched again a result a compaction took out of the context, with no write to it in between, and the tokens they sent again. It is absent when there are none. [API reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md)
- **Viewer.** A Work tab (`#/work`, <kbd>g</kbd> <kbd>w</kbd>) in `coding.sh ui` shows what needs you, from the same queue as `coding.sh work`: each bucket's items with their reason, age, next step, cost and session links, and agent, repo and time filters in the URL. Ack and Snooze 1h write the same `work.json` as the CLI. [Open the viewer](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#open-the-viewer)
- **Viewer.** On a session page, `}` and `{` move to the next and previous user turn, and the help sheet and hint bar list them. Each turn's row shows the first line of the prompt and its time into the session, and the strip beside the step list marks each turn start. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#turn)
- **Viewer.** In a session of more than three turns, each finished turn starts folded to one row with its prompt, what it did ("read 2 files, 1 edit"), tool calls, errors and length. A turn with a failure and the last turn stay open. Enter, a click or a link to a step inside opens it. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#turn)
- **Viewer.** A session page has a Chat view beside Steps (`v`): every prompt and reply in full, in order, with one line per turn counting the steps between them that goes back to Steps at that turn. The choice is remembered and is part of the link. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#turn)
- **Viewer.** Prompts and replies show their markdown formatted (headings, lists, code blocks with their language, inline code, bold, italic) and tool output drops terminal escape codes. Links show as text with their address, HTML in a message shows as written, and Copy text gives the original. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#turn)
- **Viewer.** A session with a failure opens from the list on its first failing step, and the header's First failure button (`F`) goes back to it, opening a folded turn on the way, and stays while a running session grows. A link to a step still opens on that step. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#session)
- **Viewer.** Each row in the session list is a link: a middle click, Ctrl or Cmd click, or "Open link in new tab" opens the session in a new tab and leaves the list where it was. A plain click and the keys work as before. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#session)
- **Viewer.** The top bar has a Workflows tab (Flows on a phone) beside Benchmarks, so every page of the viewer is one click away. The tab of the page you are on is marked. [Explore](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#open-the-viewer)
- **Viewer.** A Review page (`#/review`, <kbd>g</kbd> <kbd>r</kbd>) works through review queues: progress, the next session, a control for the score config, a comment, and keys for Done, Skip and opening the session. Work items show their stage and kind, with a stage filter. [Review queues](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#review-queues)
- **Work.** `coding.sh work` lists what needs you across every agent: items blocked on a prompt, failed, stalled, waiting for your next prompt or ready for review, each with a reason and a next step. `work show`, `work ack` and `work snooze` act on one item. `status` and the statusline count them; the MCP `status` tool lists the first 5. [CLI](https://coding.sh/docs/cli/work)
- **Sessions.** Sessions get `stats.waiting`, the permission prompt, question, plan or next prompt a session waits on when its events end, and `stats.endInferred`, set when `endedAt` is only the last event seen. [API reference](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **Site.** The GitHub link in the header shows the repository's star count, and so does the menu on a phone. The count is read when the site is deployed, so a page makes no request to GitHub. [coding.sh](https://coding.sh)
- **Capture.** Devin CLI: `coding.sh init` installs its hooks and registers the MCP server, and the OTel receiver reads its logs. Its permission prompts show in `coding.sh work`, and eval runs read tokens and cost from its `--export` file. Devin also runs Claude Code's hooks; those calls are stored as Devin's, once. Past sessions are not imported. [Agents](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/agents.md)
- **Harness.** `coding.sh harness tools` lists every MCP and built-in tool the proxy saw across sessions, with a hash of its description and schema. `--changed` exits 1 when a tool had more than one description or schema, differs from the hashes `harness tools pin` saved, or first showed up after the last pin. Needs the proxy with request capture. [Proxy](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy#catch-a-tool-description-that-changed)
- **Harness.** After a pin, `harness tools --changed` also keeps a pinned tool that the sessions since no longer offer (`removed since pin`), and `--json` names up to 20 `sessionIds` that saw each change. Pinning again drops a removed tool's pin. [Proxy](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy#catch-a-tool-description-that-changed)
- **Capture.** Serve and the collector also take OTel traces at `/api/public/otel/v1/traces`, where Basic auth with an ingest key as username or password works, so an SDK that sends there changes only its host. `--where user:`, `env:` and `release:` filter by the user, environment and release a trace names. [OTel](https://coding.sh/docs/otel#public-path)
- **Work.** A T3 Code thread that waits on an approval or a question is blocked on you in `coding.sh work`, with the prompt or question, whichever agent the thread runs. The wait comes from T3's store and clears when T3 resolves the request. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#waiting-state)
- **Sessions.** `coding.sh views save <name> --where <query>` keeps a query under a name, and `views list` and `views rm` manage them. `sessions --view <name>` lists its sessions, ANDed with `--where` when both are given; the MCP `sessions_list` tool takes `view`, and the viewer API serves `/api/views`. [Query](https://coding.sh/docs/query#saved-views)
- **Viewer.** Comment on a session or one step in `coding.sh ui`: each comment is a text score named `comment`, shown as text with its author and time, and `coding.sh show` prints them under their step (`comments` in `--json`). The session list's Views menu applies and deletes saved views, and Save view keeps the current filter. [Explore](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#open-the-viewer)
- **Viewer.** On the Work page a workflow run shows each phase's agents by state, with its agents grouped by phase and each phase's cost. A superseded failure links the session that superseded it. Work and the session list say how old the index is, as a warning when stale. Worktree sessions show their main checkout's repo. [Explore](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#open-the-viewer)
- **Cost.** `coding.sh usage --repo <path|name>` keeps one repo, read as `sessions --repo` and a repo budget read it, so `usage` with a budget's `--agent` and `--repo` over its period gives the total `budget status` shows. `-r` is its short form. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/track-cost.md#set-a-budget))
- **Status.** `coding.sh status` names each agent's last capture on a Last capture row and says when an update is out, as the MCP `status` tool does. `status --json` adds `version`, `update`, `lastCapture` and `index.ageMs` with the tool's values; the update comes from the update check's cache, with no network. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-status))
- **MCP.** The `work` tool takes `id` (a work id, a prefix of one, or a session id) and answers with that one item, found as `coding.sh work show` finds it, hidden items too. Before, reading one item meant paging every item. An unknown id is a tool error, and so is a shared prefix, which lists the matches. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#surfaces))
- **MCP.** The `eval_candidates` tool answers with `rejected`, the count `coding.sh eval candidates --json` gives, and `rejectedBy`, the count for each reject reason. Both cover every session it considered, not only the rows it lists. Before, it could not say how many sessions it turned down or why. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#session-to-eval))
- **Viewer.** Each Work item has its own page (`#/w/<id>`), built by the same code as `coding.sh work show`: its reason, next step, sessions, PRs, files and a run's agents by phase, with Ack and Snooze. Every card links to it, and back returns to the list where it was. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer))
- **Sessions.** `coding.sh show` prints a Retrieval line: the session's reads and the files they named, its searches and how many found nothing, how many of the files read it later edited, and the reads and searches before its first edit. Sessions get `stats.retrieval`, and `stats --json` adds totals over the window. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid))
- **Sessions.** Each session records how often and how long it waited on you, and on what: permission prompts, questions, plans, host app requests and turns. Steers, and the waits an agent's message ended, count apart. `coding.sh show` prints one line with the median wait, and `coding.sh stats --json` adds the window's totals by kind. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid))

### Changed

- **MCP.** The tools that take a session id, and `coding.sh work show`, take a unique prefix, a subagent's own id or `latest`; an ambiguous prefix lists its matches. Hints and `session_tree` and `subagents` rows use the shortest id that resolves, so more rows fit on a page. `search` and `status` keep full ids, with `shortId` beside them. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#session)
- **Export.** The OTLP sink follows GenAI semantic conventions v0.1.0: schema URL 1.45.0, the package version as scope version, and `gen_ai.provider.name` on every chat span, transcript-only Claude Code and Codex sessions included. Chat spans carry `gen_ai.request.reasoning.level`; Skill calls carry `gen_ai.skill.name`. [Sinks](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#sinks)
- **Work.** Each workflow run is one `coding.sh work` item, apart from the session that started it, with a line per phase counting running, failed and done agents. A failure older than 12 hours, or one a later passing item on the same branch replaced, no longer counts in "need you". [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#workflow-runs)
- **Compare.** A trial timeout counts in an arm's `attrition.timedOut`, not as infrastructure, and gets no re-run hint; `ETIMEDOUT` stays infrastructure. When the arms' timeout counts differ, `exp compare` and `--json` name the cases and note that the verdict depends on the time limit. [Eval methods](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/eval-methods.md#more-checks-exp-compare-prints)
- **OTel.** The receiver reads GenAI semconv v0.1.0: an `invoke_workflow` span is a subagent named by `gen_ai.agent.name`, `gen_ai.request.reasoning.level` is a model call's `reasoningEffort`, `mistral_ai` is stored as `mistral`, and an MCP tool span whose `rpc.status_code` is not `OK` is a failed call. Older names still work. [Production](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#43-plain-opentelemetry-no-sdk)
- **OTel.** With `mode = "full"`, the `otlp` sink sends a tool call's arguments and result as structured OTLP values (`kvlistValue`, `arrayValue`) when they are an object or array of 8192 characters or less as JSON, so OTLP backends show their fields. Larger values and plain strings are still sent as text cut at 8192 characters. [Config](https://coding.sh/docs/config#sinks)
- **Sessions.** A session whose events stop on a tool call with no result, and with no session end, has no `endedAt`: it did not finish. Before, its last event counted as its end, and for a transcript so did the marker written after 30 quiet minutes. [API reference](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **Viewer.** A code block's language label sits in the block's top-right corner instead of on a loose line above it, and Chat no longer opens with a "1 step" line when the only step before the first prompt is the session starting. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#turn)
- **Viewer.** On a phone, a session's Pass and Fail buttons and its eval command wait under "More details" with the other facts, so the steps start higher on the first screen. `p` and `f` still label the session. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#session)
- **Viewer.** In a session's Chat view, `}` and `{` bring the next or previous prompt to the top and `j` and `k` the next or previous message, and the hint bar lists them. Before, every step key did nothing there. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#turn)
- **Viewer.** A folded turn's row has no timing bar, so its prompt and summary run on over the timing column and more of the prompt shows. On a phone the row keeps the turn number, the prompt and its error count, and leaves the length and tool count to the turn's detail. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#turn)
- **Viewer.** The theme follows the system's light or dark setting, and changes when the setting does, until you pick one with the theme button. A picked theme is remembered and wins. The first paint already has the right theme. [Explore](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#open-the-viewer)
- **Docs.** The docs sidebar folds by group on wide screens, as the phone menu does: the first group and the one that holds the page start open, the rest closed, so every group name fits on screen. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs)
- **Docs.** The "On this page" rail lists each section's subheadings under it, shows them for the section being read, and marks both; it scrolls itself to keep the current entry in view. [SDKs](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **Docs.** Below 1280px, where the rail does not fit, "On this page" is a closed dropdown at the top of the article with the same entries, on tablets and phones. [SDKs](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **Docs.** The Quickstart and Tool evals procedures are numbered steps: each step's number sits in a ring beside its heading, with a line joining it to the next. The Markdown copies keep the numbers in the headings. [Quickstart](https://coding.sh/docs/quickstart)
- **Docs.** The FAQ folds each answer under its question, and each question is one control named by its words. A link to a closed fold, or to a section inside one such as a question or an optional step, opens it and scrolls to it, from the same page or another. [FAQ](https://coding.sh/docs/faq)
- **Docs.** The `--help` text on each CLI page, and every terminal output block, has a Copy button. Copying a block whose lines start with `$ ` copies only the commands, without the prompt or the output. Shell blocks colour the command, its flags, quoted strings and comments. [CLI](https://coding.sh/docs/cli)
- **Docs.** The Quickstart opens with a prompt to paste into your coding agent, with tabs for coding agents and for TypeScript and Python agents: each says how to install, how to set up and how to check that it records, then gives a first task. The home page's install button copies the same setup prompt. [Quickstart](https://coding.sh/docs/quickstart)
- **Site.** The install box at the top and bottom of the home page offers three ways to set up: the setup prompt for your coding agent (picked first), `npx coding.sh init` and the install script. Its copy button copies the one picked, and a note under it says where it runs. [Install](https://coding.sh/docs/install)
- **Harness.** `coding.sh harness audit` checks for each Claude Code project's `memory/` dir with a stat instead of a directory listing that fails. With 25,800 project dirs, 12 of them with memory, listing the memory files took 0.56 s instead of 1.45 s. [Explore](https://coding.sh/docs/explore)
- **Capture.** Ingest and backfill fold the next sessions while git reads repo state, instead of waiting on each git command. A fresh backfill of 358 sessions spent 6.2 s instead of 16.5 s writing sessions. The stored sessions are the same. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-ingest)
- **Storage.** `coding.sh gc` walks the blob folders once instead of twice: the temp files it sweeps are the ones the blob listing passed. On a store of 37,697 blobs, `gc --dry-run` took 5.9 s instead of 9.4 s. It removes the same blobs and temp files. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-gc)
- **Docs.** Command blocks drop their "shell" title bar: the Copy button sits in the block's corner. Blocks with a file name or output keep their title. The star beside the GitHub link is yellow. [Install](https://coding.sh/docs/install)
- **Capture.** Ingest no longer rewrites a past day's spool file with nothing to redact, and SQLite folds the WAL into the index every 64 MB and into the blob pack every 16 MB, not every 4 MB. Dogfood ingest went from 3,388 to 4,165 events/s (+778, 95% CI +83 to +1,437). The stored sessions are the same. [Architecture](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/architecture.md#storage)
- **Capture.** A large ingest compresses its small blobs on a worker thread, and commits every 20,000 events instead of 5,000 when no sink is configured. In a paired run, dogfood ingest went from 3,761 to 4,935 events/s (+1,174, 95% CI +707 to +1,635). The stored blobs and sessions are byte for byte the same. [Architecture](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/architecture.md#efficiency)
- **Capture.** The node hook loads its redaction rules only for a payload whose text may hold a secret, or when your `config.toml` sets other rules; the rules would leave any other payload as it is. A 2 KB tool call with no secret runs the hook 0.7 ms faster than 0.1.7 did (95% CI 0.1 to 0.9, Windows). [Performance](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md#redaction-in-the-node-hook)
- **Serve.** `coding.sh serve --json` no longer has `keys.ingest` and `keys.read`; read the keys from the file `keys.file` names. [Production](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md)
- **Checks.** `npm run gate`, and CI's static gate with it, also runs the dogfood scorecard's own tests (`npm run dogfood:test`). Before, no check ran them. [Contributing](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md)
- **Checks.** `npm run gate`'s site step builds the site with the star count CI uses, then runs the site's perf budget and checks each page's HTML against Site perf's budgets. Before, a page over either budget passed the gate and failed only in CI. Site perf now builds with the same star count. [Performance](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md)
- **Checks.** `npm run gate`, and CI's static gate with it, also runs the changelog helper's tests (`npm run changelog:test`) and `node scripts/changelog/add.mjs --check` on the unreleased lines. Before, no check ran them. [Contributing](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md)
- **Site.** coding.sh is served by Vercel instead of a Cloudflare Worker, which had hit its daily request limit and answered 429. Moved pages, `www`, the headers and the installer behave as before. [Release](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/RELEASE.md#deploying-the-site)
- **Dogfood.** A perf or site budget missed by a little gets partial credit: the target divided by the reading for a ceiling, and the reverse for a floor. One reading near a budget moved the headline by 4 points before. `compare` scores the previous scorecard again with the current scorer and keeps its stored headline as `prevStored`. [Dogfood design](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/dogfood.md)
- **SDK.** In Python, `coding_sh.flush()` waits at most 5 s by default, as `flush()` does in TypeScript; `flush(timeout=None)` still waits until the queue is sent or given up. Before, a script that ended with `coding_sh.flush()`, as the quick start shows, could hang for over a minute when the server did not answer. [SDK](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **Proxy.** `proxy stop` asks the proxy to stop over its own port, with a secret from its state file, so the calls in flight finish before it exits. Before, it sent a signal, which on Windows ended the proxy at once and cut those calls. A proxy still running after 10 seconds is ended. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#undo))
- **Alerts.** `alert list --json` and `alert add --json` give an alert's `states` as a list, one entry per group with `agent` under `--by agent`, so Windows PowerShell 5.1's `ConvertFrom-Json` reads them. Before, the states were keyed by group, and the empty key of an alert without `--by` made it fail. alerts.json is unchanged. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/alerts.md#check-on-a-schedule))
- **Sessions.** `coding.sh tree` on a subagent shows that subagent and its own subagents, below a line naming the sessions above it. Before, it printed the whole run, which `--all` still shows. From a subagent, `--json` adds `ancestors`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#subagents-and-the-run-tree))
- **Viewer.** The viewer page and every JSON answer of 32 KB or more go gzipped when the browser takes gzip, with `Vary: Accept-Encoding`. On a seeded store the page drops from 705 KB to 213 KB, and a 200-item Work answer from 145 KB to 19 KB. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#read-api))
- **Sessions.** A T3 Code thread whose first prompt a person sent is now interactive, also when T3 runs Claude Code through its SDK, unless the OpenCode, Cursor or Pi import reads it. `--where launch:interactive`, the launch facet and `coding.sh improve`'s default sessions include it, and `coding.sh work` lists it as your turn, not stalled, once its turn ends. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid))
- **Sessions.** Shell lines that took seconds to fold, such as an unclosed quote followed by many escaped quotes, a long `cd` chain, a long PowerShell comma list or a failed `cat` of many files, take milliseconds. On synthetic sessions the retrieval stats take 29% less time to fold (95% CI 28% to 32%). The stats are the same. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid))

### Fixed

- **Evals.** For a `coding.sh run --` session, `eval new` and `eval candidates` no longer take code (`sh -c "npm run build"`), a `NAME=value` setting or a path with a space in it for the task prompt. A session whose command has no other prompt argument is left out as having no prompt. [docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/session-to-eval.md#one-session-at-a-time)
- **Capture.** T3 Code threads an import left when it ran out of time are read by the next import. Before, they waited until T3 Code wrote to its store again, and an import out of time from the start read none of them. [Host apps](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#host-apps-t3-code)
- **Evals.** A tool eval trial whose agent took turns and then exited 127 counts as a failing trial in its arm's pass rate. Before, it was taken for an agent that never started and left out. Only a spawn error, or a non-zero exit before the first turn, means the agent never ran. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#31-task-kinds-bad-guard-control)
- **Serve.** `coding_sh_session_cost_usd_total` counts a session's cost when one of its turns ends too, so an OpenTelemetry trace with a conversation id counts its root's own cost, not only its subagents'. [Production](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#17-metrics-and-logs)
- **Work.** A session that merged more than 20 pull requests keeps every PR it opened, merged or closed; only the PRs it just viewed or linked are capped at 20. An orchestrator's early merges no longer leave their items "open" in Ready for review. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#work-items)
- **Work.** Work whose session pushed to a pull request or linked it to its T3 Code thread leaves Ready for review once another session merges that PR. Sessions get `stats.prs.own`. A PR the work only viewed still does not count. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#the-queue)
- **CLI.** `stats --since` with a time counts from that time, not its midnight. In PowerShell every command `improve` prints, `--json` included, names `codingsh`; `improve check` counts the headless sessions it leaves out. An unreadable config file is named as such. `serve healthcheck` takes `--port`, and `--limit` past 2^53 is a usage error. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#did-your-last-change-help)
- **Viewer.** Find in steps also matches a step's output and error, as the event search does. The picked `agent:t3code` chip counts the sessions listed. `coding.sh ui` starts the refold an upgrade needs and says "Rebuilding stats…" while it runs. A workflow run's error keeps its line breaks. [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apiingest)
- **OTel.** Batching no longer changes OTLP sessions: an AI SDK subagent exported before its `ai.toolCall` gets its own session, a GenAI root or subagent takes its prompt from its first chat span whatever the export, timed at the agent's start, and a subagent's answer shows once. [Production](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#43-plain-opentelemetry-no-sdk)
- **Evals.** `eval new` with a new `--name` drafts a second task instead of replacing the first, and names a moved or deleted repository with the way on. A grader with no answer to compare with leaves the run ungraded (`judge grade` exits 2), and a grader key it does not read is refused. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#grader-fields)
- **Work.** A PR merge reaches every item that lists the PR, and an item whose PRs all merged is done, not ready for review. PRs only viewed in another repo, or named in fetched text such as release notes, no longer link. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#work-items)
- **Search.** A Codex code-mode cell no longer doubles its calls in search: when a call the cell ran matches, the cell's copy of its command or output is left out, so each match is listed once and a page holds distinct hits. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#search-every-session)
- **Improve.** For a failing command, the "Find every time it failed" step now runs `coding.sh search "<command>" --where 'kind:tool.end ok:false'`, which lists the failed calls instead of every call that mentions the command. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#see-what-to-fix-first)
- **MCP.** The first calls after an upgrade answer within a few seconds. While the index is folded again, every result carries `index.refolding: true`, and a session not yet refolded shows its older stats whole. `latency` keeps to its time budget on very large stores, where it could fail before. [Configuration](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#output-and-updates)
- **Viewer.** A long session's page moves a fraction of the bytes: events load as compact, gzipped rows with long tool output clipped, and opening a step reads its events whole. The timeline, deep links and Find in steps are unchanged. [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsidevents)
- **Sessions.** `show --json` reads cut prompts and outputs back whole from their blobs. A failed model call shows in `show` with its error and sets the `llm-error` flag. An SDK session of model calls only prints `-` under TOOLS. `sessions` leaves out eval, compare and exp trials unless `--include-runs`, and keeps titles on one line off a terminal. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#read-one-session)
- **CLI.** In PowerShell and cmd, next steps and error hints name `codingsh`, the bin that runs there, in text and `--json`. So do `status`'s hint for a stopped ingest, the MCP `status` tool's update `command`, and on Windows the update pill in `coding.sh ui`. [Windows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows)
- **Privacy.** `coding.sh purge` waits for a job still recording model calls (`exp run --record` or `--replay`), then exits 1 naming its experiment if it still runs; `--dry-run` names such jobs. A job marks its `llm-cache/` folder with a `.recording.lock` file while it records. Before, purge missed what the job wrote after it. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#remove-a-leaked-secret)
- **Workflows.** A workflow run's stored agent outputs and run output go through your redaction rules, and an imported run in metadata mode keeps none. A case whose planted bug's `patch` sits inside `repoRoot` or `snapshot` is refused with a folder to move it to. A failed run's page in `coding.sh ui` shows its error. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#write-cases-with-known-answers)
- **Store.** A migration checks who owns the index under its write lock, before it copies the index. An index another process finished migrating while this one waited opens instead of failing with `store_owned`, and a refused migration makes no copy and leaves the lock free. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#store-version-errors)
- **Sessions.** A session T3 Code ran now has the agent that ran (`claude-code`, `codex`) and `host: "t3code"`, so `usage --by agent`, `status` and the viewer's Agent facet compare Claude Code with Codex. The header says "via T3 Code", and `--agent t3code` still lists these sessions. [Host apps](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#host-apps-t3-code)
- **Work.** `coding.sh work`, `work --json` and the MCP `work` tool give the review step `work show` gives: an item whose changed files are all outside its repo names them, not an empty `git diff`. A file a shell call edited by absolute path from a subfolder counts once, so `work`, `work show` and `show` agree. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-work)
- **Improve.** A failed command's sample is an error line: a notice such as taskkill's `SUCCESS: …`, a status line (`exit: 0`), a `key: value` field or a line of code is not one, so an issue with no error line shows no sample. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#see-what-to-fix-first)
- **MCP.** `sessions_list` says which time window it read: a `window` with the `since` and `until` from its arguments and any `since:` or `until:` clause in `where`, the tighter of each. Given only `until`, it still lists every session before it. [MCP](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp#tools)
- **Improve.** Failures no longer land on `cat`, `git status`, `bash` or `python -` when the line did not parse, a removal was denied or the command was cut short. `improve check` names sessions with no git HEAD. Model-driver toolevals runs get a price; `scriptPath` workflow runs and T3 delegated tasks get readable titles. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#see-what-to-fix-first)
- **Capture.** A `node --test` run reports node's own pass and fail counts, not a pytest or cargo line from an assertion, diff or printed fixture, and waiting on a log is no test run. A git checkout, switch or merge no longer counts as Claude Code or T3 Code editing the files it brought in. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#test-results)
- **CLI.** `statusline install` and `uninstall` print one JSON document with `--json`. On Windows `sessions --repo DEMO` matches every worktree, as `demo` does. `improve --repo <name>` reads that repo's git history, or fails on an unknown name. `tree` names the role once. `stats`, `harness audit` and `work show` say how subagents' numbers roll up. [CLI](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-stats)
- **OTel.** An AI SDK `ai.streamText` or `ai.generateText` run inside an `ai.toolCall` is a subagent with its own child session. A Claude Code prompt logged a few ms before its interaction span no longer opens a resume phase, and a root whose model calls all moved to subagents keeps no model from them. [OpenTelemetry](https://coding.sh/docs/otel#sessions)
- **Viewer.** The session list's query bar no longer jumps beside the title when nothing matches; the Run tree's Fix first link names the subagent by title; the empty Workflows page says runs on Work need `coding.sh workflows import`; and a failed subagent's next step resumes its parent session, not a `:sub:` id. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md)
- **Evals.** When a task has no reference patch, the `eval new` note and the `eval validate` error name the steps that add one: `coding.sh eval export <task> -o <dir>`, write `<dir>/reference.patch`, then `eval import <dir>` and `eval validate <task>`. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#session-to-eval)
- **Docs.** The `sessions.repo` column in the query reference lists the keys it stores (`root`, `head`, `branch`, and `main` for a linked worktree) instead of `commit` and `remote`, and says a bare repo's worktree has no `main`. [Query](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md#sessions)
- **Sessions.** A subagent's title skips workflow frame lines before it takes one line, and a `Project:` line is skipped only in a framed prompt. A workflow agent with only a frame is titled by its phase and label. A phase is titled by its first prompt that is not a harness notice or frame. [Query](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md#sessions)
- **Improve.** `improve` reads subagents and delegates, reads PowerShell as PowerShell so a failure is filed under its program rather than the launcher or `Select-Object`, keys issues on the program or error class instead of output text, and says how many sessions past the 2000 cap it did not read. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#see-what-to-fix-first)
- **Sessions.** `sessions --repo` and `export --repo` take a repo name or a path, as `work` does, and a name includes every worktree. The list ends with how many child sessions it hides (`hiddenChildren` in `--json`). Repo roots are stored in one spelling on Windows, and a bare repo's worktree has no main checkout. [Explore](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#list-your-sessions)
- **Capture.** A shell call gives a test result only when its command runs tests, so `tail ci.log` or `grep "test result" log` no longer reports an old run. T3 Code instance ids such as `claudeHub` map to their agent, and workflow agents carry their run id, name, phase and label. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#test-results)
- **Install.** The site and README give Windows readers `npx -p coding.sh codingsh` wherever they show `npx coding.sh`. The install page has a Windows tab, and the agent setup prompts tell an agent to type `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd, where `coding.sh` opens npm's sh script instead of running it. [Install](https://coding.sh/docs/install)
- **MCP.** `session_get`, `session_current` and `sessions_list` add `repoMain`, the main checkout's root, for a session in a linked worktree, as `show --json` does. The session resource's Repo line names it too. [MCP](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp#tools)
- **Viewer.** Review stays a top tab on a phone, where tabs scroll sideways with no name cut. The Run tree shows every name on a phone, the activity cell fits "5,050 tools · 45 failed", and an unknown session logs no 404. A skip refused because the item was scored elsewhere moves on; Review buttons are 44px on touch. [Explore](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#open-the-viewer)
- **CLI.** `cost`, `spend` or `tokens` suggest `usage`, and `waste` suggests `usage --cache`. `ui` starts no background ingest with `CODING_SH_NO_AUTO_INGEST=1`, and `show` writes nothing to the store. `harness audit` applies Claude Code's or Codex's rules to a T3 Code session by the agent it ran. `usage --by subagent` keeps a T3 Code agent switch with its parent. [Cost](https://coding.sh/docs/cost#cost-by-mcp-server-skill-or-subagent)
- **Improve.** The error line under a failing command comes from that command's own output, not from another part of the same shell line, and the title says "fails the same way" only when the errors match. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md)
- **Work.** For a session in a worktree nested inside its repo, `work show` names the worktree, its review step diffs the worktree, and its file list uses one path base. [Work tracking](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#surfaces)
- **CLI.** `show --json` and `sessions --json` add `repoMain` for a session in a linked worktree. `doctor` counts the sessions tagged by the gateway an agent routes through. `exp ab`, `refine` and `refine diagnose` take `--provider gemini`. `quota wait` says why it did not wait, and the status line creates no store. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli/sessions)
- **OTel.** `coding.sh serve` merges the span and the log record Claude Code sends for one model call, as the collector does: the call keeps the reported cost from the log and the TTFT from the span, in either arrival order. Before, it kept only the first copy. [OpenTelemetry](https://coding.sh/docs/otel#claude-code)
- **Evals.** A grader spec with a mistyped `provider` ("gemnii") suggests the close one, as `monitor add --provider` does. A `--judge` monitor whose `--provider` has no API key records which key to set (`no judge provider: gemini: set GEMINI_API_KEY`) in `monitor run` and `monitor list`. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli/monitor)
- **Sessions.** A session in a git worktree counts under its main repo: `repo:`, `--repo .`, `usage --by repo`, the viewer's repo group, `improve` and the MCP tools include it. `repo.main` names the main checkout; `cwd` and `repo.root` stay the same. [Query](https://coding.sh/docs/query#session-keys)
- **MCP.** `session_get` with `tail` pages back to the first entry with `nextCursor`. A `since` after `until` is an error naming both times. A 2024-11-05 client gets 2024-11-05 and results without `structuredContent`. Error results carry no `structuredContent`; details are in `_meta`. `status` lists the top 5 work items, not 10. [MCP](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp#protocol)
- **MCP.** `session_get` reads a session once when it shrinks a page to fit, about 8 times faster on long sessions. `harness_changes`, `exp_session_summary` and `eval_from_session` answer an error for a missing repo path or unknown id, and `since` equal to `until` no longer advises a swap. [MCP](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp#tools)
- **Capture.** The status line prints from what arrived when stdin stays open for 1 s, instead of waiting forever. It leaves out `$0.0000 session` for a session it has not stored, and `statusline --json` and `quota --json` round amounts and percents to 4 decimals. [Agents](https://coding.sh/docs/agents#status-line)
- **Capture.** A T3 Code Codex session built from T3's store judges an `exec` cell as Codex does: a script that completed with exit code 0, or still runs, is a success, and a tool request that "has no active turn" counts as interrupted, not a tool error. Stored sessions are parsed again. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#transcripts-headless-runs-isolation)
- **Evals.** An eval instruction that starts with a dash (`- fix the bug`) no longer fails as an unknown option: OpenCode and Cursor runs pass it after `--`, and Pi reads it on stdin. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#transcripts-headless-runs-isolation)
- **Sessions.** A session's title skips a workflow harness's frame line, a `Project:` header line and pasted blocks, so a work item shows its task instead of a preamble many sessions share. A prompt that is only a paste is titled by its first line. [Session](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#session)
- **CLI.** `init --dry-run` and `doctor` no longer run another tool's `agent` command (Grok's made `~/.grok`). `uninstall` warns and exits 1 when an OpenCode install sits under another `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR`. Blame and attribution credit edits made in a removed worktree, and `diff-calls` says "not recorded" instead of "same" without stored requests. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli/uninstall)
- **Viewer.** The command menu lists a session whose title holds the typed word first, even when newer sessions on a branch named for it fill the results. A t3code subagent's badge reads "subagent", not "subagent · subagent", whole-minute axis ticks read "1m", and a phase chip no longer pushes a step into its duration. [Open the viewer](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#open-the-viewer)
- **Work.** A second Claude Code permission prompt with the same message, for the next tool call and under 2 s after the first, is kept. Before, it was taken for a copy of the first, and `coding.sh work` showed the session running while it waited on you. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#waiting-state)
- **Stats.** `stats` and `/api/metrics` over top-level sessions count each subagent's p95 model-call time and TTFT, so an agent whose model calls all run in subagents, as OpenInference and Agents SDK runs do, no longer shows `-` for P95 LLM and TTFT. [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md)
- **Evals.** A tool-eval state assertion `{ command, contains }` now checks the command's stdout, as `stdoutContains` does; before, it passed whenever the command exited 0. A key an assertion does not read is refused when the task loads. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#3-task-format)
- **MCP.** Reading `codingsh://latest` on an empty store returns the "No sessions captured yet" next step instead of Resource not found. `session_get` refuses `turn: 0` rather than returning the whole session, and the session digest says "1 turn". [Ask your agent](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#ask-your-agent)
- **Capture.** A Claude Code or Codex session T3 Code ran, stored by an older `--since` import without its T3 link, gets the link and thread title back from the next re-parse, which reads T3's store once after an upgrade. `ingest --rebuild` does it too. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#host-apps-t3-code)
- **Capture.** A Claude Code, Codex or T3 Code transcript line that holds a raw U+2028 or U+2029 character inside a string is no longer dropped, so its tool call, result and file change are kept and the session gets its end time. Stored sessions are parsed again while their sources are on disk. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around)
- **Capture.** A Pi tool call stopped with Esc (`Operation aborted`) and a Gemini CLI tool call the user cancelled count as interrupted calls, not tool errors, so they no longer raise `toolErrors` or `repeated-tool-failure`. Stored sessions are parsed again while their sources are on disk. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around)
- **Viewer.** A diff the agent's log cut short says so where it stops, with how many changed lines it leaves out and a button that loads the whole diff, and no longer ends on a blank line that was not in the file. [Open the viewer](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#open-the-viewer)
- **Viewer.** Steps and Chat number prompts ("Prompt 3"), so a session whose background tasks started extra turns no longer reads "Turn 23" under "33 turns". A session opened from the command menu goes back to Sessions. A Work card's age matches its reason's, and its next step shows commands as code. [Open the viewer](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#open-the-viewer)
- **Improve.** A Codex or T3 Code code-mode `exec` call no longer counts as a failing command of its own, so a failure counts once, under the command it ran, and no issue is named after `text` or "Script completed". A command run as `powershell.exe -Command <cmd>` is filed under `<cmd>`. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md)
- **Sessions.** In `show`, a T3 Code session no longer opens with an empty start phase and a "resumed after 1 min" that never happened, and a gap under a minute reads in seconds. `harness audit` lists a file an agent named as `/c/Users/...` once, under its `~` path. [Explore](https://coding.sh/docs/explore)
- **Evals.** `eval import` keeps every file under a task dir's `tests/` as a hidden file, so a test added to a dir `eval export` wrote is no longer dropped. `eval validate` builds its impossible variant from a file the verifier runs, such as `node check.js`, whatever the file is named, as the reference tamper check already treats it. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#hand-written-tasks)
- **Capture.** `ingest --backfill --since` imports a Codex rollout or a T3 Code thread with activity in the window whole, as it already did a Claude Code transcript, so a session begun before the cutoff keeps its start, its title, every turn and its T3 Code link. `uninstall` removes the Codex config folder `init` created once it is empty. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md)
- **MCP.** `session_get` on a session whose last turn is still under way now gives one turn count: `turnCount` and the out-of-range turn error count closed turns, as `session.turns` and `coding.sh show` do, and the turn under way stays in the index marked `open`, reachable with `turn: -1`. [MCP](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp)
- **Work.** `coding.sh work` counts a file that several sessions of one item changed once, so its files-changed count matches `work show`; a reason's age floors like the Started row's ("1h ago" in both), and a wait under a minute reads in seconds, not "0m". `config get` on an unknown key suggests the closest one, as `config set` does. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-work)
- **Evals.** A toolevals `calls` check on a CLI subcommand (`must = [{ tool = "dataset add" }]`, `order = ["sessions", "show"]`) now matches a call with positionals after it (`dataset add smoke`), and the surface crawl reads a help page printed on stdout when `--help` exits non-zero, as npm's does. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md)
- **Cache.** `diff-calls` on two calls read from an agent's transcript no longer tells you to turn on capture mode full, which cannot store their requests; it says to run the agent through `coding.sh proxy` to log them. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-diff-calls)
- **Budgets.** `budget set` with a mistyped period, such as `dya`, now suggests the command it meant (`Did you mean coding.sh budget set day 20?`), like `--by` and `--window` do. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-budget)
- **Capture.** One parser reads test summaries for every agent: Gemini CLI, Antigravity, Copilot CLI, Amp, Pi, Droid, Goose and Devin CLI now record a `test.result` for `pytest -q`, and every agent takes the last Jest, Vitest, node:test or Mocha summary. Long outputs no longer take seconds. Stored sessions are parsed again while their sources are on disk. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#test-results)
- **Dogfood.** The scorecard comparison reads a rise in LCP, in the dev to held-out gap or in any perf ceiling from `bench.mjs` as worse. Before, a slower LCP and a wider gap showed as better. [Dogfood design](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/dogfood.md)
- **Evals.** The verifier-corrected pass rate in `exp show` and `exp compare` builds its 95% interval from the Wilson intervals of the pass rate and the audited precision, so it carries the uncertainty of both and no longer collapses to zero width, such as [100.0%, 100.0%], at a 0% or 100% rate. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Viewer.** In the session list, the row `j` and `k` move to scrolls in below the sticky header and column titles instead of under them. Jump to (Ctrl K) lists sessions whose title matches before sessions that match only in their events, and opens one of those on the step that matched, with the query's matches marked. [Explore](https://coding.sh/docs/explore#open-the-viewer)
- **CLI.** `init` and `doctor` read Gemini CLI's and OpenCode's version from their npm package instead of launching them, so they no longer leave `~/.gemini/projects.json.<uuid>.tmp` files or empty OpenCode cache dirs behind. [Troubleshooting](https://coding.sh/docs/troubleshooting)
- **CLI.** `attribution` and `blame` print a subagent as `a03d2f6b:sub:a7d49789`, which `show` opens, not as its parent's id. `bench show`, `history` and `report` on a benchmark that does not exist say they can't find it, as `bench run` does. `upgrade-store --json` reports `"status": "owned"` when it only took ownership of the store. [Troubleshooting](https://coding.sh/docs/troubleshooting)
- **OTel.** A GenAI semconv trace whose `invoke_agent` root span has no input takes its prompt and title from the `gen_ai.input.messages` of a chat span under it. Messages on a chat span's `gen_ai.client.inference.operation.details` event are read like those on span attributes. Before, both sessions had no user message and took the span name as their title. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md)
- **Tool evals.** `toolevals surface` counts once a CLI whose root `--help --json` already holds every command's options and subcommands, so coding.sh's surface is about half what it was. `toolevals init --cli` with a binary not on PATH exits 1 and writes nothing, and a crawl that finds no help page is an error, not a 0-byte surface. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#21-cli)
- **Sessions.** A T3 Code session takes the title of the T3 thread that ran it, in `sessions`, `show`, `tree` and `improve`. Before, it took its first prompt, so handoff and delegated threads were titled with T3's handoff or sub-agent preamble. In `show`, T3's link event names the thread instead of printing an empty `notification` line. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#host-apps-t3-code)
- **MCP.** The `search` tool's `agent` picks sessions by the session's agent and each hit's `agent` is the session's, as `coding.sh search --agent` and `sessions_list` do. Before, it went by each event's agent, so `agent: "t3code"` found nothing in Claude Code sessions run from T3 Code and their hits said `claude-code`. [Ask your agent](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#ask-your-agent)
- **Evals.** `eval new` on a session backfilled from a transcript with no start commit says how to fix it: record the task again with hooks or `coding.sh run --` inside the repository. `eval candidates` labels its replay penalty `compacted context (harder to replay)`, so it no longer reads as `+1 compaction` and `-1 compaction` on one session. [Troubleshooting](https://coding.sh/docs/troubleshooting)
- **MCP.** The `latency` tool lists T3 Code's `request_secret` card under `waitingTools` with `waitsOn: "human"`, like `AskUserQuestion`. Before, the minutes it stayed open until you pasted the secret topped `slowestTools` and `slowestCalls`. [Ask your agent](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#ask-your-agent)
- **Dogfood.** On Windows the scorecard validates the CLI suite's 37 dev tasks, runs the site's Lighthouse, and measures `perf.viewerTti` in Chrome; before, every task was invalid and the viewer bench broke. `cli.tasks.invalidReasons` names each invalid task's failed check, command, exit code and error line, and a missing Lighthouse or Chrome is a skip naming what to install. [Dogfood design](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/dogfood.md)
- **Dogfood.** The scorecard's tests gate runs the tests as CI does: when five or fewer test files fail, each is run again alone, and a file that passes then does not fail the gate. The notes name it. Before, one timing test slowed by a busy machine failed the gate and left the scorecard without a headline. [Dogfood design](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/dogfood.md)
- **Capture.** The node hook (`hook.mjs`) redacts a payload with your `capture.redact` settings before it writes it to the spool, inline or in a side file. Before, secrets sat on disk unredacted until ingest. Ingest stores the same event, with the same redaction counts. The sh hook (`hook.sh`) still leaves redaction to ingest. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#the-raw-spool)
- **Experiments.** `exp show` and `exp run` print why a task failed in the failures table: the exit code and stderr, such as `error: exited 3: model file missing`. Before, the task's command filled the 100-character reason column and cut the cause off. `--json` keeps the whole error in `runs[].error`. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Improve.** `improve` marks an issue "Not seen since" a harness change only when that many clean sessions on the new harness are unlikely by chance at the issue's rate ((1 - rate)^n below 0.2). Before, one clean session hid the Fix line of every issue. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md)
- **Site.** The files a page loads (scripts, fonts, search, images and icons) are served without running the site's edge Worker, so they no longer count toward its daily request limit. Past that limit, coding.sh answered every request with a 429 error until midnight UTC. [Release](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/RELEASE.md#deploying-the-site)
- **Sessions.** A Gemini CLI permission prompt counts for the `unapproved-action` flag: a risky call the user approved there is not flagged, whether the prompt came while the call was open or before it started, as in Gemini CLI 0.35 and older. Before, only Claude Code's prompts counted. [Flags](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md)
- **CLI.** `config set` takes the `[proxy]` keys. [Configuration](https://coding.sh/docs/config)
- **CLI.** `blame` and `improve check` outside a git repo name `--repo <dir>` with their own command (`improve check` exits 1, `not_a_repo`). [CLI](https://coding.sh/docs/cli)
- **MCP.** Following the search tool's `nextCursor` returns every hit once, in the order of `coding.sh search --json`. Before, hits that ranked the same could repeat on one page and never show on any. The session_get summary and the session resource also report failed model calls (`llmErrors`: count, first status and error). [MCP](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp#tools)
- **CLI.** `coding.sh update --dry-run` checks for a new release without rewriting `~/.coding.sh/update.json`, as its help says. Before, it replaced the cached result with the new one. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-update)
- **CLI.** `harness audit` counts a file that the agent's loaded instructions file imports with `@path` (`@AGENTS.md`) as loaded, adds its size to the load cost and no longer flags it never-read or write-only. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md)
- **Cost.** The status line's `today` cost counts subagent sessions, so it matches `budget status`, `usage --since today` and the budget percent next to it. [Status line](https://coding.sh/docs/agents#status-line)
- **Cost.** `usage --reprice` and the refresh that runs when prices change update the events and sessions in a Postgres store. Before, both skipped Postgres: estimates kept old prices, calls to a newly priced model kept no cost, and older estimates had no split by token type. [Where cost comes from](https://coding.sh/docs/cost#where-cost-comes-from)
- **CLI.** `config get refine` prints the section like `config get retention`; `ingest` shows each monitor error once; `monitor add` with both `--run` and `--judge` says they cannot go together; `workflows list`, `dataset list` and `exp list` name the command that fills them; tables say `0s ago`, not `just now`. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli)
- **MCP.** `sessions_list` lists subagent sessions when its `where` names `id:`, `parent:` or `is:child`, as `sessions --where` does, instead of an empty list without `includeSubagents`. On Windows, `session_current` and the `repo` argument match paths whatever their case and slash direction, so `c:/work/app`, `C:\work\app` and a session stored with forward slashes are one folder. [Query language](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md)
- **Harness.** Ctrl-C stops `harness audit` while it lists files on disk, and it exits 130. Before, the listing held the process until it finished, which took about 50 s on WSL's `/mnt/c`. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-harness-audit)
- **Harness.** `harness snapshot --agent claude-code` stores the files Claude Code's instructions and rules pull in with `@path` imports, such as an imported AGENTS.md, followed up to 5 hops as Claude Code does. It leaves out dot directories under `skills/`, such as Claude Code's `.trash` of deleted skills. [Compare agents](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/compare-agents.md)
- **Evals.** `--hidden-test` also hides a matching test file that is already in the start tree: the task starts from a snapshot without it, and the verifier gets it at check time. Before, only test files the session changed were hidden, so the agent could read the tests that grade it. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-eval-new)
- **Evals.** `eval init --sessions` names a command that works for a session with no prompt, such as one `coding.sh run` recorded: `eval new <id> --instruction "…"` with the same `--verifier`. Before, it said to pass `--instruction`, which `eval init` does not take. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#session-to-eval)
- **Evals.** `eval validate` fails a task whose reference patch edits, deletes or skips the tests the verifier runs, as `eval init` already treats such a diff, and names each file. Before, a reference that only rewrote the expected value validated. A file the verifier only reads, such as `f.txt` in `grep -q b f.txt`, is not a test. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#session-to-eval)
- **Install.** Uninstall for OpenCode also removes the `plugins/` folder init made for its plugin, and the OpenCode config folder, when nothing else is left in them. A folder that holds any other file stays. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#uninstall)
- **Status line.** `statusline` reads at most 1 MiB of stdin, then prints its fallback line, so endless input such as `</dev/zero` no longer hangs it while memory grows. Ctrl-C stops it while it waits for input, with exit code 130. [Status line](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/status-line.md)
- **MCP.** On Windows, `mcp config` prints register commands whose paths survive Git Bash: forward slashes, with double quotes around a path that holds a space. Before, Git Bash dropped the backslashes, so `C:\Users\me\cli.mjs` arrived as `C:Usersmecli.mjs`. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-mcp)
- **Sessions.** The `--json` of a session in `sessions`, `show` and `tree` rounds each cost to 1e-9 dollars and each millisecond figure to a whole one, so a cost of `0.0041400000000000005` prints `0.00414`, as summed costs do. Other numbers are as stored. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Sessions.** `sessions` and `usage` reject an `--agent` that no stored session has and that is close to an agent coding.sh knows, such as `claud-code`, and suggest `--agent claude-code`. Before, they printed an empty list. `usage` for an agent with no session at all suggests every agent's usage, not a wider window. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **CLI.** A mistyped command, action or flag also gets a suggestion when it is a shell habit (`exp ls` suggests `exp list`, `rm` suggests `delete`) or the start of exactly one name, three letters or more (`gc --dry` suggests `--dry-run`). Before, only a name a few letters off got one. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Install.** An agent config written as JSON on one line, such as `{"theme":"dark"}`, stays on one line when coding.sh adds or removes its entries, so `uninstall` gives back its exact bytes even when `~/.coding.sh` and its backups are gone. Before, it came back indented over several lines. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md)
- **Install.** `uninstall` removes the coding.sh MCP entry from Codex's `config.toml` after `~/.coding.sh` was deleted, when the entry is exactly the launch coding.sh writes. Before, it stayed and `uninstall` said nothing was installed. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md)
- **Capture.** A backfill skips Gemini CLI chat files that have not changed since the last one. Before, it read every chat file again each time and counted all of their sessions as updated. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#transcripts-headless-runs-isolation)
- **Capture.** A copy of T3 Code's store that a killed read left in the temp directory, up to 1.7 GB each, is removed by the next read, even when the store is unchanged. A copy that fails, such as on a full temp volume, is a backfill error, not an import of nothing. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#host-apps-t3-code)
- **Capture.** A T3 Code subagent thread is stored once when its run sits under a later Claude session of the thread, after a resume or a session switch, and so are Workflow-tool runs and their agents. A re-parse removes the t3code copies. A model call built from T3's store names its turn's model, not the thread's default. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#host-apps-t3-code)
- **Viewer.** The shortcut bar at the foot of a session page keeps `? all shortcuts` in view in a window about 1,000 px wide. Hints that do not fit on its one line are left out whole. Before, they wrapped and pushed `? all shortcuts` below the bar. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#turn)
- **Viewer.** Pressing the pressed Pass or Fail button on a session page, or its key (`p`, `f`), clears the outcome label: it is stored as `skip`, which calibration and evals leave out. Before, a label could be changed but not taken back. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#judge)
- **Viewer.** Back from a session, by Esc or the browser's Back, the session list is scrolled where you left it and focus is on the selected row, so `j` and `k` go on from there. Before, the list opened at the top. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#session)
- **Viewer.** In the session and event lists, `j` on the last loaded row loads the next page, as Load more does, and selects its first row. Before, it stayed on the last row. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#session)
- **Viewer.** A command that fails inside a Codex code-mode cell is one failure stop: the cell that ran it is marked, not counted as a second failure, and the calls a cell runs sit indented under it. A cell that fails on its own is still a failure stop. [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#session)
- **Workflows.** Ctrl-C stops `workflows import` and `workflows list` at the next run and exits 130. The runs imported so far stay stored, the import says how many are left, and the next import goes on from there; `--json` says `"outcome": "interrupted"`. Before, the first Ctrl-C did nothing until the import finished. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **MCP.** `eval_init` errors name its arguments, such as `command`, `url` and `force: true`, where `eval init` names CLI flags, and no longer suggest `--draft`, which `eval_init` does not take. [MCP server](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp)
- **MCP.** `latency` reads every session in the window, not only the newest 300 with calls, so a filter no longer finds a slower call than the unfiltered answer. `window` says how much it read (`sessionsRead`, `eventsRead`); after 30 seconds it stops, sets `truncated` and says why in `truncatedNote`. [MCP server](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp)
- **MCP.** The `codingsh://session/<id>` resource stays under 25,000 characters: it lists the first 40 subagent lines, with descriptions cut to 120 characters, names the `subagents` and `session_tree` tools for the rest, and shortens the timeline to fit. Before, a session with many subagents could return a resource past the limit. [MCP server](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp)
- **MCP.** `session_get` and the session resource split a session into the turns `stats.turns` counts: a turn a background task's notification starts is its own turn, and a prompt sent while a turn runs stays in it. Before, `turnCount` could disagree with the turn list, and those turns could not be opened. [MCP server](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp)
- **OpenTelemetry.** `coding.sh collector` stores a Claude Code model call sent both as an `api_request` log record and, with `CLAUDE_CODE_ENHANCED_TELEMETRY_BETA`, as a span, as one call with the reported cost and the span's TTFT. Before, it kept whichever copy came first, and a prompt logged before its trace read as a resume. [OpenTelemetry](https://coding.sh/docs/otel)
- **OpenTelemetry.** When a batching exporter sends the span that names the conversation, such as the root `invoke_agent` with `gen_ai.conversation.id`, after the trace's other spans, those spans and their subagents move into the conversation. Before, they stayed in a session named after the trace id, and the conversation had no subagent. [OpenTelemetry](https://coding.sh/docs/otel)
- **OpenTelemetry.** Spans of eve's own Workflow runtime, such as `workflow//eve//workflowEntry` and its steps, are dropped like the other `workflow` scope spans. Before, they added span rows to the eve session, stretched it to the last delivery, and added 0-turn sessions for session timeouts and workflow tool runs. [eve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/integrations/eve.md)
- **Install.** On Windows, `install.sh` and `init` tell you to type `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd, where `coding.sh` opens npm's sh script instead of running it. Run from Git Bash, `init` keeps `coding.sh`. The install guide says the same. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md)
- **Install.** `install.sh` warns when another `coding.sh` comes first on PATH, such as an older global install, and names its version, its path and the folder to put ahead of it. Before, `coding.sh` went on running the other one without a word. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md)
- **Install.** Started with npx on Windows, `init` names `npx -p coding.sh codingsh ui` and `npx -p coding.sh codingsh improve` as its next steps. npx runs the command through cmd there, where the `npx coding.sh ui` it named before opens npm's sh script instead of running the CLI. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#install)
- **Install.** Every command `init`, `uninstall` and `update` print, including the `--yes` rerun they ask for without a terminal, names `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd, and the npx command when started with npx. Before, most named `coding.sh`, which fails there. To reinstall, PowerShell and cmd get `npm install -g coding.sh`, not `curl … | sh`. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows)
- **Install.** Without a terminal, the rerun `init` and `uninstall` ask you to approve is the command you ran, flags included, plus `--yes`. Before, it dropped the flags: `init --agent claude-code` named `coding.sh init --yes`, which set up every detected agent, and `uninstall --agent opencode` named a rerun that removed coding.sh from all of them. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#what-init-changes)
- **Tool evals.** A CLI target whose binary name has a dot, such as `coding.sh`, runs and records through its tap. The tap is now a sh launcher next to a `.<bin>.tap.mjs` module; before, node refused the name with `ERR_UNKNOWN_FILE_EXTENSION`. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md)
- **Judges.** `judge calibrate` reports a κ of exactly 0 when the judge agrees with your labels no more than chance would, in the estimate and its interval. Before, rounding could print it as `-0.00`. [Judges](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#judges)
- **Docs.** The durable workflow guide sets up tracing with OpenTelemetry JS 3.x (`@opentelemetry/sdk-trace`, `new BatchSpanProcessor({ exporter })`), and the Agent Runs guide pins the 2.x packages durable-harness and eve need. Before, their unpinned installs failed with `ERESOLVE`. [Trace a durable workflow](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/trace-durable-workflow.md)
- **Evals.** Codex eval runs pass `--trust` when the installed Codex has it (0.169 and later), so a harness's project `.codex/config.toml` and its MCP servers load in the eval work dir. Only runs in their own `CODEX_HOME` pass it, never a run that uses `--ignore-user-config`, and it writes no hook trust hash. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md)
- **Serve.** Behind a TLS proxy with `trust_proxy` set, every HTTPS answer carries `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000`, and a sign-in sets the read cookie as `__Host-csh_rk`, marked `Secure`. Plain HTTP keeps `csh_rk`, and a browser signed in before the upgrade stays signed in. A browser that has seen the header refuses plain HTTP to that host for a year. [REST API](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **Viewer.** The sign-in form posts the key instead of putting it in the URL.
