A `/?t=` link followed from another site no longer signs in by itself: the viewer shows an Open button that posts the key. A link from the address bar, a bookmark or another app signs in as before. [REST API](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **Serve.** First-start keys are printed once, on the start that makes them. Later starts name `serve-keys.json` instead, a start that fails leaves no keys file behind, and a wrong `CODING_SH_INGEST_KEYS` or `CODING_SH_READ_KEYS` entry is named by its position. [Production](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md)
- **Capture.** `capture.redactPatterns` in a repo's own `.coding.sh/config.toml` are literal text. Regular expressions count only from your own config, so move a repo's regular expression there to keep it working. [Configuration](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md)
- **Serve.** Forwarded spans send an attribute whose key names a secret, such as `db.password` or `http.request.header.authorization`, as `[redacted:secret-field]`, whatever its value, and ingest stores such a key's value the same way. [Production](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#19-forward-and-sinks)
- **Capture.** A private key whose `END` line is missing, as in a tool output cut short, is redacted from its `BEGIN` line to the end of the text when key data follows that line. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#redaction)
- **Capture.** Redaction reaches values nested more than 64 levels deep in an event or a blob, with the same rules as any other value. A value too deep to copy is stored as `[redacted:too-deep]`. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#redaction)
- **CLI.** Text output and messages drop terminal escape sequences and control characters from captured text, such as a line `blame` prints or an error that quotes it, so printed text cannot move the cursor, set the window title or add a link. CSV and JSONL do the same when written to a terminal. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md#event)
- **Viewer.** Commands the viewer copies (`eval new`, `replay --fork --with`, `bench show`) quote each session id, path and name for sh and PowerShell. A command whose value holds a quote, a control character or `& | < >` is shown but not copied. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer)
- **Serve.** A viewer `POST` that carries an `Origin` is accepted only when the Origin's host is the host the request was sent to, on a public bind as well as on loopback. Behind nginx, add `proxy_set_header Host $host;`: the 403 says so. [Production](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md)
- **Serve.** Every route but `/healthz` answers 403 to a `Host` header serve was not set up for, as the viewer already did. The new `serve.allowed_hosts` setting (`CODING_SH_ALLOWED_HOSTS`) adds names on loopback, such as `host.docker.internal` for an agent in a container, and limits a public bind to its list (`/readyz` still answers probes). [Configuration](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md)
- **Serve.** On loopback with no keys, serve answers only requests that no proxy passed on: a request with an `X-Forwarded-*`, `Forwarded` or `Via` header gets 401, and serve logs a warning the first time. With `serve.trust_proxy` set, a loopback serve with no keys stops at start and names the keys to add. [Production](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md)
- **Scores.** `POST /api/scores` with an id that is already stored replaces the score only when the caller wrote it and can see its session. Other callers get 404 and the score is unchanged. [Scores API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md)
- **Viewer.** A session opened from a link or a reload, not only from the list, opens on its first failing step; a session with no failure opens on its first prompt instead of "Session starts". [Concepts](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts#session)
- **Docs.** Below 1280px the "On this page" dropdown lists every subheading of every section, such as each function on the SDKs page. Only the wide rail keeps to the section being read. [SDKs](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **Docs.** An API path in "On this page" wraps after a slash or a comma, so entries such as `GET /api/sessions/{id}/events` no longer split in the middle of a word. [REST API](https://coding.sh/docs/api/read)
- **Docs.** A name in a reference table's first column, such as `CODING_SH_INGEST_KEY`, stays on one line; the table scrolls sideways instead. [SDKs](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **Docs.** On Quickstart and Install, a block that shows what a command prints is captioned "output" instead of "shell", so each step reads as the command to run and what to expect. [Quickstart](https://coding.sh/docs/quickstart)
- **Docs.** On phones the "Copy page" and "View as Markdown" buttons above the title are as short as on a desktop, with the same 44px touch target, so the title sits higher on the first screen. [Quickstart](https://coding.sh/docs/quickstart)
- **Viewer.** Esc or a link pressed within 80 ms of a step key (`j`, `k`, `n`) leaves the session. Before, the step's late update of the pane could put the session's URL back, and the page stayed on the session. [Explore sessions](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md)
- **Self-host.** In the Compose stacks that run serve, Postgres and ClickHouse are healthy only when they answer on the address serve connects to. The old checks passed against the image's first-start setup server on loopback or the unix socket, so `docker compose up -d --wait` could fail on the ClickHouse stack with `clickhouse at http://clickhouse:8123/ is unreachable`. [Deploy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/production/deploy.md#docker-compose)
- **Examples.** `examples/plugin-ab/check-tasks.py` removes the temp files it makes. Before, each run left 20 answer files and 2 folders in the temp directory. [examples/plugin-ab](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/tree/main/examples/plugin-ab)
- **Search.** `coding.sh search` and bare words in `--where` find the error a subagent or a session ended with, such as a failed Copilot CLI or Droid subagent or a Cursor session that ended with an error. Before, only error events, failed model calls and tool errors were searchable. Ends stored before are indexed once on upgrade. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md)
- **Stores.** The Postgres and ClickHouse store plugins index error text stored before it was searchable: error events, failed model calls, tool errors and the error a subagent or a session ended with. Before, search there found only errors stored after an upgrade. Each store does this once, on its next open. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md)
- **Evals.** Copilot CLI, Droid, Gemini CLI, Goose and Pi eval runs keep the agent's home next to the work dir, not inside it, so they read the bundle's `~/` files and the `--carry` state, and logins and the agent's files stay out of the start commit and the diff. Aider and Amp read the bundle's config files too. [Harness A/B](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab)
- **Capture.** On Windows with Node 24, a read that kills its import child at its deadline waits up to a second for the child to let go of its SQLite store copy, then removes it. Before, `rmSync` gave up at once, so the copy (T3 Code's is 1.7 GB) stayed in the temp folder until the next read. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#host-apps-t3-code)
- **Evals.** An `--http` monitor whose evaluator can't be reached (a network error, a timeout, or a 5xx, 408, 425 or 429 on every try) keeps its place, and the next pass starts at that session again, unless the evaluator answers for a later session in the same pass. A pass stops after 2 such sessions in a row. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#monitors))
- **Evals.** Ctrl-C in `monitor run`, or `coding.sh serve` stopping, while a monitor scores a session no longer counts that session as failed or moves past it: the next pass scores it. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#monitors))
- **Evals.** `monitor list`, `monitor list --json` and `monitor add --json` show an `--http` URL without its query string, as the monitor's errors do, so a token in the query stays out of the output. Each POST still goes to the whole URL. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#monitors))
- **Alerts.** Alert webhooks and `monitor add --http` evaluators get `X-Coding-Sh-Timestamp` and `X-Coding-Sh-Signature-V2`, which signs the timestamp and the body, so a receiver can refuse a request more than 5 minutes old. `X-Coding-Sh-Signature` still signs the body alone; a copy of a request still passes it. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#monitors))
- **Harness.** `harness tools --changed` calls a pinned tool `removed since pin` only when every tools array offered since the pin leaves it out. A session whose latest call offered fewer tools, such as a subagent's, no longer makes a tool still in use look removed. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#catch-a-tool-description-that-changed))
- **Harness.** `harness tools pin` no longer pins a removed tool again on the next pin, and `--changed` no longer keeps it for its old versions, so an accepted removal stays accepted. Before, every second pin brought the removal back. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#catch-a-tool-description-that-changed))
- **Budgets.** `budget status` reports every budget when one can't be read, such as a stored `--where` query that no longer parses: that budget shows its error, in text and as `error` in `--json`, and the command exits 1. Before, one such budget failed the command for all of them. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/track-cost.md#set-a-budget))
- **Budgets.** `proxy --enforce-budget` reads only the spend of budgets it can match a call to, so a `--where` or `--repo` budget no longer slows calls. A budget whose spend can't be read is named in a warning and the others still apply; a failed read is tried again 30 s later, not on every call. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#stop-calls-over-a-budget))
- **SDK.** Without `CODING_SH_URL`, the TypeScript SDK's `observe()` and `observeFetch()` pass each call through untouched, as the docs say. Before, `observe()` still added `stream_options` to OpenAI chat streams, and `observeFetch()` still read every request and response body, so `flush()` waited on open streams. [SDK](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **SDK.** In an Anthropic tool loop, the TypeScript SDK's `observe()` records the user's prompt as each call's `user.message` and session title, as the Python SDK does. Before, it took the text of the last `tool_result` block, so the sessions were titled with a tool's output. [SDK](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **SDK.** In TypeScript, the exit flush also sends a batch that is waiting for its retry, and warns when it has to drop events. Before, a script that ended without `flush()` while the server was down or answered 503 exited with its events lost and no warning. [SDK](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **SDK.** The Python SDK reads a `CODING_SH_URL` that ends in `/v1/traces` or `/v1/ingest` as the TypeScript SDK does: events go to `/v1/ingest`, and `coding_sh.otel` spans go to `/v1/traces`. Before, a URL ending in `/v1/traces` sent events to `/v1/traces/v1/ingest`, which the server does not take, so they were dropped. [SDK](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **SDK.** The Python SDK reads `Retry-After` as the TypeScript SDK does. It waits up to 60 s, not 30 s, and it honors an HTTP date, retrying at once when the date has passed; before, it ignored dates. A value it cannot read, such as `Infinity`, now means backoff, not a 30 s wait. [SDK](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **SDK.** In TypeScript, when `shutdown()` or the flush at exit runs out of time, its warning names that flush and says the events are dropped. Before, it said `flush()` gave up and that the events would be retried while the process ran. [SDK](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **SDK.** Inside `trace()`, each Vercel AI SDK call through `aiSdkTracer()`, and each span from Python's `coding_sh.otel` provider, is a step of the trace's one turn. Before, each call was a turn of its own and added its prompt as a user message. Sessions stored before keep their turns: spans are mapped at ingest, so nothing is parsed again. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/trace-your-agent.md#trace-the-vercel-ai-sdk))
- **SDK.** In Python, when `shutdown()` or the flush at exit runs out of time, its warning names that flush, says how many events were not sent and that they are dropped, as in TypeScript. Before, both said `flush()` gave up, and `shutdown()` went on retrying in the background. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#42-python-packagessdk-python--pypi-coding-sh-import-coding_sh))
- **SDK.** Both SDKs read `Retry-After` as RFC 9110 defines it: whole seconds, or one of the three HTTP date forms, in GMT. Anything else, such as `-1`, `1.5`, `0x10` or an ISO date, means backoff. Before, TypeScript read `-1` as a date in 2001 and an asctime date as local time, and Python read `1_000` as 1000 s. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#4-sdks))
- **Docs.** Troubleshooting lists the fix for doctor's MCP, Sessions and Proxy warnings and explains the Any command row, and its doctor sample matches the output. `bad_flag` moved to the usage errors (exit 2), and the example messages match what the CLI prints. [Troubleshooting](https://coding.sh/docs/troubleshooting)
- **Docs.** Turn failed runs into evals says that every trial runs the setup `eval init` guessed (such as `npm ci`), that `eval run` asks first (`--yes` skips it), and that `eval new` takes a session id: after a run, `latest` is a trial. Its samples match the output and its validate table lists every check. [Turn failed runs into evals](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/session-to-eval)
- **Docs.** Benchmarks gives the three scripts its first run calls, so the example runs as written, and sets the case count with `bench new --cases 30`. Its samples match what `bench new` and `bench run` print. [Benchmarks](https://coding.sh/docs/benchmarks)
- **Docs.** Compare two agents adds `--permission-mode acceptEdits` to the `claude -p` that records a task, since without it Claude Code can't write the fix. It also says that `--by` needs `--gate`. [Compare two agents](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/compare-agents)
- **Docs.** Replay recorded model calls says to set `excludeTools` or `excludeModel` when you record, since a replay matches the keys the recording stored. It also says that the noise floor counts pass verdicts, not outputs, and that `exp show` prints a replay's call counts. [Replay recorded model calls](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/replay-llm-calls)
- **Docs.** Workflow experiments puts the example case's truth file outside the repo, since its `repoRoot` is the repo root and a truth file inside fails to load. It shows the truth file's format, says a planted bug needs its `patch` to be checked, and lists the `no significant difference` verdict. [Workflow experiments](https://coding.sh/docs/workflows)
- **Docs.** Test an AGENTS.md change says that Claude Code reads its own instructions file, not AGENTS.md, so for its sessions you edit that file or import AGENTS.md from it. [Test an AGENTS.md change](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/test-agents-md-change)
- **Docs.** Evaluate an Agent Skill's `verify.sh` finds `check.py` by its own path, since the verifier runs in the work tree. The page says the item gate reads `RERUN` when the skill never loads, drafts the trigger spec with `--draft`, since `--dry-run` only prints it, and names both arms to retry errored runs. [Evaluate an Agent Skill](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/evaluate-a-skill)
- **Docs.** The plugin how-to snapshots your setup with `--plugin <name>` before `compare --without plugin:<name>` drops the plugin, since a snapshot carries only the plugins it names. [Does this plugin help?](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/does-this-plugin-help)
- **Docs.** Privacy and redaction says that the working-tree snapshots in `repos/` keep your files as they are, not redacted, and lists the model replies recorded for replay in `llm-cache/`. [Privacy and redaction](https://coding.sh/docs/privacy)
- **Privacy.** When a sink header uses `${TEAM_KEY}` and that variable is not set, the error says to set `TEAM_KEY` or change that sink header in the config. Before, it pointed at the `[store]` section. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#errors-exit-1))
- **Privacy.** Model replies that `exp run --record` and `--replay` store in `llm-cache/` are redacted with your `[capture]` rules, `redactPatterns` included. Before, only the built-in `secrets` rules ran. Recordings made before still replay. In metadata mode the run prints one line saying it stores the replies. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/replay-llm-calls.md#what-is-stored))
- **Privacy.** `purge` also rewrites model replies recorded in `llm-cache/` that hold the value, keeping their replay keys, and deletes torn or leftover recordings that hold it. Snapshots in `repos/` are not rewritten: purge names each repo that holds the value and exits 1 until you delete it. Before, purge read neither folder. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#remove-a-leaked-secret))
- **Privacy.** `init --remote` said sessions from before it stay on this machine, but the import it runs and later ingests sent them to the team server. The `team` sink now has a `since` time, and sessions that started before it are not sent. Running init again keeps that time. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/self-host-team.md#4-connect-each-machine))
- **Experiments.** A replay warns about each case-trial it leaves unscored and says why: the recording has none for it, or a live call failed, with the HTTP status or network error. Before, a failed live call was never named, so `exp run --replay --no-control` showed unscored runs with no reason. [Replay recorded model calls](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/replay-llm-calls.md#3-read-the-result)
- **Experiments.** `exp run --replay` with two `--arm` specs sends each arm's model calls to the `llm.upstream` its own spec names, with or without a positional spec. Before, only the positional spec's upstream was used. [Replay recorded model calls](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/replay-llm-calls.md#set-it-in-the-spec)
- **Experiments.** `exp ab --watch` with a B it cannot watch (`--b git:…`, `none` or a harness bundle) exits 2 before any trial. Before, it ran the whole A/B, model calls included, and only then refused. [Test an AGENTS.md change](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/test-agents-md-change.md#if-something-goes-wrong)
- **Experiments.** A file given to `exp ab --b` (or `--a`, or `refine check`) replaces the harness file at its repo path, else the one with its name, as `refine test` does, so `--b drafts/AGENTS.md` tests a draft of AGENTS.md. Before, the draft was added next to AGENTS.md, so B held both versions. [Test an AGENTS.md change](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/test-agents-md-change.md#flags)
- **Experiments.** `exp ab` reads both arms with LF line ends, so with `core.autocrlf=true` a file that differs from git only in line ends is not an edit. Before, every instructions file in such a checkout counted as edited, so the default A/B tested line ends, and B's model calls carried CRLF. [Test an AGENTS.md change](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/test-agents-md-change.md#flags)
- **Experiments.** The `No arm difference` error of `exp ab` names the instructions file each agent loads, such as AGENTS.md for Codex, and suggests an `@` import line only for Claude Code. Before, it always told you to edit Claude Code's file, even for Codex sessions. [Test an AGENTS.md change](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/test-agents-md-change.md#if-something-goes-wrong)
- **Workflows.** `workflows run` warns about each field of a JSON `--variant` it does not know, with the known field it may have meant when one is close. Before, it dropped the field in silence, so a misspelled `fromCheckpoint` ran the variant from the start. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#run-two-versions)
- **Workflows.** On Windows, the first Ctrl-C lets the runs in flight finish and be saved, and the second stops them for `--resume` to run again. Before, the first Ctrl-C also stopped a workflow command, setup command or verifier command at once, and `workflows run` saved those runs as failures that `--resume` kept. [Long runs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#long-runs-resume-retries-status)
- **Workflows.** A workflow run that fails keeps why, redacted: `workflows run` prints the reason for each failed run, `workflows show` prints it on an `Error` row, and the JSON has `error`. Before, a command that did not exist showed only `failed`. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#run-two-versions)
- **Workflows.** With no run in the `--since` window, `workflows list` says so, says when the newest older run started, and prints the command that lists it. A `--name`, `--framework` or `--experiment` that matches no run points at every run. Before, it said `No workflow runs` and suggested an import. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#capture-and-measure-a-run)
- **Workflows.** `workflows stats` gives the failed and null shares of the agents that ran. An agent a resumed run took from the cache is only a cache hit. Before, cached agents were in them, so 1 null agent of 2 read as 11%. The next import reads each Claude Code run folder again to update its run. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#capture-and-measure-a-run)
- **Workflows.** When a case's truth file sits inside `repoRoot` or `snapshot`, the error names a folder outside both to move it to. Before, it suggested `hidden/` next to `cases/`, which is inside the repo, so the case failed the same check there. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#write-cases-with-known-answers)
- **Workflows.** `workflows compare` counts a pass at each case's own `threshold`, or 0.5 for a case that sets none, and `--threshold` still sets one for all. Before, every case used 0.5. `workflows run` records the thresholds, so an experiment run before this release still uses 0.5. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#compare-them)
- **Workflows.** A case file inside `repoRoot` or `snapshot` that lists bugs as `[[truth.bugs]]` is refused, as a truth file there is, and the error says where to move the bugs. Before, such a case loaded, and the workflow's agents could read its bugs. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#write-cases-with-known-answers)
- **CLI.** In PowerShell and cmd, the `--yes` rerun a consent prompt names puts an argument that needs quotes in double quotes, which both shells strip, and `init` quotes the `--` of `codingsh run -- <cmd>`, which PowerShell drops. Before, the rerun kept sh single quotes, which cmd passes on, and that `run` command failed in PowerShell without its `--`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows))
- **CLI.** The commands `doctor` suggests in its rows, next steps and `--json` warnings name `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd, and the npx command when started with npx, except the step a failed background ingest saved. Before, they named `coding.sh`, which fails in PowerShell and cmd. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows))
- **CLI.** On an empty store, the step that `sessions`, `show latest`, `status`, `stats`, `usage`, `search` and `cache doctor` print, and the hint the MCP server gives, name `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd, and the npx command when started with npx. Before, they named `coding.sh init` or `coding.sh doctor`, which fail there. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows))
- **CLI.** The steps `attribution`, `blame`, `bench` and `upgrade-store` print name `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd, and the npx command when started with npx. So does the update command in the MCP server's error for a store a newer coding.sh migrated. The `--yes` rerun of `upgrade-store` keeps the flags given. Before, they named `coding.sh`, which fails there. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows))
- **CLI.** The dim line that says a newer release is out names `codingsh update` in PowerShell and cmd, and the npx command when started with npx. Before, it named `coding.sh update`, which fails there. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#updates))
- **CLI.** Without a terminal, the `--yes` rerun that `compare`, `eval run`, `eval trigger` and `bench schedule --install-cron` ask you to approve is the command you ran, flags included, and names `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd. Before, it dropped the flags: `eval run scope --harness skilled` named `coding.sh eval run scope --yes`, which runs another harness. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#usage-errors-exit-2))
- **CLI.** `init --dry-run` ends with the command that applies its plan: the one you ran, flags included, without `--dry-run` and `--diff`. It names `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd, and the npx command when started with npx. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#what-init-changes))
- **CLI.** `init` asks to import past sessions only when an agent it sets up keeps some on this machine, and its plan names only those agents. Before, it asked on a machine with none. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#what-init-changes))
- **Improve.** When every session after a change was headless, `improve check` says how many it left out under "No sessions yet" and names `--headless`. In PowerShell and cmd, `improve fix` prints its commit step as three lines, `git add`, `git commit -m` and `coding.sh improve check`, since Windows PowerShell 5.1 has no `&&`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#did-your-last-change-help))
- **Harness.** `harness snapshot --agent claude-code` reads `~/.claude` files from `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when it is set. It records the MCP servers Claude Code would load: name, scope, transport, command or URL, and only the names of env and headers. `harness list` shows them. A trial does not load user or local servers. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab))
- **Snapshots.** On Windows, the snapshot of a working tree keeps a file whose path is over 260 characters. Before, git left it out. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#what-is-stored-and-where))
- **Experiments.** In `exp compare`, a B − A equal to the replay noise floor, to the 0.1 points the warning prints, counts as within noise, so the warning, `withinNoise` and the numbers agree. Before, an equal difference was not within noise. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#ab-a-harness-edit-on-your-own-sessions))
- **CLI.** Help for `proxy --enforce-budget`, `budget status --json`, `alert add`, `monitor add` and `harness tools` says what each does: which budgets stop a call, the error a budget that can't be read gets, the three signature headers and the receiver's 5-minute check, when a monitor pass stops, and what "removed since pin" means. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-proxy))
- **Evals.** `latest` skips sessions that eval, compare and experiment trials made, so `eval new latest` after `eval run` picks your own session, not a trial. A session with a run id counts only when there is no other. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#read-one-session))
- **Evals.** `eval init` prints the setup every trial runs, where it was guessed from, and how to change it. A `package.json` with no lockfile gets no guessed `npm install`, since that install pins no versions. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#your-agents-failed-sessions))
- **Compare.** `compare --experiment <id>` with no arms takes both from the stored comparison, baseline first, instead of failing on a missing `<A>`. When nothing records which arm was the baseline, it asks you to name both. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/compare-agents.md#5-look-again-without-re-running))
- **Evals.** `eval trigger` prints recall once, with its interval, which is marked unweighted when prompt weights change the rate. A missing `triggers.toml` suggests `--draft`, the hint when a draft exists names `--force` with every flag you gave, and the hint after a draft keeps `--spec`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/evaluate-a-skill.md#does-the-skill-load))
- **Evals.** `eval run` says in the first failure column why a trial failed or got no outcome: a setup step that failed, a refused login, or the agent's exit code and error line, redacted. Before, it was empty or read "verifier exit 1". A suite where nothing was graded still lists its tasks. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#the-report))
- **Evals.** Under the `eval run` and `compare` report, a failed run's verifier output starts at the first failing test when a test runner reported one (node:test, TAP, pytest, Jest, mocha, go test, cargo test), so it shows the test's name and error. Before, it showed the last 8 lines, often only the runner's summary. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#verifier-output))
- **Capture.** `coding.sh run --` takes no repo snapshots when `snapshot.enabled` is `false`, and records the repo's HEAD and branch only. Before, it still snapshotted the repo before and after the command, with built-in limits. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#snapshot))
- **Docs.** The `[snapshot]` config reference says that ingest also snapshots the repo for live capture, and that a repo's own config can set the keys. Past `maxFiles` or `timeoutMs`, a snapshot keeps tracked files only; the page said it was skipped. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#snapshot))
- **Self-host.** The example `deploy/compose/config.toml` uses the `[prices]` table that Configuration documents, in place of the older `[pricing]` spelling. Both spellings still work. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#prices))
- **Self-host.** The team Compose file no longer says the image is unpublished, and `deploy/compose/.env.example` suggests pinning 0.1.7, a real release, in place of 0.2.0. The example and the docs link the list of image tags. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/self-host-team.md#back-up-and-upgrade))
- **Serve.** When serve refuses to start because a key is missing, it names the command that makes that key. A missing read key now gets `coding.sh serve keygen --project '*' --read`, not the ingest key command. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/self-host-team.md#2-make-your-own-keys))
- **Serve.** The viewer sign-in page of `coding.sh serve` offers a user sign-in only when `CODING_SH_BASIC_AUTH` is set. Before, it offered one on servers with read keys only, where no user exists. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/self-host-team.md#2-make-your-own-keys))
- **Self-host.** Self-host for a team says how to keep every `docker compose` command on the Postgres stack (`COMPOSE_FILE` in `.env`), how to back up Postgres, what to do when Git Bash breaks `serve keygen`, and where to look when the container is unhealthy. The team guide shows the admin CLI inside the serve container. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/self-host-team.md#if-something-goes-wrong))
- **Serve.** When `coding.sh serve` runs on loopback with no keys and refuses a request that a proxy passed on, its warning names the commands for both keys a server behind a proxy needs, an ingest key and a read key. Before, it named only the ingest key command. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#12-auth))
- **Self-host.** When the server refuses the key, `init --remote` says where a server with no configured keys keeps its own: `serve-keys.json` in its data folder, and gives the `docker compose cp` command that copies it out. Before, it pointed at `docker logs`, but the server prints the keys only on the start that made them. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/self-host-team.md#1-start-the-server))
- **CLI.** `coding.sh doctor` warns `hooked but stale` when the OpenCode plugin on disk is not the one this coding.sh writes, or is for another OpenCode major version, and names `coding.sh init --agent opencode` to rewrite it. Before, the row passed while OpenCode ran no plugin. Version probes read `opencode v2.3.2` as 2.3.2, not 3.2. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#sessions-dont-show-up))
- **CLI.** `doctor` and `init` wait up to 15 s, not 5 s, for the `--version` of Antigravity CLI, Aider, Amp, Copilot CLI, Devin, Droid, Goose and Pi, the time they give Claude Code and Codex. Before, on a busy machine doctor could show `installed` where it had shown the version. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-doctor))
- **CLI.** `doctor` warns `Agents none detected` and names `coding.sh run -- <cmd>` when it finds no supported agent, and `doctor --json` carries the same warning. Before, the Any command row, which is always shown, kept that warning from ever printing. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#sessions-dont-show-up))
- **CLI.** `init` ends with one outcome per agent, the one its plan row gave it: `Already set up` for an agent an earlier run set up, `Set up` for one this run wrote to. Before, it said `Set up` for every agent whenever another agent or its own config changed. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#what-init-changes))
- **Docs.** The Quickstart's `coding.sh run` sample shows the line `run` prints (`the command ran 573ms, exit 0`), and its `show` sample, the OpenTelemetry page's samples and the SDK tracing guide's sample print a count of one as the CLI does: `1 file changed`, `1 tool call`. ([docs](https://coding.sh/docs/quickstart))
- **Docs.** Every docs page that shows `npx coding.sh` also gives `npx -p coding.sh codingsh`, the form that runs on Windows. The Install page gives the PowerShell line that deletes `~/.coding.sh`, and each Quickstart prompt tells an agent on Windows to run `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd. ([docs](https://coding.sh/docs/install#uninstall))
- **Doctor.** `coding.sh doctor` no longer counts a fast model's calls, such as one served by Cerebras or Groq, as past physical limits. A model whose calls usually top 500 output tokens a second is held to 10,000; any other model is still held to 500. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#sessions-dont-show-up))
- **Install.** The install script installs the latest GitHub release, the one `coding.sh update` installs: from npm when npm has it, else the release tarball. It used to install npm's latest, which can be a release behind. Without an answer from GitHub in 30 seconds, it installs npm's latest. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#updates))
- **CLI.** `coding.sh update --refresh-bin` run by a second install no longer moves the hooks of the install that owns `~/.coding.sh` without asking, so its next command can't migrate the store past what the owner opens. It changes nothing and names `coding.sh upgrade-store`, which asks first. `update` says when hooks still run another install. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#updates))
- **CLI.** `doctor` says an agent may not be capturing, not that it is not capturing, when its only problem is a stale hook file it still runs, such as an OpenCode plugin edited by hand. The last line counts it as `N agents with stale hooks`. A plugin OpenCode will not run still counts as not capturing. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#sessions-dont-show-up))
- **CLI.** `init` waits up to 15 s, not 5 s, for `opencode --version` when it picks which OpenCode plugin to write, as `doctor` does, and `doctor` waits as long for `gemini --version`. Before, on a busy machine init could write the 2.x plugin for OpenCode 1.x and remove the 1.x one. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-init))
- **Cost.** `coding.sh proxy --enforce-budget` adds `x-should-retry: false` to its 429, so the Anthropic and OpenAI SDKs raise the budget error at once. The Anthropic SDK used to wait the whole Retry-After, up to the next reset, before it showed anything. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#stop-calls-over-a-budget))
- **Cost.** The budget 429 from `coding.sh proxy --enforce-budget` and the proxy's `blocked a call` line show amounts under $1 to four decimals, as `coding.sh budget status` does, instead of `$0.00 spent of $0.00`. The line gives the reset as `5h 20m`, not in seconds. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#stop-calls-over-a-budget))
- **Cost.** `coding.sh proxy --enforce-budget` counts in its start note only the budgets that can block a call, and says when a `--repo` or `--where` budget, which never blocks one, is set, as `proxy --help` now says too. Before, it counted every budget. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#stop-calls-over-a-budget))
- **Capture.** In PowerShell or cmd, `codingsh proxy env` and `codingsh proxy` print PowerShell's `$env:` lines, so `codingsh proxy env | Invoke-Expression` points a PowerShell session at the proxy. Before, they printed `export` lines, which PowerShell and cmd can't run. Git Bash and other sh shells still get `export` lines. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#2-point-your-client-at-it))
- **Capture.** When `coding.sh proxy --background` can't start, it says why, such as a port in use or a budgets.json it can't read, and names the same command on a free port, keeping `--background` and `--enforce-budget`. Before, it said only that the proxy did not start. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#1-start-the-proxy))
- **Capture.** On Windows, `statusline install` writes `codingsh.cmd statusline`, which runs whether Claude Code uses Git Bash or PowerShell. Before, it wrote `coding.sh statusline`, which PowerShell cannot run, so a machine without Git Bash showed no status line. Run `codingsh statusline install` again to update a line installed before. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/status-line.md#install-it))
- **CLI.** The next steps and suggestions of `budget`, `alert`, `proxy` and `statusline` name `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd, and the npx command when started with npx. Before, they named `coding.sh`, which fails there. A `budget set` suggestion now quotes a `--repo` path with a space and a `--where` with `$`, so the shell passes each on. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows))
- **Budgets.** On Windows, `budget set` with `--repo` updates the budget for that folder when the path differs only in case, as in `C:\Work\web` and `c:\work\web`. Before, it added a second budget, such as `month-web-2`, that counted the same sessions. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-budget-set))
- **Alerts.** When an alert command fails, the error `alert list` and `alert check` show keeps the start and the end of its stderr, where cmd, PowerShell and Python name the problem. Before, it kept one line, so a missing program read `operable program or batch file.` with no program named. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-alert))
- **Budgets.** `budget set 20 day`, with the amount before the period, suggests `budget set day 20`. Before, it said only that the period must be day, week or month. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-budget-set))
- **Docs.** A new page shows how to add, test and schedule an alert, check a webhook by its `X-Coding-Sh-Signature-V2` and timestamp, and write a command target for Windows, where it runs in cmd.exe, as troubleshooting's `delivery_failed` row now says. `alert --help` names the V2 header; before, it named only the body signature, which does not stop a replay. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/alerts.md#verify-a-webhook))
- **CLI.** When `~/.coding.sh/bin/cli-path` names another install that is still there, `doctor` names `coding.sh upgrade-store`, which asks before it moves the hooks, instead of `update --refresh-bin`, which leaves them alone. With a store plugin, `upgrade-store` now moves the hooks too. Before, it said there was nothing to do. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#sessions-dont-show-up))
- **Viewer.** A link to another step of the session on screen, such as a pasted step link or back and forward, moves there on the events already loaded instead of downloading the whole session again. A link made while the replay loads or plays always lands. A link to another session still loads that one. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer))
- **Viewer.** On a phone, the step detail's Steps button brings the selected step fully below the top bar, and below the replay bar during a replay, instead of partly behind it. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer))
- **Viewer.** Failure counts say what they count: the tests badge reads "1 test failed" for the last run, the tool errors badge names on hover the benign and interrupted calls it leaves out, and the step bar and folded turns say "failed steps", which counts every step that failed. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer))
- **Viewer.** Durations round as the CLI's do, on the server-drawn list too: past an hour to the nearest minute and past a day to the nearest hour, so 8,975,379 ms reads 2h 30m, not 2h 29m. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer))
- **MCP.** `session_get`, `session_current` and the `codingsh://session/<id>` resource print durations as the CLI does: 119.6 s is 2m00s, not 1m60s, and 8,975,379 ms is 2h30m, not 2h29m. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#ask-your-agent))
- **Evals.** `coding.sh monitor list` shows what a `--judge` monitor spent today and its `--max-usd-day` cap to four places under a dollar, such as ($0.0005/$0.0001 today), where a spend under a cent showed as $0.00. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#monitors))
- **Evals.** `coding.sh score add --id` with an id that held a score on another target or name says which score it replaced, and `--json` gives it as `replaced`. Before, it printed "Updated" for a score the target never had. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#scores))
- **Judges.** `coding.sh judge calibrate` and `judge sample` say which label names the cases have when none is named after the judge's criterion, and the `--criterion` that reads them. Before, the viewer's Pass and Fail labels (named `outcome`) were left out of a judge with another name without a word. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#judges))
- **Judges.** The calibrate command that `coding.sh judge sample --out` prints runs as printed: it is named for the user's shell (`codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd) and passes `--split` for a plan not drawn from `test`, and `judge calibrate --plan` reads the labels named after the plan's criterion. `judge calibrate --sample-passes` names the shell's CLI too. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#which-cases-to-label))
- **Evals.** The `score_add`, `score_add_batch` and `score_list` MCP tools take a short session id, as the CLI does, and `latest` names the session `coding.sh score add latest` scores. Before, a short id was refused or listed nothing, and `latest` could be a newer session with no work in it. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#scores))
- **Judges.** `coding.sh judge slop` on a session with no captured diffs names the `git diff` command to grade instead with the CLI of the user's shell (`codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd). ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#agent-slop))
- **Docs.** The evals guide says `coding.sh judge slop` exits 1, not 2, when there is nothing to grade, and 2 on a usage error. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#agent-slop))
- **Evals.** `review next`, `done` and `skip` with `--json` print the item's command for the user's shell (`codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd), and `review create` with a query that matches nothing prints that query for `sessions --where`, quoted for the shell. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#review-queues))
- **Judges.** `coding.sh judge calibrate` keeps a judge's stored calibration when a run reads no labels, for example with a `--criterion` no label has. Before, the run replaced a calibration measured on labels with an empty one, and `exp compare` lost the judge's bias correction. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#judges))
- **Docs.** The evals guide says `score add case:<id>` needs the id of a case in one of your datasets (its example printed "case not found"), and that the hook runs no monitors: `monitor run` scores captured sessions now, and `monitor run --watch` keeps scoring new ones. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#monitors))
- **Improve.** `improve check` says once that no session ran with a change, and when only headless sessions ran, it says so and how to count them, in text and in `--json`. Commit subjects are cut as `sessions` cuts titles. The note on headless sessions left out counts them in the folder it reads, and names it. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#did-your-last-change-help))
- **Harness.** `harness snapshot --agent claude-code` stores a file in `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` once, under its `~/.claude/...` key, when that directory is inside the folder it snapshots, not again as a project file. A snapshot of a bundle already stored says it already exists, and `--json` gives outcome `unchanged`, or `updated` when only its MCP servers or note changed, not `created`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab))
- **Status line.** `statusline install` and `uninstall` change only the `statusLine` member of Claude Code's `settings.json`, so a file with CRLF line ends or one-line arrays keeps them. Before, they rewrote the whole file with LF line ends and one array item per line. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/status-line.md#install-it))
- **CLI.** In PowerShell or cmd, the key `compare --gateway`, `gateway enrich`, `exp replay` and `replay --fork` ask for, and the env `collector` prints, are `$env:` lines, and the commands beside them name `codingsh`; `exp replay` repeats your command with `--dry-run`. Before, they were `export` lines, which PowerShell and cmd can't run. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows))
- **Proxy.** `proxy env` says on stderr when no proxy answers on the URL it prints, and names the command that starts one. Its stdout is unchanged, so `eval` and `Invoke-Expression` still run the lines. Before, it printed the lines for a proxy that was not running and said nothing. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#if-something-goes-wrong))
- **Proxy.** `init --proxy` writes no base URL when another program holds `proxy.port`, when it is 0, or when this home's proxy runs on another port. It still sets up the hooks, says why, and prints the command that fixes it. Before, it pointed Claude Code and Codex at a port the proxy did not hold. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#errors-exit-1))
- **Status line.** On Windows, while Claude Code's status line still runs `coding.sh statusline`, as one installed by 0.1.7 does, `doctor` warns and `update --refresh-bin` and `update` print `codingsh statusline install`. Claude Code runs the line in PowerShell when Git Bash is not installed, and PowerShell can't run it. None of them rewrites `settings.json`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/status-line.md#install-it))
- **Cost.** GPT-6.2 Astra and Sol and Gemini 4 Neon, Hydrogen and Carbon have their own price rows. GPT-6.2 Astra was estimated at GPT-6 Astra's higher rates, and the three Gemini models had no cost. GPT-6.2 flex calls get standard rates, and its Ultrafast is 3x. Antigravity's `-orchestrator` models are priced as the Gemini 4 model they run. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/track-cost.md#where-cost-comes-from))
- **Cost.** GPT-6 Rosalind is priced at OpenAI's promotional $10 input and $50 output per million tokens, which OpenAI says runs at least through December 31, 2026. Before, it was priced at $15 and $75. GPT-6 Cyber prompts over 272K tokens get its long-context rates; before, they got short-context rates. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/track-cost.md#where-cost-comes-from))
- **Evals.** The `review create --assign` help says only the `serve` read key with that name sees the items, while the CLI sees every item. It said keys with no item of their own saw them too. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#review-queues))
- **CLI.** `coding.sh cache` with no subcommand runs `cache doctor`, its only subcommand, and takes the same options. Before, it stopped with "Missing command" and named doctor. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-cache))
- **Serve.** A session of OpenTelemetry traces that share a conversation id (`gen_ai.conversation.id`, an AI SDK `sessionId`) counts in `coding_sh_sessions_total` on `/metrics`. Such a session sends no end, so it counts once, when serve stores its first trace; before, only its cost was counted. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#17-metrics-and-logs))
- **OTel.** A model call's span with `gen_ai.server.time_to_first_token` gives its time to first token, read in seconds as OpenLIT sends it, or in milliseconds from Strands Agents' Python tracer. Before, a session of such spans had no time to first token on its Latency line. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#post-v1traces-post-v1logs-post-v1metrics))
- **OTel.** A root or subagent session from GenAI spans lists its `session.start` first, then the prompt it takes from a model call, though both have the agent's start time. Exporters send a span after the spans under it, so before, `show` could list a subagent's prompt first, both at +0ms. Traces stored before keep their order. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#43-plain-opentelemetry-no-sdk))
- **Workflows.** `workflows show`, `stats`, `score` and `replay` take a unique prefix of a run id, 4 or more characters, as the session commands do. A prefix that several runs share fails and lists them, newest first. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#capture-and-measure-a-run))
- **Workflows.** `workflows show` prints the first 6 lines of what a run returned, as stored and redacted, on an `Output` row, and `--json` prints all of it as `run.output`. Before, a run's stored output showed nowhere. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#capture-and-measure-a-run))
- **Workflows.** When `workflows run` or `workflows score` refuses a case, the error prints each path resolved, with one kind of separator, and points at `--cases` only when the cases came from the default folder or `--cases` named no folder. Before, it mixed slashes and suggested `--cases` again for a folder it was given. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#write-cases-with-known-answers))
- **Work.** `work show`, `work ack` and `work snooze` take a prefix of a work item id, 4 or more characters, that only one item in the window starts with. A prefix that several items share fails and lists them, newest first. Before, an item id worked only whole. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#surfaces))
- **MCP.** `resources/read` takes the session ids the tools take: `codingsh://session/<id>` accepts a unique prefix of 4 or more characters, `latest` and a subagent's own id. A prefix several sessions share fails with invalid params and lists them, as the tools do. Before, only a whole id was found. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#ask-your-agent))
- **Privacy.** `purge` reads packed snapshots when the store's folder is 121 characters or longer on Windows, and names a snapshot repo whose objects git can't read as one that may hold the value. Before, git could not open such a pack, and purge passed over its objects as holding nothing. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#remove-a-leaked-secret))
- **CLI.** Options that take a whole number, such as `--limit` and `--trials`, take decimal digits only: `1e3`, `0x10` and `5.0` are refused instead of read as numbers. The error quotes the value as typed and words the range the same way for every option and for `limit:` in `--where`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#output-and-exit-codes))
- **CLI.** `stats --since` with a time in the future and no `--until` says `--since` is after now and prints both times, instead of asking for it to be before a `--until` that was not given. It still exits 2. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-stats))
- **Config.** A bad config value prints as written, `got -1` or `got "5"`, without a type name such as `number` in front of it. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#where-settings-come-from))
- **Search.** `search ""` and a search for blank text are a usage error that exits 2, not a search that finds nothing. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#search-every-session))
- **CLI.** An unknown `--agent` with no close match lists the agent ids to pick from, on `init`, `doctor`, `ingest`, `uninstall`, `harness audit`, `harness snapshot`, `eval run` and `eval trigger`. A close match is still suggested. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/agents.md#coding-agents))
- **Query.** `--where` given more than once joins the queries with AND, as if written in one, instead of keeping only the last. A later `sort:` or `limit:` wins. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md#filter-with---where))
- **Query.** When `sessions --flag` or a `flag:` term finds nothing and the name is close to a flag coding.sh sets or a stored session carries, `sessions` exits 2 and names that flag. A name a stored session carries is not a typo. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#usage-errors-exit-2))
- **Work.** `work snooze` on an item blocked on you says it stays hidden until the snooze ends, even if it waits on you again. Before, it said the item comes back sooner if it moves to a more urgent bucket, and no bucket is more urgent. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#acknowledging-and-snoozing))
- **CLI.** An error whose next step needs the user's approval no longer tells a person at a terminal to ask the user: the step prints as is. The line still prints without a terminal, and at one when a variable agents set is present (`CLAUDECODE`, `AI_AGENT`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID`), as an agent reads it there. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#errors-by-reason))
- **Store.** When a second install refuses to migrate the store (`store_owned`, `store_version`, or the `--restore` hint of `store_newer`), the command it names runs that install: `node <its cli.mjs> upgrade-store`, unless the bin on PATH starts it. Before, it named `coding.sh upgrade-store`, which ran the install on PATH. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#store-version-errors))
- **Store.** After an index upgrade too small to print progress (under 100,000 events), a person at a terminal now reads one dim line naming the copy of the index it kept and the command that puts it back. Before, only big stores said where the copy was. The commands in these lines name `codingsh` in PowerShell and cmd. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#the-first-command-after-an-upgrade-is-slow))
- **CLI.** In PowerShell and cmd, the fix `run` prints when `--` is missing quotes it (`codingsh run "--" claude`), as PowerShell drops a bare `--`. A missing argument's `Usage:` line and the commands inside `exp ab`'s report and `refine`'s hints and log now name `codingsh` there, and npx's command under npx, instead of `coding.sh`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows))
- **Evals.** `eval candidates` and `eval init --sessions` say in plain words why each session is left out and what would change that. A session whose repository is no longer on this machine is left out with that reason, where it ranked first and `eval init` then failed on it. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#your-agents-failed-sessions))
- **Evals.** `eval candidates` and `eval init --sessions` take a `coding.sh run --` session's prompt from its command's prompt argument, as `eval new` does, where they left it out for having no prompt. One that changed no files, or whose agent recorded its own session inside it, is left out with that reason. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#your-agents-failed-sessions))
- **Evals.** `eval new` takes a wrapped command's `--prompt=<value>` or `--task=<value>` as the instruction only when the value has a space in it, as it already did for `--prompt <value>`, so `--task=lint` is no longer read as a prompt. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/session-to-eval.md#one-session-at-a-time))
- **Evals.** On Windows a trial no longer fails when another process holds its work folder for a moment while the verifier's tree is swapped in: each move waits briefly and tries again. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#verifier-isolation))
- **Evals.** In PowerShell and cmd, the commands that `eval run`, `eval init`, `eval new`, `eval validate` and `eval trigger` print inside notes and reports name `codingsh`: the `show` beside a failed trial, and the import, validate, compare and init steps. They printed `coding.sh`, which PowerShell can't run. Started with npx, they print npx's form. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#the-report))
- **Evals.** A trial whose agent program is not installed or not on PATH counts as unknown, not failed, and is retried once like a failed setup step. Its reason says `the agent could not start: claude was not found; install it or put it on PATH`, and the verifier no longer grades the untouched start. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#long-runs-resume-retries-status))
- **Evals.** `eval trigger` names each expected route once, summed over the prompts that expect it: two prompts that expect `docx` read `docx 2/2`. They read `docx 1/1, docx 1/1`, one entry per prompt. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#skill-triggers))
- **Evals.** `eval run` on a case suite names each line of the cases file it leaves out for repeating an earlier line's input and expected answer, and `--json` lists them in `duplicates`. A file of 10 lines reported 9 cases with no word on which one went. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#an-app-or-a-prompt))
- **Evals.** `eval candidates` and `eval init --sessions` count a session whose last test command failed as tests still failing, which adds to its score. Before, a test script that exits 1 with plain output, such as `node test-sum.js`, gave no failure counts, and the failed session could rank last. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#your-agents-failed-sessions))
- **Query.** When `export`, `search` or `usage` finds nothing, a `--flag` or `flag:` name close to a known flag, or an `--agent` close to an agent id, exits 2 and names the one meant, as `sessions` does. `export --evidence --force` refuses before it clears the directory. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#usage-errors-exit-2))
- **Sessions.** `coding.sh sessions` and `coding.sh show` name the host app that drove a session: the AGENT cell reads `claude-code via t3code`, so a row that `--agent t3code` lists says why it matched. `--json` keeps `agent` and `host` apart, as before. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#list-your-sessions))
- **CLI.** A command an agent runs in a terminal (Codex runs some in one) no longer prompts: with AI_AGENT, CLAUDECODE, CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID or CODEX_THREAD_ID set, a step that needs consent stops and tells the agent to ask the user, as it does without a terminal, and progress prints as plain lines. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#output-and-updates))
- **CLI.** In PowerShell or cmd, an unknown option or extra argument after a value with a space says how to write a double quote inside a value. Windows PowerShell 5.1 passes such quotes on unescaped, so a `--notify` command with inner quotes reached coding.sh in pieces and failed with only `Unknown option -Path`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#usage-errors-exit-2))
- **Work.** A second `coding.sh work snooze` of an item replaces the first, even when run with a shorter `--since`. Before, a shorter snooze made after the item moved to a more urgent bucket left it in the queue, as the earlier one ended later, and the earlier one hid the item again once the new one ended. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#acknowledging-and-snoozing))
- **Cost.** A token count below 0 or not a finite number bills nothing: a model call no longer gets a negative, NaN or infinite cost estimate, and stored estimates are recomputed once. Tool evals, benchmarks and replays bill it as nothing too, and a tool eval no longer takes it off another call's tokens. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/track-cost.md#where-cost-comes-from))
- **Doctor.** `doctor` and `doctor --conformance` print their rows to stdout, not stderr, as `status` does, so `coding.sh doctor | less` and `coding.sh doctor > doctor.txt` keep the whole report and not only its last line. The next steps stay on stderr. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#sessions-dont-show-up))
- **Viewer.** The session list names the app that drove an agent after its name, `claude-code via T3 Code`, so a row that `agent:t3code` lists says why it matched. The page drawn before its script runs reads the same. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#host-apps-t3-code))
- **CLI.** The one-time GitHub star line waits for a person. A command run with `CLAUDECODE`, `AI_AGENT`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` or `CODEX_THREAD_ID` set no longer prints it, even at a terminal, where Codex runs some commands, so an agent no longer uses it up before you see it. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#updates))
- **Alerts.** A notification that was not delivered is sent again. Each `alert check` sends the change until a delivery gets through, with `previous` set to the state the target last got; before, the new state was saved and the change was lost. The cut-off after 5 failed deliveries in a row still applies. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/alerts.md#when-a-delivery-fails))
- **Alerts.** `alert add --help` shows a command target for Windows, `"command:node C:\alerts\notify.mjs"`, beside the `notify-send` one, and on Windows the error for an empty `--notify command:` suggests it. A command target runs in cmd.exe there, where `notify-send` fails. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/alerts.md#run-a-command))
- **Budgets.** A repo budget counts the sessions `sessions --repo` lists for its path: set on a checkout's root, or on a folder in a linked worktree, it takes in every worktree of the repo. Before, it counted only the sessions at or under the path, so a repo budget's spend may rise. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/track-cost.md#set-a-budget))
- **Budgets.** The 429 from `proxy --enforce-budget` names `budget status` as the shell the proxy started in runs it: `codingsh budget status` in PowerShell or cmd, where `coding.sh budget status` fails. Before, it always said `coding.sh budget status`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#stop-calls-over-a-budget))
- **Budgets.** `proxy --enforce-budget` matches a call to the agent the proxy stores it under. A Codex call that sends no session id is stored as agent `proxy`, so a `--agent proxy` budget now blocks it and a `--agent codex` budget no longer does. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#stop-calls-over-a-budget))
- **Docs.** The proxy guide gives the PowerShell form of `eval "$(coding.sh proxy env)"`: `codingsh proxy env | Invoke-Expression`. It says the budget 429 carries `x-should-retry: false`, so the Anthropic and OpenAI SDKs raise it at once, and the cost guide says `--enforce-budget` ignores `--repo` budgets as well as `--where` ones. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#2-point-your-client-at-it))
- **Docs.** The alerts guide is a page on the site, under Look at runs in the docs sidebar, and the troubleshooting row for `delivery_failed` links its Run a command section. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/alerts.md#run-a-command))
- **Cache.** `cache doctor` names the CLI in its `diff-calls` examples, in text and in `--json`, as your shell runs it: `codingsh` in PowerShell, and through `npx` when npx started it, where `coding.sh` fails. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-cache-doctor))
- **Cache.** `diff-calls` warns when the second call ran before the first, and names the command with the two swapped, or when the two calls come from different sessions. Before, its Cache and Why rows read such a pair as one call after the other in one conversation. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-diff-calls))
- **Replay.** A run made with `exp run --replay` keeps its task spec, so it can be the source of the next replay with a control arm, as the docs say. Before, that replay stopped with `no_control`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/replay-llm-calls.md#what-is-stored))
- **Replay.** When a replay's source stored no task spec, the `no_control` error suggests a command you can paste, and its message names `--no-control`. Before, the suggested command ended in prose, so pasting it failed. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#usage-errors-exit-2))
- **Replay.** The replay noise floor line says `first live call 2` when every case-trial went live at the same call, instead of `first live call 2–2 (median 2)`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/replay-llm-calls.md#3-read-the-result))
- **Replay.** `replay --fork` prints its keep-this-step command and the retry after `nothing_to_check` with every change flag you gave and `-k`, each argument quoted for your shell. Before, the keep line dropped `--system` and `-k` and left a `--with` path with a space unquoted, and the retry dropped every change flag. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#fork-from-a-step))
- **Replay.** `replay --fork` on a prompt step says it forks the agent's first call after the prompt, the call it uses. Before, it said the agent's last call before the step. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#fork-from-a-step))
- **Replay.** With no API key, `replay --fork` and `exp replay` print a line that sets it in the shell you use, a `$env:` line in PowerShell, and the `exp replay` usage example names the CLI the way your shell runs it. Before, PowerShell got a Bash `export` line and `coding.sh`, which fail there. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#errors-exit-1))
- **Replay.** `replay` on a session logged only by the proxy, or by an SDK call outside `trace()`, says why it has no steps and points to `coding.sh show`, which lists its model calls. Before, it printed `0 steps` with no reason, and `--step 1` said the step was past the last one. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/use-cases/replay-agent-session.md#which-agents-can-i-replay))
- **Replay.** `replay --step` prints its see-the-whole-file command with the path quoted for your shell. Before, a path such as `app/(auth)/login/page.tsx` was printed bare, which Bash reads as a syntax error and PowerShell as a command to run. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/use-cases/replay-agent-session.md#read-one-step))
- **Replay.** `exp run --replay` with its control arm warns when every model call of your task matched the recording, so it made no live call. The replay key leaves out the system prompt, so a change to it alone is never sent. Before, such a run printed a comparison with no difference and no hint why. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/replay-llm-calls.md#3-read-the-result))
- **MCP.** The `status` tool shows a password in the store URL as `***`, as `coding.sh status` does, and so does the error of a store that fails to open. Before, the password went into the agent's context. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/stores.md#pick-a-store))
- **Work.** `work` and `work show` write a pull request of the item's repo as `#N` when its sessions ran in a linked worktree, as in the main checkout. Before, a worktree folder named unlike the repo made linked PRs, or all PRs next to another repo's, read `owner/name#N`. `work --json` adds `repoMain`, the main checkout's root. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#work-items))
- **Work.** A failed item with more than one session says whose end its reason times (`main session ended 6h ago on api 529`, or a workflow run's `failed agent`), and its stage says `last active 4h ago` for the item's last activity where it said `stopped 4h ago`, so the two times no longer read as contradictory. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#the-queue))
- **Improve.** The MCP `improve` and `harness_changes` tools say when only headless runs used a change, and how many, as `improve check` does, and `harness_changes` takes `headless: true` to count them. The next steps `improve check` prints (count the headless sessions, re-check with `--watch`) keep the flags you gave, such as `--since` and `--agent`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#did-your-last-change-help))
- **Store.** The first command after an upgrade or a price change no longer waits while stored cost estimates are repriced: the background ingest reprices them, folding each session once, and sessions show their old estimates until it finishes. With `CODING_SH_NO_AUTO_INGEST=1` the command still reprices first. `usage --reprice` waits up to 30 seconds while another process reprices or rebuilds stats. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#the-first-command-after-an-upgrade-is-slow))
- **Store.** The line that closes an index upgrade counts the repricing the same command ran and names the parts (`index upgraded in 24.7s: migrate 1.2s, reprice 23.5s`) instead of timing the migration alone. A repricing that runs past 2 seconds at a terminal says how far it is, once a second. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#the-first-command-after-an-upgrade-is-slow))
- **Sessions.** A session id whose shortest prefix runs past 40 characters or needs quotes in a shell, like a long T3 Code thread id, prints as an alias such as `x_d52r41f3` in `work`, its next steps and ambiguous-id lists. `show`, `tree`, `replay`, `work show`, call references (`<session>#<n>`) and the MCP tools take the alias. Stored ids do not change. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md#session))
- **Harness.** `coding.sh harness tools` reads the window's model calls first, and when none stored a tools array or sent tools through the proxy, it reads no session's events. Before, it read every session in the window to report nothing. The answer is the same. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#catch-a-tool-description-that-changed))
- **Harness.** `harness audit` no longer counts a written file that is gone from disk as write-only. It is flagged `deleted` and listed after the files still on disk, and `--json` counts it in `totals.deleted` and the `harness_audit` MCP tool in `unused.deleted`, apart from `writeOnly`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#memory-and-instruction-files))
- **Viewer.** Work headings count the whole bucket ("119 of 671") instead of only the cards the page loaded, and Show all lists up to 1,000 items in place, beside the CLI command that lists them all. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer))
- **Work.** When the index is old, `work show` counts a review item's age from the last ingest, as `work` and the Work page do, so all three give the same reason. [Work](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#an-old-index)
- **Capture.** T3 Code's store is read where it is while T3 Code has it open, each thread in one short read transaction, instead of copied to the temp directory on every read. A SQLite store is copied only when the temp directory keeps room to spare, and a refused T3 copy names the space needed and free. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#host-apps-t3-code))
- **Sessions.** `sessions`, `tree`, `work`, `blame` and `attribution` print a session whose id is `latest` or `last` by its `x_` alias, which opens it. They printed the word, which opens your newest session. Some `x_` ids and subagents printed a longer id than they needed; they print the shortest that opens them. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#sessions-dont-show-up))
- **Cost.** After `coding.sh gateway enrich`, a session costs what the gateway billed, also when new prices are applied to its calls while enrich runs. Before, a call's new estimate could be added on top of the bill. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/integrations/ai-gateway.md#cost-and-provider-per-call))
- **Capture.** Redaction replaces a private key whose `BEGIN` line overlaps another `BEGIN` line, as in a header repeated without a line break. Before, the key data after it could be kept. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#redaction))
- **Capture.** A vendor token after a secret name, as in `GITHUB_TOKEN=...`, `"api_key": "..."`, `?token=...` or a password in a URL, keeps its own kind in the redaction counts, so the session gets the `secret:exposed` tag. Before, the name's rule replaced the token's marker and it counted as a generic secret. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#redaction))
