- **Capture.** Redaction covers more secret formats: keys from more than twenty other services and tools with a fixed prefix, private keys in base64, Azure storage account keys, `Authorization` headers with any scheme, cookies, URL signatures, `.netrc` passwords, passwords in URLs with no user name, and values set after names like `client_secret` or `aws_secret_access_key` in config files. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#redaction))
- **Capture.** The node hook redacts a JSON document inside a string as that document also when a rule has changed the text around it, so a value that only its key marks as secret is replaced before it reaches the spool, and each value is counted once. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#the-raw-spool))
- **Capture.** Matching a `capture.exclude` glob takes time in proportion to the glob and the path, so a glob with many stars, such as one a repo's `.coding.sh/config.toml` sets, no longer stalls ingest. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#capture))
- **Capture.** A repo's `.coding.sh/config.toml` can turn snapshots off or lower `snapshot.maxFiles` and `snapshot.timeoutMs`, but no longer turn snapshots on or raise their limits over your own config. A snapshot keeps files as they are, not redacted. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#snapshot))
- **Capture.** The OTLP receiver that `coding.sh collector` and `compare --otel` start gives a client 20 seconds to send its request headers and 120 seconds for the whole request, as `coding.sh serve` does, instead of the 60 seconds and 5 minutes Node allows by default. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#which-process-serves-what))
- **Capture.** Spans and logs an agent exports during `compare --otel` are redacted with your `capture.redact` and `capture.redactPatterns` rules, as ingest does, instead of only the built-in secret rules. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#opentelemetry-per-run))
- **Capture.** The node hook, and ingest when it rewrites a spool side file, replace a value nested too deep to copy with `[redacted:too-deep]`, as the index does. The node hook now writes a payload with a JSON document that deep inside a string, instead of dropping it. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#redaction))
- **MCP.** On Windows, coding.sh no longer exits when it runs git in a folder whose path is 259 characters or longer, so the MCP server answers a tool call that names one. That git call, or one in a missing folder, fails with an error that says why. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#from-inside-your-agent))
- **Install.** `uninstall` removes coding.sh from an agent's other config files when it can't read or parse one of them, leaves that file as it is, prints the error and exits 1. Before, it removed nothing from that agent and could say that nothing was installed. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#uninstall))
- **Capture.** On Windows, the node hook writes an event whose agent name Windows keeps for a device, such as `con` or `nul.txt`, or ends in a dot, which Windows drops from a folder name, under `spool/unknown`, as it does for other names that are not safe folder names. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md#the-raw-spool))
- **Work.** After `work snooze`, the note and `work snooze --help` say the item shows only while it is in a more urgent bucket until the snooze ends. Before, they said it comes back sooner, as if the snooze ended. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/work.md#acknowledging-and-snoozing))
- **Sessions.** Antigravity's `view_file_outline` and `view_code_item` are reads; files its `run_command` or Droid's `Execute` writes count in `filesChanged`. Shell paths resolve against `Cwd` or Gemini's `dir_path`, and `/c/x` is `C:\x` when the working directory is on a Windows drive. `cat list | xargs rm` is not a read. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid))
- **Sessions.** Checks that read a shell command now read Antigravity's `run_command` line, so `stats.waste`, `stats.memoryUse`, `stats.toolErrors` and the `no-progress`, `looping`, `stalled` and `failure-streak` flags see it: a shell write is progress, `sleep` or `watch` is waiting, a search that finds nothing is an answer, not a failure, and flag details name the command. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid))
- **Sessions.** An agent CLI started from Antigravity's `run_command` or Droid's `Execute` (`codex exec`, `claude -p`) links to that call as its parent. A call that names an absolute folder (`Cwd`, `workdir`) is matched in that folder. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid))
- **Sessions.** A search counts as empty in `stats.retrieval` when it printed only grep's warnings, zero counts from a pipeline whose last command counts (`grep -c x`, `grep x | wc -l`), or the lines a literal `echo` in the same command prints. A `grep -q` search is empty only on status 1. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid))
- **Capture.** After `ingest --backfill --since`, a later import skips each Claude Code transcript, Codex rollout and T3 Code thread the window imported whole while it is unchanged. Before, an import with no window, such as the one a read runs for T3 Code, read all of them again. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#transcripts-headless-runs-isolation))
- **CLI.** `blame`, `attribution` and `tree`'s in: line name each session by the shortest reference that opens it, not by 8 characters many T3 Code thread ids share. In `sessions`, `tree`, `search`, `status` and `blame`'s session table, an id over 40 characters, one that needs quotes in a shell, or `latest` or `last` prints as that reference. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#search-every-session))
- **Sessions.** Checks that read a shell command share one list of shell tools, so `unapproved-action`, `test-tamper`, commit attribution and the test command `eval init` guesses see a command run through Antigravity's `run_command`, Droid's `Execute` or Devin's `exec` as through Bash. `eval init` also finds one run through `shell_command` or `powershell`. ([docs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid))

## [0.1.7] - 2026-11-16

### Added

- **Sessions.** A session the model API held up gets the `api-stall` flag: three or more 429 or 529 errors, a minute of retry waits, retries run out, or a last rate-limit error it never got past. One 429 that a retry got past is not flagged. [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md)
- **Search.** `coding.sh search` and bare words in `--where` find sessions by their error text: error events (the message, a type such as `rate_limit_error`, the status code), failed model calls and tool errors. Errors stored before are indexed once on upgrade. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md)
- **Export.** The OTLP sink sends `gen_ai.input.messages` and `gen_ai.output.messages` as structured values (a list of `{role, parts:[{type, content}]}`) instead of a JSON string, as the GenAI semantic conventions now require. Each text part is cut at 8192 characters. [Sinks](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#sinks)
- **Evals.** The `objective` grader scores a measured number (speed, size, cost) against two dated anchors on a clipped log or linear ratio, so runs that all pass a test still rank; a `gate` grader that fails scores 0. [Graders](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **CLI.** Three days after first use, a successful command prints one dim line asking for a GitHub star, and `coding.sh ui` shows a small card after 5 sessions. The line prints once; the card shows until you dismiss it or click Star. Neither makes a network call. `CODING_SH_NO_STAR_PROMPT=1` turns both off. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#updates)
- **Checks.** `npm run gate` runs every pre-commit check in one pass under a throwaway home: repo hygiene, branch freshness, lint, actionlint, generated files, build and tests, store plugins, the Python SDK, the site, a secret scan and adapter versions. A failing step prints the command that reruns it. [Contributing](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md)
- **Lint.** New rules: vendor-literal, site-voice, universal-transition, reduced-motion, global-install and personal-data. `slop.mjs --strict-allowlist` fails on allowlist entries that no longer match anything. [Contributing](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md)
- **Doctor.** `coding.sh doctor` counts recent model calls past a physical limit (speed, time to first token, duration) and names the session with the most. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md)
- **SDK.** `coding_sh.weights_edit()` in the Python SDK records each weight edit a script makes as one `notification` event with `payload.kind: "weights.edit"`: tensor name, shape, weights changed, L2 and max-abs of the change, and the base and edited sha256. It adds no event kind, and `coding_sh.weights_delta()` computes the counts and norms for small arrays without numpy. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **SDK.** `weightsEdit()` in the TypeScript SDK records a weight edit as the same `notification` event the Python SDK's `weights_edit()` sends, and returns `true` only when the event was queued. `weightsDelta()` computes the shape, count and norms for nested arrays and typed arrays. Both SDKs' tests check one fixture of the events an edit must send. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **Sessions.** A session that recorded weight edits gets `stats.weightEdits` (edits, distinct tensors, weights changed) and the `weights:edited` tag, and is listed even when it holds nothing else. `coding.sh show`, the viewer and MCP `session_get` print one line per edit, and `--where 'weights.changed>1000'` filters by size. Stored sessions are folded again. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **OpenTelemetry.** A span or log record named `weights.edit` with `coding_sh.weights.*` attributes is read as a weight edit, and the `otlp` sink sends one `weights.edit` span per edit (so does `[serve.forward]`, for edits posted to `/v1/ingest`). The `invoke_agent` span carries `coding_sh.stats.weight_edits` and `coding_sh.stats.weights_changed`. Metadata mode keeps only the counts and norms. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **Evals.** `coding.sh exp run --weights <SHA256>` records which checkpoint a run's task ran (the SHA-256 of the weights file, which you compute). `exp compare`, `exp show`, `exp list`, the viewer and the MCP tools show the stamp, and a comparison warns when both sides ran the same weights or only one recorded them. [Compare an edited model with its base](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#compare-an-edited-model-with-its-base)
- **SDK.** `coding_sh.weights_checkpoint()` and `weightsCheckpoint()` record the file a script saved its edited weights to: one `notification` event with `payload.kind: "weights.checkpoint"`, the file's SHA-256 (the value `exp run --weights` takes) and optionally the base file's. Both SDKs' tests check one fixture. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **Sessions.** A session that recorded a weights checkpoint lists the file hashes in `stats.weightEdits.checkpoints` and gets the `weights:checkpoint` tag. `coding.sh show`, the viewer and MCP `session_get` print `checkpoint 58899f90572c ← 0496e5111097`, and `--where 'weights.checkpoint:58899f90572c*'` finds the session. Metadata capture keeps both hashes. Stored sessions are folded again. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **OpenTelemetry.** A span or log record named `weights.checkpoint` with `coding_sh.weights.sha256` is read as a checkpoint. The `otlp` sink and `[serve.forward]` send one `weights.checkpoint` span per event. Metadata mode keeps the two file hashes and drops the note. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **Evals.** `exp show` and `exp compare` name the session whose checkpoint recorded an experiment's `--weights` hash, with its edits. `--json` and the MCP tools carry the ids as `weights.sessions`. `coding.sh show <session>` and MCP `session_get` list the experiments that ran its checkpoints. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/experiments#weights)
- **Viewer.** A weights stamp on the experiment and compare pages links the session that recorded that file as a checkpoint, and the session's header links each experiment that ran it. `/api/compare`, `/api/experiments/{id}` and `/api/sessions/{id}` carry the same ids as the CLI's `--json`. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/experiments#weights)
- **Evals.** `coding.sh exp compare A B --given <grader>` compares only the cases where that grader passes in both runs, and prints how many it kept and dropped. With a `terminated` grader this separates "finished more answers" from "got more of the finished answers right". [Compare an edited model with its base](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#compare-an-edited-model-with-its-base)
- **MCP.** `coding.sh mcp` serves four Agent Skills over the MCP skills extension (`io.modelcontextprotocol/skills`): `improve-agent-setup`, `trim-agent-setup`, `review-agent-session` and `build-agent-evals`. A host that supports the extension lists them and loads one when a request matches. [Skills](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp#skills)
- **MCP.** In 2026-07-28 requests, list, read and discover results carry `ttlMs` and `cacheScope`: the tool, prompt and template lists are cached for an hour, `server/discover` for five minutes, and sessions are not cached. [MCP server](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp#protocol)
- **Site.** The front page is also in German, Spanish, French, Japanese, Korean, Brazilian Portuguese and Simplified Chinese, at /de, /es, /fr, /ja, /ko, /pt-br and /zh. Each page names the others with hreflang links and the sitemap lists them as alternates. A switcher in the footer moves between them. The docs, blog and changelog stay in English. [简体中文](https://coding.sh/zh)

### Changed

- **Sessions.** `stats.filesChanged` counts files an agent wrote from a shell (`sed -i`, `>`, `tee`, `cp`, `mv`, `Set-Content`, `Out-File`), not only through edit tools. A file edited both ways counts once. [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md)
- **Capture.** A backfill writes each batch of events in one transaction, and the read marks of its transcript files a few thousand to a transaction, instead of committing once per session and once per mark. A 2-day Claude Code import (26,000 events) took about 155 s instead of 222 s. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md)
- **Capture.** T3 Code, Goose and Antigravity databases are read from a read-only copy, so closing it no longer folds the copied write-ahead log into a file about to be deleted. `ingest --rebuild --agent t3code` went from 47 s to 14 s on a 600 MB T3 Code store. [Host apps (T3 Code)](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#host-apps-t3-code)
- **Harness.** `harness audit` finds nested instruction files from typed directory listings, nearest directories first, and counts every directory it checks toward its 2,000-directory cap: 4,400 file checks instead of 85,700 over 38 session directories. [Memory and instruction files](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#memory-and-instruction-files)
- **Store.** `gc` finds expired tool output and paged model requests with two SQLite queries instead of reading every stored event, 3 to 5 times faster on a 26,000-event store. [gc](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-gc)
- **Site.** The terse-mode guide is now a blog post that leads with what we measured on one plugin (output −8%, cost +11%, same pass rate and clarity), then shows how to run the same test on your tasks. /docs/terse-mode answers with a permanent redirect to it. [Does a terse mode save money?](https://coding.sh/blog/terse-mode)
- **Docs.** The docs home, the home page FAQ and llms.txt say what coding.sh does: it records prompts, model calls, tool calls, file changes and cost, shows each run in a local viewer, and turns failed runs into evals. Other products are named only on the compare pages and where they are an integration, and a site test enforces it. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs)
- **Docs.** `--help`, the package descriptions, the README, the site's description and llms.txt say what coding.sh records instead of "open-source, local-first observability and evals for any AI agent", and the site's status line shows the current release (it said v0.1.4). A site test fails on stacked category words in published text. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs)
- **Node.** The minimum Node is 22.16, because 22.15 has no SQLite FTS5. A store that lacks FTS5 now fails with reason `no_fts5` and names Node 22.16. CI runs the packed CLI on Node 22.16.0. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md)
- **CI.** New jobs: the static gate, the Postgres store, the Python SDK on 3.9 and 3.13, and the release smoke test. The site job fails instead of skipping when its build is missing. [Contributing](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md)
- **Stats.** `stats` rolls each subagent's cost, tokens and tool calls into its top-level session. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md)
- **Experiments.** `exp compare --gate` says "Don't ship" only when B's losses beat chance, by an exact one-sided binomial test on the cases that changed outcome. Losses within that noise hold. Each group under `--by` keeps the old rule, as refine's guard set does. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **CLI.** A JSON error's `next` is always a label and a command. A suggestion that needs the user's approval is labelled "With their approval:". Next-step hints shell-quote the names you typed and print full session ids. [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md)
- **Evals.** `judge grade` exits 2 with reason `grader_error` when the grader could not run, so a verifier can tell it apart from a fail verdict (exit 1). [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md)
- **Docs.** The docs are regrouped by task, with a quickstart for your agent or a coding agent, a first eval that ends with a fix, TypeScript and Python tabs, viewer screenshots, and Copy page and View as Markdown on every page. New pages cover cost, the query language, LLM judges, eval methods, deploying the server, privacy and the roadmap. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs)
- **Docs.** The README has a quickstart in three steps, a table of what you get with the detail folded under each area, and an agents table with what each status means. The CLI README lists every command, including `statusline`, `tree`, `replay`, `blame` and `sql`, and each package README links its docs. See the [README](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/README.md).
- **Site.** Docs pages no longer jump when the web fonts load on a phone: layout shift on /docs/cli fell from 0.208 to 0.0003 and on /docs/quickstart from 0.009 to 0.0002. Desktop screens get home screenshots sized to their slot, 1.6 kB less. [Site speed](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md#font-swap-and-image-sizes)
- **Site.** The edge answers moved URLs with permanent redirects, keeps a page prefetched on hover for the click, caches hashed images and search files for a year, compresses the changelog feed, and names the page as canonical on each Markdown copy. [Site speed](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md#font-swap-and-image-sizes)
- **Agent text.** llms.txt lists the blog and links every CLI command. llms-full.txt leaves out the per-version changelog pages and the per-command CLI pages (647 kB, was 1,046 kB), and each Markdown copy's `updated:` is its page's last change. [llms.txt](https://coding.sh/llms.txt)
- **Site.** The front page is new: the headline and install command, a moving row of the agents it works with, a diagram of agents feeding one record, three steps to start with a drawing of one session, and four short answers. Its footer lists the docs by section. [coding.sh](https://coding.sh)
- **Site.** The card shown when a link to coding.sh is shared has the front page's headline. [coding.sh](https://coding.sh)
- **Site.** The line under the front page's headline reads "Record, measure, compare and benchmark every agent run." Under the install command, a line links to the notes for the latest release. [coding.sh](https://coding.sh)

### Fixed

- **Capture.** A read that finds a capture pending no longer runs git while it ingests (the next ingest without a budget takes the snapshots), its transcript import saves its progress file by file, and only one process imports transcripts at a time (`tail.lock`). A read on a backlog could take minutes, and with auto-ingest off it never saved progress. [Config](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md)
- **CLI.** `coding.sh ingest` stops on the first Ctrl-C: what it read stays stored, the lock is released, and it exits 130 (`{"outcome":"interrupted"}` with `--json`). Run it again to finish. [CLI](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Viewer.** With subagents included, the sessions header no longer counts subagents twice: a parent with two subagents reads "1 session + 2 subagents" in both scopes, and the cost is marked partial when any session, subagents included, has no price. [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apistats)
- **Store.** Under Git Bash on Windows, `/tmp/…` and `/c/…` in a `sqlite://` or `file://` URL mean the shell's paths (the user's temp dir, `C:…`), not `C:	mp`. An index that can't be opened names the path it tried. [Config](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md)
- **Search.** Hits that rank the same come back newest first and the same on every call, where a page of tied hits was arbitrary. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md)
- **Evals.** An `objective` grader's `clip` must be at least 1, since a lower clip could never pass, and the bench score trend and the viewer's grader bars fit mean scores above 1 instead of drawing past the chart. [Scores above 1](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#scores-above-1)
- **CLI.** A read that finds a capture pending (`sessions`, `show`, `search`, `status`, `usage`) answers at its 1.5 s budget again: the reads of T3 Code's database and of transcripts of agents without hooks run in a worker it stops waiting for, where they took 5 to 12 s on one thread. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md)
- **Workflows.** `workflows list` reads at most 2 MB of changed run transcripts before it answers and leaves a live run's larger directory to a background `workflows import`, showing the last import with a `pending` field in `--json`, where it took 10 to 50 s while a run was live. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Capture.** The re-parse after an upgrade opens only the Claude Code and Codex transcripts of the sessions it rebuilds, so a store with tens of thousands of transcripts takes seconds to a minute instead of tens of minutes. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md)
- **Capture.** `ingest --rebuild --agent t3code` removes the T3 Code threads of Claude subagents that the Claude Code transcripts already store. A read's import starts no further agent's transcript scan once its budget is spent. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md)
- **Capture.** `ingest --backfill --since` imports a Claude Code transcript written inside the window whole, so a session that began before the window keeps its first prompt, title, branch and start time. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md)
- **Speed.** A read command's auto-ingest stops walking the transcript tree when its time budget runs out, and the walk lists project dirs ahead of the one being read. A large `~/.claude/projects` tree no longer costs seconds on every read. `workflows list` remembers its scan of the projects dir and stops at 1.5 s, saying so. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md)
- **Search.** `search --agent` filters on the session's agent, as `sessions --agent` does, and its JSON hits report that agent. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md)
- **Status.** `status` prints its report to stdout, not stderr. [CLI](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Export.** `export --json` is a usage error: export already prints one record per line. [CLI](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Workflows.** `workflows show` refreshes a run that was still running, and a group is `running` while any of its agents runs. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Config.** `config get` lists `refine.*` and `serve.forward.*` keys set in the config file. [Config](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md)
- **CLI.** A mistyped command or action with two letters swapped (`eval lsit`) suggests the right one. `judge slop` exits 1, not 2, when there is no diff to judge. [CLI](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Config.** The search for a repo's `.coding.sh/config.toml`, and `doctor`'s search for project hook settings, stop at the account's home even when `HOME`, or `USERPROFILE` on Windows, points elsewhere, and `doctor` stops at the git root. On Windows, where the temp dir sits under the profile, a command run there no longer reads `~/.coding.sh/config.toml` or `~/.claude/settings.json` as a project's. [Config](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md)
- **Sessions.** A subagent session stored as `<parent>:sub:<agentId>` with no `session.start` takes its parent from the id, so the default `sessions` list no longer shows it as a top-level run and capture health stops counting it as a failed capture. Stored sessions are folded again. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts)
- **Capture.** Codex backfill reads the compressed `rollout-*.jsonl.zst` files Codex writes for rollouts older than a week, so that history is no longer skipped. A plain `.jsonl` beside it is read instead, and a Stop hook whose transcript is gone reads the `.zst`. A cut-off `.zst` gives the events before the cut. Needs `zlib.createZstdDecompress` (Node 22.15 or newer). [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md)
- **Capture.** A Codex turn that ends with an error (`task_complete.error`) keeps it as an error event with `source: "api"`, its `errorType` and an `errorClass` such as `rate_limit`, so the session counts it as a failed model call. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md)
- **Sessions.** An API error event (source `api`, or an HTTP status of 400 or more) counts in `stats.llmErrors`, and the viewer shows `N model errors (429)` on agent sessions as well as API rows. Stored sessions are folded again. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/concepts)
- **Viewer.** Session ids up to 2048 characters open (T3 Code thread ids are long). The command menu shows `Ctrl K` outside a Mac and finds stored sessions that the loaded list leaves out. [Viewer](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer)
- **Viewer.** A loaded session is announced to screen readers, an empty list offers to include matching subagents, and a resume a few seconds after the start is no longer a phase. [Viewer](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer)
- **OTel.** Spans exported before their subagent's `invoke_agent` span move to the subagent's session when that span arrives, so the result no longer depends on how an exporter split the batches. [OTel](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md)
- **Evals.** `toolevals compare` reports pass^k and pass@k as a share of tasks, not a count, and a run where every trial failed on infrastructure is `void` and exits 1. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Evals.** `eval init --from-sessions` counts a session whose files changed only through Bash as edited, gives a repeated task name its directory name, and credits a T3 Code session to the agent that ran it. Drafting a task again keeps its `suite:` tags. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Evals.** `exp compare` prints no stability line when every difference is 0, and judge calibration no longer reports human labels as noisy when the judge gave every case the same verdict. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **MCP.** A tool call answers within its read budget while an ingest runs, sends `notifications/progress` to a host that passed a `progressToken`, and keeps a page under the size limit when it adds `pending`. [MCP](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Store.** A statement that fails with "database is locked" while a background ingest opens or closes the index is tried again for up to 5 s. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md)
- **Install.** `install.sh` under Git Bash on Windows looks for the `coding.sh` shim in npm's prefix, not `<prefix>/bin`, runs the binary it installed, and says which directory to add to PATH. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md)
- **Site.** The install script is served with `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` at both of its paths, /install and /install.sh. The two paths return the same file, and neither named a canonical. [Install](https://coding.sh/docs/install)
- **Sessions.** `coding.sh show`, the viewer and MCP `session_get` print a weight edit's or a checkpoint's `note` at the end of its line. Before, the note was stored but never shown. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **Sessions.** A session with no prompt, such as an SDK `trace()` that only records weight edits, is titled by its trace's name in `coding.sh sessions` and the viewer. Before, its title was empty. Stored sessions are folded again. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **Evals.** `exp metrics` prints a count or score below 0.0005 with two significant digits (`0.0001`) instead of `0.000`, so a small value such as a gain per edited weight no longer reads as zero. [Metric A/B](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#metric-ab-ms-bytes)
- **SDK.** `coding_sh.weights_edit()` reads a 0-d tensor or array as the number it holds, so `l2=delta.norm()` records the edit. Before, a torch scalar was refused and the edit was dropped with a warning, though the docs said torch scalars count as numbers. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/api)
- **SDK.** `coding_sh.weights_edit()` warns about a dropped edit once per process, as its docs say and as the TypeScript SDK does. Before, every `init()` warned again. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs/sdk)
- **MCP.** A request cancelled with `notifications/cancelled` gets no reply (a cancel for `initialize` is ignored), a cursor on `tools/list`, `prompts/list` or `resources/templates/list` is an error, a request with a null id gets an error instead of no answer (a null-id `notifications/` message is still a notification), and a 2025-03-26 session accepts batches. [MCP server](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp#protocol)
- **MCP.** Tool answers stay under 25,000 characters: a longer page has fewer rows and `limitUsed`, and a tool with a `cursor` also returns `nextCursor`. `session_replay` pages its files and cuts text and lists only when an answer would not fit, saying so with `truncated` (a range was 5 million characters). [MCP server](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp#tools)
- **MCP.** `eval_init` and `score_add` are marked destructive, since both can replace stored data, and `score_list`'s error names its `since` argument instead of the CLI's `--since`. [MCP server](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp#tools)
- **Site.** The changelog and quickstart pages no longer shift on a phone while loading: release area chips sit in a fixed grid and the quickstart tab list is laid out from first paint. Layout shift fell from 0.052 to 0 and from 0.023 to 0. [Site speed](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md#font-swap-and-image-sizes)
- **Stores.** A `file:///C:/...` blob URL and a `sqlite://user:password@/path` index URL open the path they name on Windows, and the user and password are no longer read as a folder. See [stores](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/stores.md).
- **Capture.** Model calls no longer show impossible speeds such as 3750 tok/s. A hook reading a transcript from mid-response, or late Codex lines, gave a call a start time inside the response; such a call now has no duration, time to first token or speed. A Claude Code call gets them back when the import reads the whole transcript. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around)
- **Tests.** The viewer's browser tests no longer hang when Chrome drops its DevTools connection: a waiting call fails at once, and any call fails after 30 s. The helper lets go of Chrome's pipe after closing it, and the CI runner ends each test file once its tests finish, so an open handle cannot hold a Windows job. [Contributing](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md)
- **Capture.** A blob store outage no longer drops spool events while the cursor moves on, and a full disk no longer leaves a torn spool line or loses the whole line `hook.sh` appends after one. Ingest redacts short payloads inside the spool file once their day has passed. A session run with `CODING_SH_DISABLE=1` no longer comes back through backfill. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md)
- **Doctor.** `doctor` reports a missing `hook.sh`, a spool file hooks cannot write and recent failed hook writes. `doctor` and `status` mask the password in a store URL. `init` exits 0 when only an agent's setup failed, and `init --dry-run` lists the bin files it would write. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md)
- **Init.** `init` registers no MCP server for an agent whose hooks it could not install, so a failed agent is left as it was. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md)
- **Stores.** `error:` matches an error event's message, a quoted `*` is a wildcard and `\*` a literal star, and the Postgres and ClickHouse stores compile `--where` as SQLite does. Concurrent `dataset add` runs merge instead of losing a version. An empty dataset import exits 1. [Query](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md)
- **Stores.** The index, its WAL files, blob packs and backups are written owner-only (0600), and new store folders 0700. On the SQLite store, a search of only emoji or symbols finds matches. `upgrade-store` on a plugin store says there is nothing to upgrade. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md)
- **Stores.** `tree`, `cache doctor` and `diff-calls` on a `--store` file that does not exist stop with `store_missing` instead of creating an empty index. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md)
- **Sessions.** A prefix of a parent session opens the parent, not one of its subagents. A bare agent id or an SDK id without its project opens that session. `latest` skips empty sessions and resolves an agent run under `coding.sh run` to its wrapper. Terminal tables keep session ids whole. [CLI](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **CLI.** Impossible dates such as 2026-11-31 are refused, `diff` prints call times in local time, `show` prints the end of a failed tool's full output, and `sessions` counts what it hid. `config get` and `config set` know every documented key, and `config set` prints the old value. [CLI](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Cache.** `cache doctor` keeps its priced total and counts breaks on an unpriced model apart. [CLI](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md)
- **Evals.** `toolevals` drivers honor ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL and OPENAI_BASE_URL, `judge calibrate` counts labels on case targets, `eval export` and `eval import` work across two homes, and `eval init --cases` works from a subdirectory. Task sandboxes no longer inherit `type: module` from a parent package.json. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Scores.** `score add` keeps an earlier comment when you leave it out and the value is unchanged. It refuses a run that does not exist and experiment targets, each with a next step. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Experiments.** `exp ab` gives each arm only the files the session's agent loads, and an edit that agent does not load stops with "No arm difference" before any model call. `exp show latest` works, and an HTTP 529 reads "overloaded". [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Bench.** A pair with over 10% of cases excluded says "no verdict" and why, and an interrupted run names the exact `--resume` command. [Benchmarks](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/benchmarks.md)
- **Improve.** Fix lines go to the file the flagged sessions' agent reads and name the test command it uses. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md)
- **Workflows.** A run folder copied under a second session is imported once, and agents silent for 15 minutes show as stalled. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md)
- **Serve.** `serve` honors the `[proxy]` section of `--config`, the collector refuses a host other than loopback, and the proxy rejects bad upstream URLs. [Production](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md)
- **SDK.** `require()` of the TypeScript SDK works, and both SDKs warn once on a bad collector URL. [SDK](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/sdk.md)
- **Viewer.** The live header refreshes in place, empty fields no longer show "null", server-rendered rows match the page's, and an unknown session id costs one request. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md)
- **Viewer.** Enter presses the focused button, filter chip or link, and Space the focused button, also inside an open list row. Before, they opened the selected session, folded the row or started the replay. The arrow keys scroll the focused step detail, and a redraw moves focus to the rebuilt control or its pane instead of dropping it. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer)
- **Packaging.** The published package.json no longer has `source` export conditions that point at files the tarball does not hold. `smoke.sh` runs under a throwaway home, and the GitHub Action has an `annotate` input. [Release](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/RELEASE.md)
- **Site.** The docs sidebar and the phone menu open on the current page, and the phone menu fits the screen. A terminal block no longer touches the paragraph above it, wide code blocks scroll by keyboard, code copy selects the code when the browser blocks the clipboard, and search results open without a redirect. [Docs](https://coding.sh/docs)
- **Site.** Changelog notes link docs to their page on the site instead of showing raw Markdown or going to GitHub, and the changelog counts only shipped releases. [Changelog](https://coding.sh/changelog)
- **Site.** Search snippets end on a whole word, structured data resolves on every page, sitemap dates follow each page's last change, and pages no longer carry hidden text for agents. The site build now fails on each of these. [Site](https://coding.sh/)
- **Site.** The skip link opens below the header, one Tab press crosses the agent logo row, the footer links sit in rows of four and three on a phone, and a long word wraps instead of widening the page. [Site](https://coding.sh/)

## [0.1.6] - 2026-11-11

### Added

- **Privacy.** `CODING_SH_PURGE_WAIT_MS` sets how long `purge` waits for another process's read of the index or the blob pack before it says which file still holds the old bytes. The defaults stay 10 s before the VACUUM and 5 s after it. [Configuration](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#paths-and-stores).
- **Trace format.** A JSON Schema (draft 2020-12) for `AgentEvent` and `Session`, generated from the TypeScript types, ships as `coding.sh/schema.json` and is served at https://coding.sh/schema.json. Every adapter and OTLP fixture validates against it. [Trace format](https://coding.sh/docs/trace-format#json-schema)
- **Serve.** `CODING_SH_FORWARD_URL` (or `[serve.forward]`) makes `coding.sh serve` forward every trace it commits to an OTLP/HTTP collector, with the same trace and span ids plus `coding_sh.project`. Native batches go out as `invoke_agent`, `execute_tool` and `chat` spans. Metadata mode sends no messages, tool arguments or results; a down collector never slows ingest, spans wait in a bounded buffer, and overflow is counted in `coding_sh_forward_spans_total`. [Forward and sinks](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#19-forward-and-sinks)
- **Sinks.** `[[sinks]]` now mirror events from the OTLP receiver (`coding.sh collector`) and from `coding.sh serve`, so a team can set one sink on serve instead of on every laptop. [Config](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#sinks)
- **Serve.** `createFetchHandler()` from `coding.sh/server` runs serve's ingest routes (OTLP, `/v1/ingest`, `/readyz`) as a `Request` → `Response` function for Vercel Functions, Deno, Bun or Workers. With `coding.sh-store-postgres`, several instances can write one session at once: each session rebuild takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the session id. [Serverless](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/production/serverless.md)
- **Evals.** Cases and tool-eval tasks take an optional `kind`: `bad` (fails before the change, passes after), `guard` or `control` (pass on both). `exp run`, `exp compare`, `toolevals run` and `toolevals compare` split results by kind and name broken controls as overcorrection, and `toolevals validate --against <variant>` reports a bad task that passes on the old variant. See [Case kinds](docs/guides/evals.md#case-kinds-bad-guard-control) and [Task kinds](docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#31-task-kinds-bad-guard-control).
- **Tool evals.** `coding.sh toolevals traces <server> --sessions 'since:30d'` (or `--from <experiment>`) prints each MCP tool's calls, errors, repeats and retries after an error, and `--out <dir>` writes the failing traces first (at most 3 passing), an `index.jsonl`, `tool-health.json` and `tools.json` for an improver to read. Held-out trials are never exported. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#121-toolevals-traces)
- **Tool evals.** `toolevals run --shell-model <provider/model>` has a model play the shell for a model driver on a CLI target, with a fixed date; the shell model is part of the arm name and `compare` refuses arms with different shells. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#42-model-driver-agent-free)
- **Tool evals.** The model driver's MCP client reads resources and prompts, counts progress notifications, runs task-augmented tool calls, answers sampling with the driver's model, elicitation with a task's scripted `[mcp]` answers and roots with the sandbox, and runs OAuth (discovery, client registration, PKCE or client credentials) on a 401. `calls` checks can match `resources/read` by uri and `prompts/get` by name. [MCP client](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#10-mcp-client)
- **Tool evals.** The model driver caches the prompt on Anthropic (top-level `cache_control`; `promptCache = false` turns it off) and reads the final cache counts from the stream; the report shows cost with cache prices beside flat prices, the cache hit share, and says when the cheaper arm flips. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#42-model-driver-agent-free)
- **Cache.** `diff-calls` gives the line where a system prompt edit starts and the share of the prompt after it that the cache could no longer read (`system.firstChangeLine`, `system.wastedPrefixFrac` in `--json` and the viewer API). When over 20% is lost, `Why` says to move dynamic content such as timestamps to the end of the system prompt. [Capture with the proxy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#4-see-why-the-prompt-cache-missed)
- **Experiments.** `compare --signals` checks whether each session flag and tool errors predict failure beyond task difficulty: pooled, within-task and within-task-and-bundle AUROC with a task-cluster CI, beside difficulty, bundle and length references, and a verdict (`adds-run-signal`, `tracks-difficulty`, `no-signal`, `too-few-pairs`). [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#do-the-flags-predict-failure)
- **Experiments.** `exp sweep` and a matrix spec in `exp run`, with three or more configs or cells, name the plausibly best set: the configs not significantly worse than the best, by a paired-bootstrap multiple comparison with the best, which holds the truly best config 95% of the time. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#matrix)
- **Experiments.** `compare --pick N --pick-by irt` fits a Rasch (or 2PL) model on stored runs and runs first the tasks with the most information at the baseline's ability: mid difficulty, sharp discrimination. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#picking-tasks-and-a-budget)
- **Experiments.** `exp run --record` saves each case-trial's Anthropic and OpenAI calls, and `exp run --replay <EXP>` serves them again up to the first changed call (`--replay-until N` stops after call N), so a late change re-runs only the calls it touches. System-prompt and `cache_control` edits still replay; API keys are never stored; with one task, the recorded config runs as a control arm and compare prints its noise floor. See [docs/how-to/replay-llm-calls.md](docs/how-to/replay-llm-calls.md).
- **Experiments.** `exp run --seq K --feedback bit|reasons|critic=<grader>` tries a failing case-trial again with feedback on its earlier attempts, up to K attempts, and reports Seq@1..K with intervals, EGS/LGS, unchanged revisions, cost to solve and, with 2+ trials, pass@k against seq@k by cost. The pass rate stays the first attempt's; feedback never shows the expected answer; `exp compare` adds a `seq@K` row and `--verdict seq` (arXiv 2610.37859). [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#sequential-attempts-with-feedback).
- **Experiments.** `exp run --failing <EXP>`, `--errors-from`, `--case <PATTERN>`, `--sample N --seed S` and `--first-n` run part of a dataset. When B ran only on A's failures, `exp compare` warns of regression to the mean, says inconclusive and `--gate` holds until A's config runs on the same cases. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#running-a-subset-failures-samples-the-first-n).
- **Experiments.** `exp run` stores the experiment so far while it runs; after a crash `exp list` marks it `(partial)`, and `exp run … --resume <experiment>` runs only what is left (`--retry-errors` also re-runs its task errors). A resume with other graders, trials, task, dataset version or case selection is refused and names what changed. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#long-runs-resume-retries-status).
- **Experiments.** `exp sweep --guard <dataset>|prior` re-runs the start and winning configs on earlier suites and keeps the start config, exit 1, when the winner breaks more than `--guard-tau` of what the start solved there, even when the target suite ships. Shipped sweeps are remembered in `adoptions.jsonl` for `--guard prior`. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#datasets-and-experiments).
- **Experiments.** `exp compare` takes `--pairwise` more than once: it reports each judge, Cohen's κ between judges, and `replicated-better`, `judges-disagree` or `unresolved`, screens out a judge whose calibration failed, and with `--gate` holds a ship when any judge prefers A or two judges do not both find B better (arXiv 2611.04090). [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#judges).
- **Experiments.** `exp compare --labels-a --labels-b` corrects each LLM judge grader's B − A with human labels of each arm's outputs (PPI++), and warns when the judge errs differently on the two arms. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#correcting-a-judges-delta-with-human-labels).
- **Refine.** `coding.sh refine mine` ranks failure patterns across sessions with no model call: session flags, normalized error lines (20 `TS2307` errors in 5 sessions are one pattern), instructions you typed again and again, and failed eval runs, each with its count, sessions and example events, redacted. `refine propose --mined FILE` shows the top patterns to the proposer. [Mining failure patterns](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#mining-failure-patterns).
- **Refine.** `coding.sh refine diagnose` asks a model what went wrong in recent sessions and groups the answers into Signals with stable ids; `refine signals` lists them. Every problem needs a citation that code checks against the redacted trace, severity is computed by code, replies are cached so a rerun makes no call, and with no API key it prints a $0 plan of the sessions and tokens it would send. [Diagnosing sessions into Signals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#diagnosing-sessions-into-signals).
- **Refine.** `refine test|run --suite --canary-model <model>` re-tests a kept edit that deletes or rewords lines (`--canary all`: every kept edit) under a cheaper model and rejects it as `canary-regression` when the pass rate falls there; `refine recheck <r_…> --model` runs the same check later. A subtractive edit kept without a canary gets a note. Wrapper agents take a `{model}` slot in their flags. [Canary re-test](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#canary-re-test).
- **Refine.** `coding.sh refine distill` writes an experiment's runs (or recent sessions) as a redacted corpus, leaving out held-out tasks, and has Claude Code or Codex analyze it with its own code and write one skill in an isolated directory. The skill is size-capped, checked by the critics and ledgered as a proposed candidate with its provenance for `refine test`. [Distilling a skill](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#distilling-a-skill).
- **Refine.** The proposal prompt lists, per earlier edit that was not kept, the tasks it fixed, regressed and left failing, read from the ledger, and asks for lines aimed at the still-failing tasks. With no such rounds the prompt is unchanged. [Refine loop](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#refine-loop).
- **Export.** `coding.sh export --evidence <dir>` writes each matching session as a folder of files: `index.md` with stats and a timeline, `session.json`, `events.jsonl`, and one file per tool call with its input and full output. Everything is redacted first, files are size-capped, and the same store gives the same bytes. [Query and export](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md#evidence-folders).
- **Privacy.** An opt-in PII pack: `capture.redact = ["secrets", "pii"]` also redacts email addresses, phone numbers, card numbers (issuer prefix and Luhn check), IBANs (country length and mod 97) and national ids (US SSN, UK NINO, Canadian SIN). Each kind (`phone`, `card`, `iban`, `national-id`, `email`) can be turned on alone. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md).
- **Evals.** Typed scores: a number, true or false, a category or text on a session, event, dataset case or eval run, from a person, judge, code, the API or a monitor. Score configs fix a name's type and range, and a value outside it is refused. Add and list them with `coding.sh score`, `POST`/`GET /api/scores` and `/api/score-configs`, or the `score_add` and `score_list` MCP tools. Existing labels move into scores as human pass or fail scores, and `labels` stays readable as a view. [Scores](docs/guides/evals.md#scores)
- **Capture.** `coding.sh doctor --conformance` runs each agent's backfill over the transcripts on this machine and checks the events: duplicate ids, timestamps, tool and subagent pairing, parent links, double-counted usage, sessions without a prompt and failed commands marked ok. It only reads, never opens the store, and exits 1 on a violation. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#check-what-the-adapters-read).
- **Explore.** `coding.sh stats` and the viewer's new Explore page (`#/explore`) show sessions, failure rate, cost, tokens, tool error rate and p95 model-call time per day or week, split by agent, model, repo or flag, from one engine that `GET /api/metrics` also serves. [Explore sessions](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#see-trends), [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apimetrics).
- **Viewer.** A model call in `coding.sh ui` opens in a playground: the call is rebuilt as system prompt, messages and tools, you edit it, run it against Anthropic, OpenAI or any OpenAI-compatible server, see the answer beside the recorded one, and save it as a dataset case. [Explore sessions](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#model-calls), [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apiplayground-get-apiplaygroundcallsid-post-apiplaygroundrun-post-apiplaygroundsave).
- **API.** `GET /api/sessions/{id}/rows` pages through one turn of a long session, with runs of routine events folded and failures always shown; the outline adds turns, failures, compactions, subagents and a minimap; `GET /api/events/{id}` returns one event; and `GET /api/blobs/{sha256}?range=a-b` reads part of a blob. [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsidrows).
- **Viewer.** The viewer page arrives with the first sessions page drawn and its first responses embedded, so the list shows before the script runs (on Slow 4G with a 4x slower CPU, the first row shows about 2.7 s sooner, 95% CI 2.71 to 2.75 s), and a session the pointer rests on, the keyboard moves to, or a press lands on loads in the background, so it opens without a request. [Explore sessions](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare` grades each run in a fresh copy of the task's start tree plus the agent's source changes. A `conftest.py`, `sitecustomize.py`, `.pth` or `.npmrc` the agent wrote, an edited test config or Makefile, and a changed `test` script in `package.json` no longer reach the verifier, and each run records what was left out in `isolation`. `--no-isolation` grades the agent's own tree. [Verifier isolation](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#verifier-isolation)
- **Harness A/B.** The `compare` report adds cost and tokens per solved task, `pass@k`, `pass^k` and the flaky tasks with `--k 2` or more, and how many paired tasks would detect the observed effect at 80% power. [Efficiency, reliability and tasks needed](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#efficiency-reliability-and-tasks-needed)
- **Harness A/B.** `compare --otel` gives each agent an OTLP endpoint, a `TRACEPARENT` and `coding_sh.run_id` in its resource attributes, so the spans it exports are stored with the run and join its session. [OpenTelemetry per run](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#opentelemetry-per-run)
- **Harness A/B.** `compare --sequential` runs each arm's tasks in order and carries the agent's home (or `--carry <path>`) from one task to the next, and the report adds the pass rate by position. [Sequential runs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#sequential-runs)
- **Attribution.** `coding.sh attribution` matches the lines each commit added to the captured session that wrote them, from the session's snapshots or its edit tool calls; a rebased commit still matches, and a session is credited only for files it could have written. `coding.sh blame <path>[:line]` shows it per line, and `coding.sh attribution export` writes Agent Trace (agent-trace.dev, RFC 0.1.0) records. git-ai notes are read as a second source. Read-only on git. [Attribute commits](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/attribute-commits.md).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare --pick N` and `--budget <USD>` run the tasks that best tell harnesses apart in stored runs first and tasks every run passed last. `--budget` fits the tasks to an estimated cost, caps the spend and shows the estimate in the consent prompt; `--dry-run` lists the picks and runs no agent. [Picking tasks and a budget](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#picking-tasks-and-a-budget)
- **Judges.** `judge grade --trace` reads `codex exec --json`, Cursor stream-json and OpenAI chat or Responses request bodies, and exits 2 with `trace_incomplete` when tool calls in the file did not reach the judge; `--allow-partial-trace` grades anyway with a warning, and `--json` carries `traceCoverage`. [Checklists and trace checks](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#checklists-and-trace-checks)
- **Judges.** `"abstain": true` on an `llm-checklist` lets the model answer `cannot_decide` and name what is missing; the item leaves the score, a critical abstention fails the case as undecided, and `judge calibrate` counts undecided cases apart. Without it, prompts and version ids are unchanged. [Checklists and trace checks](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#checklists-and-trace-checks)
- **Judges.** An `llm-panel` grader: one judge per provider scores 1-10 and the median decides, the pass target never reaches the prompt, anchor cases are re-scored fresh to report drift per member, and advisory members are shown but not counted. [Judge panels](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#judge-panels)
- **Judges.** `pairedDiscrimination` from `coding.sh/judge` measures whether a judge reads the trace: each passing case gets children with the same output and a failure in the trace (skipped verification, failing final test, ignored tool error, destructive command, skipped test), and the report gives the flag-rate difference per kind with an interval. [Does the judge read the trace?](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#does-the-judge-read-the-trace)
- **Judges.** `createWasteJudge` from `coding.sh/judge` asks a model which spans of a session were restated, padding, duplicate fetches or detours, drops fabricated quotes and misread streams, and prices the waste in output tokens. [Waste judge](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#waste-judge)
- **Evals.** `coding.sh eval candidates` (and the MCP tool `eval_candidates`) ranks captured sessions as eval tasks. Subagents, runs, and sessions with no git start, no prompt or no edits are left out; the rest are ordered by failure flags, pushback, a guessable verifier and recency. `eval init --sessions` drafts from that ranking and says how many it left out. [Your agent's failed sessions](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#your-agents-failed-sessions).
- **Evals.** `eval init --where '<query>' --validate` drafts tasks from the sessions a query matches and validates each one. A session diff that passes the verifier becomes the reference and the task is marked validated. Tasks from flagged sessions are tagged `captured-failure`, and `eval run` (and the GitHub Action's comment) says which of them now pass. [Your agent's failed sessions](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#your-agents-failed-sessions).
- **Evals.** `eval new --statement ledger` writes the task instruction from every user turn and approved proposal, as cited obligations, instead of the first prompt only. With `--judge` a model merges them and drops the ones a later turn replaced, and code checks every citation and the leakage of the reference patch. [Session to eval](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#session-to-eval).
- **Evals.** `eval trigger <skill> --draft` writes `triggers.toml`. It starts from the prompts of this repo's captured sessions that loaded the skill (should) or a sibling skill (should_not), then has a model fill each side to 8. Mined prompts are redacted, each prompt records its source, and the reply is cached. [Evaluate an Agent Skill](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/evaluate-a-skill.md#does-the-skill-load).
- **Capture.** A tool call that loads a skill names it on its `tool.start` (`payload.skill`, and `payload.skills` when it loaded several): a Skill tool call, a read of a file under an agent's `skills/<name>/` folder, a `cat`, `head` or `sed` of one by a literal path, or an MCP read of a `skill://` resource. Every adapter goes through the same check. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#subagents).
- **Capture.** Copilot CLI hook events keep the OpenTelemetry trace from the `traceparent` Copilot 1.0.107 passes its hooks, and ingest records which session the trace belongs to, so an OTLP span of that trace that names no conversation lands in the hook session. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around).
- **Export.** `eval export --format atif` writes the task's source session as an ATIF-v1.8 trajectory (redacted, with subagents embedded). `--format harbor` now writes `task.toml` schema 1.4 with an explicit verifier mode, and the trajectory under `solution/`. Both pass Harbor 0.25's loaders. [Session to eval](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#session-to-eval).
- **Compare.** `coding.sh compare --parity <A> <B>` reads two pages (URLs, or commands that print one) and lists the fields that differ: status, redirects, title, description, canonical, robots, `og:` and `twitter:` tags, the `hreflang` count and JSON-LD types. It exits 0 on parity, 1 otherwise. [Page parity](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#page-parity).
- **Cost.** `coding.sh cache doctor` grades the prompt cache (hit rate, A to F) and ranks what broke the cached prefix by extra cost: model switches, tool set changes, a volatile line such as a timestamp in the system prompt, other system prompt changes, rewritten history, idle gaps. Each cause has its break count, sessions, tokens re-billed, the fix and a `diff-calls` example. [Prompt cache health](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#model-calls).
- **Cost.** `coding.sh usage --baseline` and the MCP `usage` tool's `baseline` compare each agent's cache hit rate with Requesty's published rate for that agent (April 2026), as a signed point delta, `-` for an agent it does not list. Without the flag the output is unchanged. [Prompt cache health](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#model-calls).
- **Experiments.** `codingShReporter` (from `coding.sh/eve`) is an eve eval reporter: each `eve eval` run becomes a dataset version and an experiment with a grade per assertion, sent to `coding.sh collector` or to `coding.sh serve` with an ingest key. Skipped evals are listed, repeats become trials, the server redacts before storing, and a re-sent run is stored once. [eve guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/integrations/eve.md#send-eve-eval-runs-to-codingsh).
- **OpenTelemetry.** The receiver reads reasoning tokens (`gen_ai.usage.reasoning.output_tokens`, the AI SDK's `reasoningTokens`, Codex's `reasoning_token_count`, Gemini CLI's `thoughts_token_count`) into `payload.reasoningTokens` on the `llm.call`; skills (`gen_ai.skill.name`, Claude Code's `skill_activated`, Codex's `codex.skill_invocation`) as `payload.skill` and a `skill:<name>` tag; `gen_ai.conversation.compacted` and Claude Code's `compaction` event as `context.compact`; and `gen_ai.main_agent.name`, Claude Code's `agent_id`/`parent_agent_id` and Codex's `codex.agent_response` for the agent and its subagents. Spans without them map as before. [OpenTelemetry](https://coding.sh/docs/otel).
- **OpenTelemetry.** An eve or durable-harness agent can send its Agent Runs spans to `coding.sh collector` as well as to Vercel, with one extra span processor; each run becomes a session with its turn, model calls, tool calls and usage, under the agent `durable-harness` for durable-harness. [Tee Agent Runs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/tee-agent-runs.md).
- **Collector.** `coding.sh collector import --clickhouse <url>` reads agent traces from an OpenTelemetry Collector's ClickHouse `otel_traces` table (Map or JSON attributes) into the local store, with the same mapping, redaction and pricing as spans posted to the collector. It keeps its place per table and row filter, so a second run imports nothing new; `--since`, `--from-start`, `--where`, `--table`, `--all`, `--dry-run` and `--json` shape it. [Pull from ClickHouse](https://coding.sh/docs/stores#pull-from-clickhouse).
- **Docs.** A guide to whether a terse mode saves money on your tasks: run them with and without a be-concise rule, skill or plugin, read pass rate and cost with intervals, and split output tokens from the input the instruction adds. It cites our A/B of a terse-output plugin: output −8%, cost +11%, same pass rate and clarity. Its commands are checked against the CLI in tests. [Terse mode guide](https://coding.sh/blog/terse-mode).
- **Benchmarks.** `bench show` and the report add a routing headroom check: the any-contender pass rate against the best single contender, compared with a Rasch model where no contender specializes, so per-case routing is only suggested when the gap beats run noise. [Routing headroom](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/benchmarks.md)
- **Benchmarks.** Contenders that cross models with harnesses (agents, or `axes = { model, harness }` on any contender) get a grid: interaction residuals with intervals, rank reversals, Cross-Model Fit per harness and leave-one-model-out regret. [Model × harness grid](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/benchmarks.md)
- **Capture.** `coding.sh run -- aider` records each reply's tokens and cost while Aider runs: the wrapper reads Aider's analytics log (a temp `AIDER_ANALYTICS_LOG`, or the log you named) and the cache split from its chat history, so live Aider sessions have usage and cost, not only eval runs. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#transcripts-headless-runs-isolation)
- **Cost.** CLIProxyAPI is a gateway: sessions whose base URL is on port 8317 (or `CLIPROXYAPI_URL`) are tagged `gateway:cliproxyapi`, and `gateway enrich --gateway cliproxyapi` reads its usage queue with the management key and joins each record to its llm call by session id, or by time and model. The call gets reasoning effort, service tier, speed, time to first token, compaction, tokens, upstream cost and a hashed account id. The read empties the queue for other readers, so it runs only when named, prints a warning, and keeps every record until it joins. [LLM gateways](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/integrations/ai-gateway.md#cliproxyapi).
- **Sessions.** An agent started by another agent's shell tool (`codex exec` or `gemini -p` from Claude Code's Bash tool) is linked to that session as a child of type `harness`, and `coding.sh show` lists it under the parent. The link comes from the session id Claude Code and Codex export to the commands they run, else from the parent's shell call that ran the child's CLI while the child started in the same directory. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Workflows.** `coding.sh ui` shows workflow runs at `#/workflows`: one bar per agent grouped by phase, the critical path outlined, barrier idle shaded, a concurrency strip against the cap, links to each agent's session, and per-phase and metrics tables. On a phone it opens as a table. The page reads `/api/workflows`, the same documents `workflows show --json` prints. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#capture-and-measure-a-run)
- **Workflows.** Verifier cases take named `[[truth.tests]]` and score the share that pass, reporting tests marked `before = "pass"` that fail after the run as regressions. Judge cases (`kind = "judge"`, a `rubric`) are graded by an LLM judge and flagged uncalibrated until `coding.sh judge calibrate` has calibrated that judge version. `workflows run` now scores every case kind as `workflows score` does. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#write-cases-with-known-answers)
- **Workflows.** `workflows run` checks each seeded bug on its case's `[start]` snapshot before any run: the bug's test fails seeded and passes with the patch reverted (`--no-validate` skips it). A case whose truth file sits inside its `[start]` tree no longer loads. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#write-cases-with-known-answers)
- **Workflows.** A command-driver variant with `fromCheckpoint` or `replayFrom` passes `--from-checkpoint <id>` or `--replay-from <node>` to the program, for LangGraph checkpoint forks and node replays. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#replay-from-a-recorded-run)
- **Store.** Every one-way index migration first keeps a copy of the index next to it (`index.db.v<N>.bak`, a copy-on-write clone where the filesystem has one; a full copy is refused with the space it needs when it would not fit), and `coding.sh upgrade-store --restore` puts it back. The index it replaces is kept as `index.db.v<N>.undone`. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#store-version-errors)

### Changed

- **Docs.** An adapter that nobody has run on a live install is now labelled "Not run live" in the README, the agents guide and the site. Only Cursor CLI and Amp are in that state; both need an account login. [Agents](https://coding.sh/docs/agents)
- **Improve.** `improve check` says Helped only when failures fall: the flagged share, or one flag (Holm-adjusted) with no other rising significantly in its place. A new line the agent follows while flagged sessions stay put is now "Followed, no effect yet", and a change after which sessions make 1.25x the tool calls or take 1.25x as long, with no fall, is "Costlier"; both suggest `refine test git:<previous>` to test the harness without it. `--json` adds `work` and `next`. Regressed and its exit 1 are unchanged. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#did-your-last-change-help).
- **Tool evals.** The model driver's 16 KB tool-output cap is structure-aware by default: JSON stays valid and keeps every key, and logs keep whole lines; output under the cap is unchanged. `outputCap = { mode = "prefix" }` on a driver or variant brings back the old byte cut for an A/B, and the report counts cuts and broken-JSON cuts. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#42-model-driver-agent-free)
- **Sessions.** `stats.toolErrors`, `repeated-tool-failure` and the `failure-streak` derail count only real failures. A search that found nothing (`grep`, `rg` exiting 1 with no output), a probe that answered no (`test -f`, `command -v`) and a `diff` that found differences are counted in the new `stats.benignToolErrors`, and a stopped call in `stats.interruptedToolCalls`. Stored sessions are counted again on the next ingest. [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid).
- **Sessions.** A shell command run from a REPL or code tool's cell (`bash("npm test")`, `subprocess.run(["pytest"])`, `os.system`, an IPython `!` line, a `%%bash` cell) counts as verification for `no-verification`, as the same command in a shell tool does. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Sinks.** The otlp sink sends `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens` as the whole prompt, cache reads and writes included, as the OpenTelemetry GenAI conventions ask; it used to send only the uncached part, so backends undercounted cached calls. Cache writes move to `gen_ai.usage.cache_write.input_tokens` (the old `cache_creation.input_tokens` is still sent through 0.1.5), the uncached part is `coding_sh.usage.uncached_input_tokens`, and reported reasoning tokens are `gen_ai.usage.reasoning.output_tokens`. The OTLP receiver reads the sink's spans back to the same token counts and cost. [Export to ClickHouse](https://coding.sh/docs/stores#export-to-clickhouse-through-an-opentelemetry-collector).
- **Search.** Every search word must match a whole word, and only the last may start a longer one, so `the fix` no longer matches every word that begins with "the". Matches come best first, and a search with more than 20,000 matches lists them newest first instead of ranking them all. On a 756k-event store, `search` for "the", "voxel" and "error" answers in 34, 12 and 38 ms at p95 instead of 719, 171 and 81 ms. The Postgres and ClickHouse plugins and `--where` bare words match the same way. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-search), [Storage](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/architecture.md#efficiency).
- **Store.** The index is about a quarter smaller. The search index no longer keeps its own copy of tool text, each working directory is stored once, and an unused index is gone. On a 756k-event store, after `gc --compact`, it takes 1.38 MB per 1,000 events instead of 1.89. The upgrade builds the new search index and leaves old rows as they are; `gc --compact` empties their old copies and gives the space back. A coding.sh older than 0.1.5 refuses the upgraded index and says to upgrade. [Storage](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/architecture.md#efficiency).
- **CLI.** `--agent`, `--tag`, `--session` and the other repeatable id flags take a comma list: `--agent claude-code,codex` is the same as `--agent claude-code --agent codex`, on `sessions`, `usage`, `search`, `export` and the rest. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md).
- **Collector.** `coding.sh collector` stores concurrent requests together, in one transaction with one blob flush, and no longer keeps every source span as a blob (`--keep-raw` turns that back on; `serve` still keeps them). On the desk, 8 clients posting 61-span requests went from 1,437-3,075 to 5,585-6,865 spans/s, with request p95 from 283-613 ms to 125-155 ms. A long session per client is not faster. [OTLP collector](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md#otlp-collector).
- **Capture.** Outside Windows the hook agents run is a POSIX sh script, `~/.coding.sh/bin/hook.sh`, which writes the same spool line without starting node: under bash 5, about 3 ms more than `/bin/true` per hook call for payloads up to 256 KB, instead of about 30 ms ([Hook](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md#hook)). It hands `--reply`, eval runs, gateway base URLs and anything else it does not cover to `hook.mjs`. Run `coding.sh init` again to switch existing hooks. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#hook-command).
- **Docs.** The instructions file is called AGENTS.md wherever any agent is meant: the home page, docs titles and prose, command examples, `--help` and the MCP tool descriptions. Lists of the files coding.sh reads start with AGENTS.md, and the site, the docs, `--help` and the hints `coding.sh improve` prints name no other instructions file: where a sentence is about one agent's own file, it says "the agent's instructions file". The five-minute guide moved to `/docs/how-to/test-agents-md-change`; the old URL redirects to it. No command changed which file it reads or writes. [Test an AGENTS.md change in five minutes](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/test-agents-md-change).
- **Docs.** The docs pages are shorter and easier to skim: each starts with what it is for and one real example, flags are in tables, and depth sits in "How it is computed" blocks or behind a link. A site test keeps bullets, paragraphs and guide pages within word budgets. [Experiments](https://coding.sh/docs/experiments).
- **Query.** A date without a zone in `--since`, `--until`, `since:` and `until:` is local time: `2026-11-09` is local midnight (it was UTC midnight) and equals `2026-11-09T00:00:00`. `today` and `yesterday` work, and `until` is exclusive. `sessions`, `usage` and `stats` print the resolved window on stderr and carry `window` in `--json`.
