The MCP tools read times the same way, and `GET /api/metrics` reads a date without a zone as midnight in the request's `tz`, the zone its buckets use, so the first and last day of a range are whole days. [Query reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md#filter-with---where), [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apimetrics)
- **Query.** `sessions --where` with `event.duration` or `error` alone reads an index instead of every event. On a 1.4M-event store on the test desktop, `--where 'event.duration>60s'` went from 840 ms to under 1 ms and `--where 'error:*timeout*'` from 2.7 s to 3 ms. The first run upgrades the index once (schema v11), with progress on stderr and the old index kept beside it; that store (2.7 GB) took 59 s and grew by 1%. [Query reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md)
- **CLI.** Default `--json` reads are digests an agent can afford. `sessions --json` rows, `tree --json` nodes and `status --json` `latest` drop `stats.phases` and each derail's `eventId` and `index`. `show --json` on a session over 200 events prints `events: []` with `eventCount` and the phases unless `--limit N` or `--all` is given, each event drops `sessionId` (and `agent`/`cwd` when equal to the session's), and `subagents` lists the first 50 with `subagentCount` and `subagentsTruncated`. `tree --json` and MCP `session_tree` return 50 nodes a page with `nextCursor` and `truncated` (`--limit`/`--cursor`; MCP `limit` up to 200). New `--fields a,b.c` on `sessions` and `show` picks paths from the full document. [Explore guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#long-sessions)
- **Harness.** `harness audit` (CLI and MCP) reads session rows instead of every event: ingest keeps the memory and instruction files each session's events name in `stats.memoryUse`. On a store of 3,600 sessions and 1.4M events it takes 0.4 s and 132 MB, where it took 10 to 27 s and up to 969 MB. After an upgrade the audit reads events as before until an ingest has refolded the stored sessions. [Memory and instruction files](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#memory-and-instruction-files)
- **Viewer.** Moving through sessions and steps with the keyboard is instant: rows snap, the query suggestions and pills do not animate or push rows under the cursor, and a held `j` or `k` stays smooth in long sessions. Counts read "1 tool call", large costs and token counts keep their separators, long durations roll up to days, relative times keep refreshing, and long titles, model ids and file names stay readable. In the benchmark table a long cost source no longer pushes the cost out of its column. [Viewer](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md)

### Fixed

- **Capture.** A secret written as `NAME=value` right after a newline no longer survives in a stored raw payload blob. Every string is redacted before the payload is serialized. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md)
- **Capture.** Once ingest has stored a hook line, it rewrites that line's `spool/<agent>/raw/` payload file with secrets redacted. The docs now say a payload written inside its spool line stays as written until `gc`. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md)
- **Capture.** A failed Codex shell command is now stored as failed with its exit code, from the hook and the rollout alike, so `tool.errors` counts it and `sessions --where "tool.errors>0"` finds the session. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md)
- **Sessions.** A Claude Code session whose model calls arrive both over OTLP logs and from its transcript now counts each call once, so its tokens and call count match the transcript. [Agents guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/agents.md)
- **Sinks.** A sink gets each event once when an agent hands the same one over in two spool lines (a Claude Code helper's events come from both the parent's Agent call and the helper's own stop), so a consumer that sums chat spans no longer overcounts tokens. [Config](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md)
- **Evals.** `compare` on one task reads the runs of that task: a Newcombe interval for the difference of the two pass rates and Fisher's exact test, labelled as holding for that task only. It no longer prints a zero-width bootstrap, McNemar p = 1.000 or a `tasks needed` line from one task, and `--json` adds `report.oneTask`. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Docs.** The Langfuse comparison now says the viewer has a playground that reruns one model call; only prompt management is missing. [Compare](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/compare/langfuse.md)
- **Attribution.** A session is credited a file from the tree diff only when its tools named the file or it ran a shell or subagent call; a named file takes the lines its edit steps added, not the diff. Lines a person typed in the same window match no session of a run that only read files. A session that ran a shell tool can still be credited a file a person wrote by hand, at lower confidence. [Attribute commits](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/attribute-commits.md#read-the-result)
- **Cost.** A Claude Code subagent whose transcript sits under more than one session is stored once, under the session that wrote it. A workflow run resumed in another session (`resumeFromRunId`), a symlinked run dir or a fork used to store each copy as its own subagent session, so its calls and cost counted again. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#subagents).
- **Cost.** Copies already in the index are removed on the next re-parse. A session an older coding.sh stored from a copy of another session's transcript (a Claude Code subagent copied into a resumed workflow run or a fork) is deleted with its events once the session that wrote it is stored, so its calls and cost stop counting twice. Re-parse used to keep it as a session whose transcript is gone; a session whose transcript is really gone is still kept. `ingest --rebuild` prints how many it removed. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#old-sessions-show-doubled-counts).
- **Cost.** Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.2 are priced at their own rates ($8 input, $40 output, $0.20 cache hit, fast mode $16/$80) instead of Fable 5.1's $10/$50, which put standard calls 25% high and fast calls 37.5% low. Stored sessions are repriced on the next ingest. [Prices](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#prices).
- **Privacy.** A purge given a wait (`CODING_SH_PURGE_WAIT_MS`, or `waitMs` in code) now keeps to it while another process reads the index. The checkpoint before the VACUUM used to wait the full 10 s whatever wait was given. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md).
- **Store.** An index upgrade on a store of 100,000 events or more no longer runs silently. It says so on stderr within a second, prints its progress at most once a second, and ends with how long it took and, when old data is left, `gc --compact`. A command that opens the index while another process upgrades it waits and prints that process's progress. Upgrading a 740k-event store from schema v2 took 20 s instead of 119 s. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#the-first-command-after-an-upgrade-is-slow).
- **Docs.** Status claims match the code and the registries again. Tool evals and workflows are tagged built, and `coding.sh`, `@codingdotsh/sdk` and the Postgres, ClickHouse and S3 store plugins are no longer called unpublished; the Python SDK is still not on PyPI. The dogfood plan gives each step's state with code pointers. A site test fails when a page calls something planned whose module exists, or when the agent list disagrees with the adapters in the code. [What is built](https://coding.sh/docs#status).
- **Sinks.** Metadata sinks no longer receive a session's `stats.llmErrors.error` text, which could quote the prompt. [Config](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#sinks)
- **Privacy.** The `env` redactor no longer replaces `PWD`, `OLDPWD` or variables named `*_PATH`, `*_DIR`, `*_FILE` or `*_SOCK`, or any value that is an existing path, so the working directory stopped turning into `[redacted:env]` in every path. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md).
- **Capture.** A Claude Code response with parallel tool calls is one `llm.call` again, with its usage counted once. Claude Code writes a tool's result between the lines of the response that called it, and each part used to become its own call; the call is now timed from the request's `api-request` line. A subagent that another subagent spawned hangs off that subagent's session instead of the main one. Stored sessions are parsed again on the next ingest. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around).
- **Capture.** A Codex subagent thread has a prompt and a title: the task its parent sent it (an `agent_message` addressed to the thread) is its `user.message`, with `from`, `taskName` and `encrypted`. Codex encrypts the task text, so the prompt says which agent asked for which task. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#subagents).
- **Capture.** OpenCode history imports when OpenCode has set its database aside as `opencode.db.schema-mismatch-<ms>` and not yet written a new one, and `doctor` says when a database has no session tables coding.sh knows or cannot be opened, where backfill used to import nothing without a word. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around).
- **Capture.** An Antigravity command that exited nonzero is a failed tool call with its `exitCode`, read from the result's `The command exited with code N.` line, and the result's `Created At`/`Completed At` time it. Every Antigravity tool call used to count as ok. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around).
- **Sessions.** `show`, `tree`, `usage`, `improve`, `harness audit` and the other reads answer within the read budget while a re-parse runs, from what is indexed. `show`, `tree`, `usage`, `replay` and `harness audit` used to ingest every pending capture first, with no budget. They leave the spool to the re-parse, say how many files wait (on stderr, or `pending` with `reparsing: true` in JSON), and a session not found says it may be among them. [Configuration](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md).
- **OpenTelemetry.** A span that reports a cache read or cache write of 0 tokens no longer adds `cacheReadTokens: 0` or `cacheWriteTokens: 0` to the call's usage; non-zero counts and cost are unchanged. [OpenTelemetry](https://coding.sh/docs/otel).
- **OpenTelemetry.** eve's `agent.step` and approval spans become `notification` events (`eve.step`, …), as the eve mapping documents, instead of generic `span` events. [eve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/integrations/eve.md).
- **Capture.** The sh hook no longer hangs when an agent starts it with stdin closed. Under macOS `/bin/sh` and bash 3.2 it never exited and left a child process behind. It now treats a closed stdin as an empty payload and exits 0 at once, as `hook.mjs` does. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#hook-command)
- **Capture.** `hook.sh` no longer fails the agent's hook call when it hands an event to `hook.mjs` and node cannot run (not on the agent's `PATH`, a version manager's shim that fails, a missing `hook.mjs`): it exits 0 and still prints the `--echo-continue` reply. Before, an eval run, a gateway base URL or `--reply` made the hook exit 127 on such a machine. That event is not captured. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#hook-command)
- **Capture.** Claude Code hooks registered in two settings files with different commands (user settings on `hook.sh`, a project's `.claude/settings.local.json` still on `node …/hook.mjs`) no longer store each prompt, turn end and session end twice. The spool lines of one hook firing (the same payload, at most 2 s apart) get the same event ids; the same prompt sent again later is still a second event. `coding.sh init` also looks for project hooks at the root of the repository it runs in, reports them and skips the user install unless `--force`. Stored Claude Code sessions are parsed again. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#hook-command)
- **Capture.** A session whose hooks ran with a stale `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` or `CODEX_THREAD_ID` is no longer hidden as a subagent of a session that is not in the store or had already ended. The marker links a child only when the named session is in the store and was open when the child started; stored sessions with such a parent become top-level after the refold. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md)
- **Capture.** When the ingest the hook starts fails, `coding.sh status`, the status line and `coding.sh doctor` print `ingest stopped 4h ago` with the command that fixes it, and `status --json` and `doctor --json` carry `ingestStopped`. `status` prints the home, the spool and that line even when the store can't be opened. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md)
- **Store.** The store records the coding.sh version that last wrote it. A build that is not a newer release than that one (a source checkout, a stale dist) no longer migrates the index by itself, with or without a `bin/`: it stops with `store_version`, and `coding.sh upgrade-store` migrates on request. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#store-version-errors)
- **Store.** The error for a store a newer coding.sh already migrated says captures are safe in the spool, how many files wait there, which version wrote the store, and to run `coding.sh update`; it offers `upgrade-store --restore` when a copy from before the migration is kept. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#store-version-errors)
- **Store.** `coding.sh upgrade-store` always leaves `bin/cli-path` naming the running coding.sh (also when there is no `bin/`, or it names a CLI that is gone), and upgrade progress counts events instead of the highest rowid. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#the-first-command-after-an-upgrade-is-slow)
- **Doctor.** An agent whose hook is not installed or is blocked is a `!` warning row with every blocker and the file and line to change (Codex: `config.toml:<line>`); recent events alone no longer earn a check mark. The last line is `N agents not capturing` instead of `ok`, and `doctor --json` lists them under `warnings`. `ok` and the exit code still report spool and store health only. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md)
- **Cost.** `usage` and `cache doctor` total every session in the window; they stopped at the newest 2,000 without saying so. Past 100,000 sessions (`CODING_SH_SCAN_CAP`) they print `Stopped after N sessions: narrow --since or --where.` and set `truncated` in `--json`. [Configuration](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#paths-and-stores)
- **Cost.** `coding.sh cache doctor` counts every session in the window in its breaks, re-billed tokens, extra USD and grade, so they match `usage` whatever `--limit` is. `--limit` (default 200) now only bounds how many sessions are read to classify causes: a session past it keeps its stored cause or counts as `unclassified`, and `--json` adds `breaksNotRead`. Before, the totals and the grade covered only the sessions that were read. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#model-calls)
- **Cost.** A `gateway enrich` record with no upstream cost no longer removes `(estimated)` from a session's cost. Only a record with a billed cost marks the call as billed. [LLM gateways](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/integrations/ai-gateway.md)
- **Gateways.** `gateway enrich --gateway cliproxyapi` no longer joins another client's usage record to your call. A record whose session id is not in the store is kept for a later run, a record joins by time and model only when it has no session id, and records that name a session join first, so each call gets its own tokens, cost and account. [LLM gateways](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/integrations/ai-gateway.md#cliproxyapi)
- **Gateways.** `gateway enrich --gateway cliproxyapi` no longer joins a failed or retried CLIProxyAPI request to a call that answered, which gave every later call its neighbour's usage record. A failed record joins only a call that failed, a record that answered joins only a call that did not fail, and a call whose token counts equal the record's wins over a nearer one. A failed record with no failed call is kept for a day, then dropped. [LLM gateways](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/integrations/ai-gateway.md#cliproxyapi)
- **Gateways.** After `init --proxy` chains through a gateway (CLIProxyAPI, OpenRouter, LiteLLM, …), new sessions keep their `gateway:<name>` tag: the proxy names the gateway on each call it logs, also in capture mode `metadata`. `gateway status` and `doctor` report the route, marked "through the coding.sh proxy". [Capture with the proxy](https://coding.sh/docs/ai-gateway)
- **Gateways.** A background re-parse, refold or end-of-turn snapshot no longer drops the `gateway:<name>` tag and the billed cost that `gateway enrich` stored at the same moment. A session that got events while its row was being built is folded again before the row is written, and `gateway enrich` stores its events and the session rows in one transaction. [LLM gateways](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/integrations/ai-gateway.md#cliproxyapi)
- **Sinks.** `coding.sh serve` and `coding.sh collector` now send the `otlp` sink a session's `invoke_agent` span while the session is still open: once it has been quiet for 10 seconds, and every 60 seconds while it stays busy. An OTLP session keyed by a conversation id (Claude Code, Codex, `gen_ai.conversation.id`, Agent Runs) never ends, so it used to reach the sink as `chat` and `execute_tool` spans with no parent and no session row. Each send repeats the span id with newer totals, and the ClickHouse queries now take the latest row per span id. [Export to ClickHouse](https://coding.sh/docs/stores#export-to-clickhouse-through-an-opentelemetry-collector)
- **Sinks.** `coding.sh serve` and `coding.sh collector` send an event to `[[sinks]]` once. A request that was posted again, such as a client retry answered `"inserted":0`, used to send all its events again and doubled rows and cost downstream; now only the events a batch newly stored go on, and a batch that overlaps stored events sends the new ones. A commit that fails after its events were written sends them when the retry commits. [Forward and sinks](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/PRODUCTION.md#19-forward-and-sinks)
- **Sinks.** Local ingest now sends the events it imports from agent transcripts to `[[sinks]]`, not only the events from the hook spool, so a sink has every model call the store has. When an import adds events to a session without ending it, the otlp sink gets the session's `invoke_agent` span again with the new totals. A sink that is down does not stop the import and catches up after its backoff, and a second ingest sends nothing new. [Config](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#sinks)
- **Collector.** `coding.sh collector import` keeps its place per table and row filter (`--all`, each `--where`), so a run without the filter reads the rows a filtered run skipped. A `--since` given on the command line reads from that time again, and the new `--from-start` does the same with the default 7 days, for a root span that reached the table more than an hour late. Nothing is stored twice. [Pull from ClickHouse](https://coding.sh/docs/stores#pull-from-clickhouse)
- **Collector.** `coding.sh collector import` stores each page with its sessions before it saves its place, so a run that loses ClickHouse part-way leaves no events without a session. A dropped connection or a 502, 503 or 504 is tried three more times; a run that still fails says how many spans it read and prints the command that reads the rest. Before, the next run exited 0 with fewer sessions than traces. [Pull from ClickHouse](https://coding.sh/docs/stores#pull-from-clickhouse)
- **Privacy.** A source span or log record that `coding.sh serve` or `coding.sh collector --keep-raw` keeps as a blob, and an oversize structured value moved to a blob, is now redacted one string at a time (a string that holds JSON as JSON) before it is serialized. A `NAME=value` line after a newline and a JSON field such as `api_key` or `password` used to stay in clear in the stored blob and were served by `/api/blobs/<sha>`, while the event showed them as redacted. Blobs stored before 0.1.6 are not rewritten: remove such a value with `coding.sh purge`. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md)
- **Refine.** `refine signals show <id>` takes the Signal id or a prefix of it again; it rejected every id with `Unexpected argument`. An unknown id says `No Signal <id> in <file>` and exits 2. [Diagnosing sessions into Signals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#diagnosing-sessions-into-signals)
- **Refine.** `refine log`, the `refine test` verdict line and the `refine apply` refusal lead with the canary's reason for a round the canary rejected or left inconclusive, not the reason the candidate was first kept. [Canary re-test](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#canary-re-test)
- **Refine.** `refine distill --keep-corpus DIR` no longer deletes a directory that holds a file named SCHEMA.md next to other work. Only a directory that holds nothing but an earlier corpus is replaced, entry by entry; any other non-empty directory is refused and left as it is. With `--dry-run` nothing is written to DIR. [Distilling a skill](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#distilling-a-skill)
- **Refine.** `refine diagnose --session` takes an id prefix or `latest`, as `show` does. An id that matches no session is a not-found error (exit 1) instead of an empty plan. [Diagnosing sessions into Signals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#diagnosing-sessions-into-signals)
- **Refine.** `refine mine` reports failed eval runs with the default `--repo`: a run counts for the repo when its task starts from it or belongs to a suite under its `evals/`. The summary line and `--json` (`evalRuns`) say how many eval runs were read. [Mining failure patterns](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#mining-failure-patterns)
- **Improve.** A `Helped` line from `improve check` now names every flag whose share rose after the change, significant or not, as in `repeated-tool-failure rose 4 to 8 of 12, not significant`. The verdict is unchanged: one flag falling is Helped unless another flag rose significantly. [Improve guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#did-your-last-change-help)
- **Judges.** A `ran-command` check with `"ok": true` no longer passes a failed command on OpenAI chat and Responses traces. `judge grade --trace` reads the exit code a tool result states on a line of its own (`Process exited with code 1`, `exit code 0`), and a result with no status or exit code makes the item N/A, not a pass. [Checklists and trace checks](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#checklists-and-trace-checks)
- **Judges.** `judge grade --trace` exits 2 with `trace_incomplete` for a file cut mid-line, a record with no `type`, `role` or `kind`, and a file with no JSON records; it used to grade them as whole traces. `--json` counts them in `traceCoverage.unparsedLines` and `unreadRecords`. An agent's plain-text stdout as the default trace is still no trace. [Checklists and trace checks](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#checklists-and-trace-checks)
- **Evals.** `eval new --statement ledger` keeps a reply that approves and goes on ("yes, but call it goodbye instead") as its own obligation, after the proposal it changes. A short reply that opened with an approval word used to count as a bare approval, so the instruction asked for the assistant's proposal and dropped the user's change. [Session to eval](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#session-to-eval)
- **Evals.** `eval init --validate` no longer marks a session that rewrote the test as the validated reference. A session diff that edits, deletes or skips the tests the verifier runs is kept as a near miss, with or without `--validate`, and a note names the test file. A diff that only adds test cases to a test file still counts as a reference. [Your agent's failed sessions](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#your-agents-failed-sessions)
- **Evals.** `eval trigger <skill> --draft` reads only the sessions of the current git repo, so a `triggers.toml` you commit no longer holds prompts typed in other projects. `--repo DIR` reads the sessions under another directory and `--repo all` reads every repo's. [Does the skill load?](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/evaluate-a-skill.md#does-the-skill-load)
- **Evals.** `coding.sh score add event:<id> --session <ID>` (and `POST /api/scores` with a `sessionId`) no longer stores a score on an event that is not in the store, or under another session. An unknown event is `event not found` (exit 1, HTTP 404), and a session that is not the event's own is refused with both ids named (exit 2, HTTP 400). [Scores](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#scores)
- **Evals.** A stored score keeps its type. A value that reads as another type (`1,5` over a number, `0.5` over a pass or fail label) is refused (exit 2, HTTP 400) and names `--type` and `score config`, instead of quietly turning the score into a category. `--type`, `dataType` or a config for the name still change the type, and `score add` now prints the type: `Scored n1 = -5 (numeric)`. [Scores](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#scores)
- **Experiments.** `compare --signals` no longer gives a verdict from too few tasks. A signal needs task+arm pairs from 8 tasks or more, however many pairs there are; below that the verdict is `too-few-pairs` and the table says how many tasks it found. With one task the 95% CI column shows `–`, where it used to print a zero-width interval such as `[0.65, 0.65]`. A coin-flip signal on one task with `--k 10` used to get `adds-run-signal` in about 45% of tables. `--json` adds `withinTaskArm.tasks` to each row. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#do-the-flags-predict-failure)
- **Tool evals.** `toolevals validate --against <variant> --driver <d>` no longer counts a trial where the agent never worked on the task as a failing trial. A logged-out agent, a failed setup step, an agent that could not start (exit 127) or one that exited non-zero before its first turn used to make every bad task look like it holds; each such task is now reported as `couldn't run on <variant>` with the reason (`infra: auth`, `agent never ran, exit 127`) and does not hold. `toolevals run` records the same never-ran trials with an error. [Task kinds](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#31-task-kinds-bad-guard-control)
- **Benchmarks.** Routing headroom now prints the best single contender's own pass rate, the one on the leaderboard, and measures the gap and its expected range from it. With several trials it used to average the best contender of each trial, so the rate beside the name was too high and the gap too small. Runs stored before the fix keep their old numbers. [Routing headroom](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/benchmarks.md)
- **Benchmarks.** The model × harness grid no longer states noise as fact. A rank reversal is listed only when the 95% interval of both pass-rate gaps excludes 0, each leave-one-model-out regret and the mean carry a 95% interval (`regretCi` and `meanRegretCi` in `--json`), no cell is starred under 20 paired cases, a bound that rounds to 0 no longer stars some cells of a 2 × 2 and not others, and the legend says the stars are per cell and not adjusted for the number of cells. [Model × harness grid](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/benchmarks.md)
- **Attribution.** Without snapshots (a store filled by `ingest --backfill`, or a session whose end snapshot is gone), a session is credited only for the lines its own Write, Edit and patch calls added, less the ones it later removed. Lines a person added to the file between two of the agent's edits used to be credited to the session once the agent read the file again. Sources cached by an older build are built again. [Attribute commits](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/attribute-commits.md#read-the-result)
- **Attribution.** A person's copy of a long line in another file of the same commit no longer takes the session's credit. Every file of a commit gets its same-file matches before a long line may match across files, so `blame` credits the agent's own line and shows `-` for the copy. [Attribute commits](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/attribute-commits.md#read-the-result)
- **Attribution.** A commit made on a branch that was no longer checked out when the session's end snapshot was taken got 0 lines, because an empty or unrelated snapshot diff hid the session's own edit tool calls. `coding.sh attribution` and `coding.sh blame` now take every file the session's tools named that the snapshot diff does not hold from those calls, at confidence 0.8. [Attribute commits](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/attribute-commits.md#read-the-result)
- **Compare.** `compare --parity` reads a tag whose attribute value holds a raw `>` in full, so two pages that differ after it no longer read as the same. [Page parity](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#page-parity)
- **Viewer.** "Open in playground" works for model calls in subagent and workflow-agent sessions: the request is rebuilt from the subagent's own conversation, without the calls of a subagent it started. Every such call used to answer 404 `no_request`. [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apiplayground-get-apiplaygroundcallsid-post-apiplaygroundrun-post-apiplaygroundsave)
- **Docs.** The troubleshooting page lists each error reason under the exit code the CLI gives it, and the session rows example in the API reference is the real answer for the page's example session. [Errors by reason](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#errors-by-reason), [API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md)

## [0.1.5] - 2026-11-09

### Added

- **Sessions.** A new flag, `unapproved-action`, marks a session where the agent ran a destructive or paid command after a user turn that did not approve it: `rm -rf` outside temp and build dirs, `git reset --hard` or `git push --force`, dropping a database, a tool's own `--yes` on a repair, reinit, migrate, delete or purge subcommand, deleting a data dir, or a known paid cloud or batch job. Asked only to "fix it", agents run the `--yes` their tool's error names. `stats.unapprovedActions` counts them, and `--where unapproved>0` lists the sessions. [Flags](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).

### Changed

- **Refine.** `refine test|run --suite` keeps a candidate whose pass rate is significantly better even when a listed objective such as cost got significantly worse, as long as the rise is within `--max-cost-rise` (default 50%, or `refine.maxCostRise` in config). It used to reject +27 to +67 pt gains because each run cost a few cents more. The verdict prints the trade, such as `+33.3 pts per task … for cost / run +20% … (limit +50%)`, and a rise past the limit still rejects. The gain must still pass the exact permutation test. [Climbing on an eval suite](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#climbing-on-an-eval-suite).
- **CLI.** An error whose next step deletes or overwrites data, cannot be undone, or spends money now says the user must approve it first, and its `--json` line has `askUser: true`. That covers the `--yes` a confirmation asks for (`init`, `uninstall`, `eval run`, `compare`, `refine`, `workflows run`), the tool-eval cost gate, and the `--force`, `purge` and `rollback` steps that errors suggest. The text no longer says "Did you mean" before a `--yes` command. Asked only to "fix it", agents ran the `--yes` an error named without asking. [Output and exit codes](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#output-and-exit-codes).
- **Store.** A blob that compresses to under 4 KiB, such as a hook line's raw payload, is a row of one SQLite file, `blobs/pack.db`, instead of a file of its own; larger blobs stay files. On the 100k-event ingest benchmark the store takes 1.72 MB per 1,000 events instead of 5.51, and ingest runs at about 6,200 events/s instead of 1,700. Blob files written before still read, and `gc --compact` moves the small ones into the pack and removes the blob folders that leaves empty. A coding.sh older than 0.1.5 does not see packed blobs: its CLI reads sessions as usual, and its viewer shows their stored content as "not in the local store". Run `purge` with 0.1.5 or newer: an older purge leaves secrets in packed blobs, and they show again once you upgrade. Running the same purge again with 0.1.5 removes them, since it reads every packed blob. [Storage](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/architecture.md#efficiency), [Store ingest](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md#store-ingest).

### Fixed

- **Store.** A coding.sh that doesn't own the store no longer migrates it and strands the install your hooks run. The owner is the CLI `~/.coding.sh/bin/cli-path` names (`init` and `update` write it); any other install stops before a migration, names both versions, and leaves the store as it was until you run the new `coding.sh upgrade-store`, which also points the hooks at it. Upgrades in place and `coding.sh update` migrate as before. coding.sh run from a source checkout also no longer opens `~/.coding.sh` unless `CODING_SH_HOME`, `--store` or `CODING_SH_DEV_REAL_STORE=1` says to. On 2026-11-08 a build from source migrated a real store to index schema v9, and the installed 0.1.2 could not open it for 10 hours. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#store-version-errors).
- **Sessions.** A session coding.sh fails to fold into stats no longer leaves the sessions after it with events but no row, invisible to `sessions`, the viewer and stats. The import used to stop at the first such session and, for Codex history read on every command, stop at it again each time; one 0.1.4 store had 906 Codex sessions like that. The session now gets a row tagged `fold:error`, with the error in `stats.foldError`, and the import goes on. `coding.sh ingest` folds sessions that have events but no row, and fold:error ones, again, and `coding.sh doctor` counts both. [Flags](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md), [Session stats](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md).
- **Sessions.** A shell command named like a built-in object key (`constructor`, `toString`) no longer stops a session from being folded with `PAID[tool]?.test is not a function`. [Flags](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Sessions.** `sessions`, `show` and the viewer no longer crash with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'briefs')` after another build of coding.sh worked out the same store. Each session's saved progress and the store's refold mark now carry the fold version and a hash of the progress's shape, so a store last folded by another build is folded again once. Saved progress with a missing part, or that fails to fold, is dropped and the session is worked out again from its events. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#old-sessions-show-doubled-counts).
- **Evals.** An agent that is not logged in, or a setup step that fails, no longer counts as a task failure. A refused login (`Not logged in`, `Invalid API key · Please run /login`, an HTTP 401) is never retried, and a setup step that exited non-zero or a work folder that could not be made is retried once; either way the trial is infra-unknown and left out of pass rates in `compare`, `exp compare`, `refine --suite`, `eval run` and `toolevals`, which count it by kind. A run stops once 5 trials in a row fail on infrastructure (`--stop-after-infra`) and says it is no quality verdict, a comparison left with no pair is `void:`, and a refine round lost this way writes no verdict and stops the climb. An expired login had read as "quality held". [Long runs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#long-runs-resume-retries-status).
- **Evals.** A few cases, tasks or prompts that all move the same way no longer pass a gate on their own. The bootstrap interval is then a single point, and the support share is 1, however few they are. `exp compare` (with `--margin` and `--pairwise`), `eval trigger --fail-on regression`, `toolevals compare` metric regressions, `compare --promote` and refine's efficiency keeps now also need an exact sign-flip test to agree, over clusters when there is a cluster key. A verdict changes only when fewer than about 6 units carry the difference: 3 improved cases read `inconclusive` instead of `better` (exact p 0.25), and one improved task no longer promotes. With one trial per case the test's p is the McNemar p, so larger comparisons keep their verdicts. JSON output gains `signFlip`, `p` or `exact` fields. [Audit](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/stats-audit.md), [Paired comparison](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Privacy.** `purge` no longer reports success while another process (the viewer, `serve`, a `sqlite3` shell, a backup) holds a read on the index or the blob pack. SQLite keeps the old bytes in that file or its WAL until the reader closes, and purge used to ignore the busy checkpoint that says so. It now waits a few seconds, then names the file that still holds them and exits 1; the same purge run again once the reader closes removes them. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md).
- **Sessions.** `sessions --where id:<id>` and `export --where id:<id>` find a subagent's session, such as a Claude Code workflow agent's, as `show <id>` does. Both commands leave out subagents unless you pass `--subagents`, and that default hid every session the query named. A query with `id:`, `parent:`, `is:root` or `is:child` now decides that itself, so `--where is:child` lists subagents too. [Query and export](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md#filter-with---where).
- **Workflows.** A Claude Code workflow run that is still going is named by its script's `meta.name`, read from the copy of the script Claude Code keeps in the session's `workflows/scripts/` folder, instead of by its `wf_…` id. Claude Code writes the record, which held the only name coding.sh read, when the run ends. `workflows import` reads runs it stored before this fix again once. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/workflows.md#21-claude-code-workflows).
- **Capture.** The hook no longer reads `.git` on session-start and prompt events, which made each of those hook calls about 3.5 ms slower than in 0.1.2 in a repo with a 1 MB `packed-refs`. Ingest takes a hook session's start commit from the reflog again, and still uses the HEAD a 0.1.3 or 0.1.4 hook recorded. `CODING_SH_NO_GIT_HEAD` has nothing left to turn off. [Architecture](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/architecture.md#spool-the-hot-path).
- **Cost.** A Claude Code fork subagent (`subagent_type: "fork"`) no longer counts the parent's model calls again. Its transcript opens with a copy of the parent's context, and each copied call was stored twice: 2,593 calls and $161 too many across 52 forks on one machine. The fork now holds only its own calls, its turn opens with the directive, and `stats.inheritedTokens` says how much context it started with. Stored sessions are parsed again. [Subagents](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare` counts cache reads and writes in `tokens / run`. With prompt caching the row tracked output tokens only, so a harness that cost 40% more could read as 11% more tokens. [Harness A/B](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab).
- **Privacy.** `coding.sh purge` no longer reports `Integrity check failed` and exits 1 after every purge. The check asked SQLite's full-text search to find an entry for every event, but events with no text, such as a session start or a model call, are never indexed. It now checks that the index is sound and holds exactly the events with text. Search was not affected. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare` leaves out `time to first derail` when no run in either arm derailed, where it only repeated the session length. [Derails](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#derails).
- **Refine.** `refine` and `exp ab` carry a skill's whole folder into each trial: its `scripts/`, `references/` and other files as well as its SKILL.md, so a skill that runs a helper script is tested with it. Files over 1 MB or with binary content stay out, and the protected-line and duplicate checks still read only the instruction files. [Refine loop](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#refine-loop).

## [0.1.4] - 2026-11-08

The 0.1.3 changes, published to npm.

### Fixed

- **Release.** 0.1.3 never reached npm: the publish job passed npm a tarball path that npm read as a GitHub repository. 0.1.4 carries all of 0.1.3's changes, and the release checks now dry-run the npm publish before it is approved. [Releasing](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/RELEASE.md).

## [0.1.3] - 2026-11-08

Sessions read back from Claude Code and Codex transcripts keep more of what the agent recorded.

### Added

- **Docs.** Two guides for one agent each. coding.sh with T3 Code covers what is read from T3 Code's local store for each agent it runs, and how to turn a thread into an eval run by Codex or Claude Code. coding.sh with Pi covers the Pi extension, Pi's own cost per model call, and evals with `--agent pi`. [T3 Code](https://coding.sh/docs/agents#t3-code), [Pi](https://coding.sh/docs/agents#pi).
- **Workflows.** `workflows stats --json` gives each per-agent row the agent id, the attempt (a label run again after a resume is attempt 2) and how it ended (`endReason`: ok, failed, cancelled, superseded, null, schema-retry, …), so two agents with the same label can be told apart. The text table adds `#2` to a re-run label. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/workflows.md#4-metrics).
- **Improve.** `improve` and the MCP `improve` tool list what lost prompt cache cost, from the cache breaks ingest already stored: the extra dollars, their share of the spend, which sets the severity so the item ranks against the others by cost, and a fix per cause, costliest first. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#see-what-to-fix-first).
- **Sessions.** A new `coding.sh tree <session|run>` command shows a whole multi-agent run as one tree: the top-level agent, every subagent it spawned, and their subagents, with per-node model, cost, tokens, duration and tool calls, the run's rolled-up totals, the worst flag across the loop, and which subagent to fix first. A fan-out of separate agent processes groups into one run when they share `CODING_SH_RUN_ID`. The viewer serves it at `/api/sessions/{id}/tree` and the MCP `session_tree` tool returns it for your agent. It is built from the parent links capture already records, so no session re-folds. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md).
- **Capture.** A tool output the agent cut before the model saw it is marked on its `tool.end` as `payload.cut`: Codex's head-and-tail cut of a long command or code-mode result (with the original token count, and `jsonBroken` when cut JSON no longer parses), and Claude Code's `<persisted-output>` preview of a result saved to a file (with its size and path). Backfilled sessions only for now; a hook-captured call keeps the hook's copy. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around).
- **Capture.** API errors Claude Code retried keep what the transcript says about them: the HTTP status, the error type and class (`rate_limit`, `overloaded`, …), the rate limit that was hit (`seven_day`, `five_hour`), when it resets, how long Claude Code waited and which attempt it was. Before, every one was a bare "api error", so a three-day weekly-limit stall looked like one transient error. [Read API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid).
- **Cost.** A Claude Code backfill keeps Claude Code's own cost tally (the transcript's `cost-state` line: total cost, API time and cost per model) on the session's `session.end` as `payload.reportedUsage`, so you can check the computed cost against it. It is never added to the session's cost. [Where cost comes from](https://coding.sh/docs/cost#where-cost-comes-from).
- **Capture.** The hook records the repo root, HEAD commit and branch on session-start and prompt events, read from `.git` files without running git (worktrees, submodules, packed refs, detached and unborn heads). A hook session's start commit, which `eval new` starts from, is now the HEAD when the session started. Before, ingest looked it up in the reflog and, when the reflog had nothing, took the HEAD at ingest time. `CODING_SH_NO_GIT_HEAD=1` turns it off. [Configuration](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/config.md#capture).
- **Capture.** Before `coding.sh init`, a read now works with no setup: `usage`, `sessions`, `show` and the viewer import the Claude Code transcripts already on disk, so `coding.sh usage` shows your history straight away. Once the hooks are installed, live capture takes over and the transcripts are not re-read on every read. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md).
- **CI.** A GitHub Action in this repo (`uses: jackulau/coding.sh@<ref>`) installs coding.sh, runs one command such as `eval run triage --yes` or `exp compare base head --margin 0.05`, writes the report to the step summary and to one pull request comment that later runs edit, and fails the job when the command fails. `eval run --summary <file>` appends the report as Markdown, and the workflow `eval init` prints uses the action. [Catch a regression in CI](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/ci-regressions.md).
- **MCP.** A `harness_audit` tool and a `trim` prompt let your agent audit your setup and trim what goes unused: each memory file, AGENTS.md, rule and skill with the sessions that loaded and read it and its load tokens per session, unused ones first. With `check: true` it runs `refine check` on your uncommitted edits, and it returns the `refine run` command instead of running it. Shorter shared argument descriptions keep the tool list within its byte budget. [Memory and instruction files](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#memory-and-instruction-files).
- **Harness.** `harness audit` warns about Claude Code subagent files whose `tools:` frontmatter is a map such as `edit: false`, which leaves the subagent with no tools, and gives the `disallowedTools:` line to use instead. `--json` lists them under `agentFiles`. [Explore guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#memory-and-instruction-files).
- **Capture.** `coding.sh statusline` prints a Claude Code status line (model, this session's cost, today's cost and the worst failure flag) from your stored sessions. `statusline install` wires it into the Claude Code `settings.json` and `statusline uninstall` restores the previous one byte-for-byte. [Status line how-to](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/status-line.md).
- **Refine.** `refine test --suite` and `refine run --suite` take `--reuse baseline` (or `both`), copying an earlier round's finished runs of the unchanged base arm instead of running the agent again, so a long climb spends roughly half the agent sessions. Reuse is keyed by content like `compare --reuse`, copies count `$0` toward `--max-cost`, and the round warns that the reused arm did not run interleaved; the verdict is unchanged because the copied runs are identical. [Climbing on an eval suite](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#climbing-on-an-eval-suite).
- **Cache.** `coding.sh usage --cache` turns the cache rate into advice: per agent, model, day or repo it shows the hit rate, cache-read vs cache-write tokens, the read-to-write ratio (below 1:1 you wrote more cache than you read back), the prompt-cache breaks and what they cost, and names the break causes to change: compaction, a mid-session model switch, or an idle gap past the cache lifetime. Every number is a captured token count or the per-call-priced extra the breaks cost; there is no blended-rate guess. A session that wrote a cache of at least one block over three or more turns but read back fewer tokens than it wrote is tagged `cache:low`, and `--where cache.breaks>0`, `cache.lost` and `cache.cost` query and sort on the breaks (`coding.sh sessions --where 'cache.breaks>0 sort:-cache.cost'`). `exp compare` gains `cache breaks / run` and `cache waste / run` metrics so an A/B shows whether a setup change reused the cache better, and the viewer shows the read-to-write ratio next to a session's cache rate. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md).
- **Capture.** Claude Code's `model_fallback` and `model_refusal_fallback` transcript records (the main model was overloaded and Claude Code moved to `--fallback-model`, or a refusal went to another model) become a `model.fallback` notification with both models and the trigger, and tag the session `model:fallback`. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around).
- **Cache.** Idle cache breaks say where the wait was, in `stats.cacheBreaks.byIdle` and as one `usage --cache` line each: a foreground tool call that ran past the cache lifetime (`wait`: keep foreground waits under the 5-minute subagent cache lifetime and poll in chunks), a subagent idle between turns (`subagent`), or the main session idle between turns (`away`: unavoidable while you are away; the 1-hour cache helps if you return often). [Read API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid).

### Changed

- **Site.** The top bar links only Docs and Changelog. The comparison pages stay in the docs sidebar and the blog stays in the footer. [Site](https://coding.sh/).
- **Setup.** `coding.sh init` ends by saying that recording starts with the next agent session, since an agent loads hooks when a session starts. Once capture is set up, an empty store no longer sends you back to `init`: `show latest`, `sessions`, `status`, `improve`, the MCP `status` and `session_current` tools, and the viewer ("Waiting for your first session") say to start a new agent session and to run `coding.sh doctor` if none shows up. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#what-init-changes)
- **Setup.** The installer run without a terminal (an agent's shell, CI) and without `CODING_SH_YES=1` says no agent was set up and names `coding.sh init --dry-run` and `coding.sh init --yes`, instead of `coding.sh`, which only prints help there. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#installer)
- **Improve.** `improve` and the MCP `improve` tool leave out headless sessions (`claude -p`, an Agent SDK, `codex exec`: `stats.launch` is `"headless"`), say how many they left out, and count them with `--headless` (`headless: true`). Before, one-shot scripts, judges and evals filled the report with issues an AGENTS.md line does not change. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#see-what-to-fix-first).
- **Evals.** `compare --reuse baseline` says in its help and docs that it copies the runs of an `eval run` too, so a suite's baseline from `eval run` is not paid for again in each comparison. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#reuse-finished-runs).
- **Capture.** Hooks append to one spool file per agent per UTC day (`spool/<agent>/<day>.jsonl`, continued in `<day>.1.jsonl` past 64 MB) instead of one file per hook call. A busy day used to leave about 11,000 files, each with an ingest cursor that every ingest and `gc` looked up. `gc` keeps the current day's file, and removes a deleted file's cursor instead of setting it to 0. [Architecture](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/architecture.md#spool-the-hot-path).
- **Sessions.** `model:switch` fires on every mid-session model change the agent recorded as a fallback, a switch and back included, and on any model change inside a subagent; before, only a main-thread hand-over for good counted. `stats.modelSwitch` adds the fallback `reason`, `subagent`, `count`, `switchedCalls` and `extraUsd`: what the calls on the other model cost over the first model's list prices. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).

### Fixed

- **Workflows.** A Claude Code workflow agent you stopped is `cancelled`, and one a resume re-ran under the same label is `superseded`, instead of `failed` with "no result recorded". `workflows show` and `stats` list them apart from failures and leave them out of the failure rate (`cancelledAgents`, `supersededAgents` in `--json`). On a run stopped and resumed twice, all 7 "failures" were these. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/workflows.md#4-metrics).
- **Workflows.** The wall time of a Claude Code workflow stopped and resumed in the same run dir runs from the first launch to the last result. Before, it covered only the last segment (4h43m of a 6h43m run), so the critical path came out longer than the wall and parallelism and utilization were off. `workflows show` and `stats` add the active time without the gaps between segments (`activeMs`, `segments`), and parallelism and utilization use it. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/workflows.md#4-metrics).
- **Workflows.** `workflows import`, and the import `workflows list` runs first, skip an unchanged run dir before reading its agent transcripts, and write each run's events to the index in a few large batches. On 16 synthetic runs with 470 MB of agent transcripts, a repeat import took 0.3 s instead of 13.6 s and a first import 19.5 s instead of 26.7 s. The host session's transcript is never read. [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md).
- **Workflows.** Cache hits on a Claude Code workflow resumed in the same run dir are read from the run's persisted record, since the journal writes no cache lines there. `workflows stats` showed 0 cache hits and no resume savings for a run whose record marks 11 agents cached; it now counts them and sums what they took when they ran as "resume saved", and `show` marks them "replayed on resume". [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/workflows.md#4-metrics).
- **Workflows.** `workflows show`, `stats` and the Gantt list a run's phases in the same order, by when each phase first started. Before, `stats` put phases the script dropped before a resume (such as Review and Verify) last. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/workflows.md#4-metrics).
- **Workflows.** `workflows import` redacts Claude Code workflow agent transcripts before it stores them, with the same capture settings as ingest and backfill (redaction, `[capture] redactPatterns`, metadata mode). Before, agent prompts, replies and tool output went into the index and blob store unredacted. Re-import earlier runs with `--force` to redact them. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md).
- **Workflows.** `coding.sh workflows import|show|stats` now shows cost for Claude Code Workflow runs. Claude Code transcripts carry tokens but no per-call cost, so each agent's model calls are priced from their tokens and the configured prices (as everywhere else in coding.sh), filling per-agent, per-phase and whole-run cost and `costShareByPhase`. Before, every captured workflow showed a dash for cost, hiding which stage spent the tokens. Re-import a run with `--force` to price one captured earlier. [Workflows](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/workflows.md).
- **Setup.** `coding.sh init` started with `npx coding.sh` ends with `npx coding.sh ui` and `npx coding.sh improve` as the next commands. Before, it named `coding.sh ui`, which is not on your PATH until you install it. [Install](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#npm)
- **Setup.** `doctor` no longer reports an agent as hooked while it also says the hook is missing. Each agent row names the settings file it read, and says when that file's hooks run another coding.sh home's `hook.mjs` (another `HOME` or `CODING_SH_HOME`); `doctor --json` lists the files under `hookFiles`. `init` no longer skips an agent's hooks because a project already holds hooks for another home. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md).
- **Capture.** `ingest` and `ingest --backfill` no longer print a bare event count after their summary. `ingest --json` still carries the counts. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-ingest).
- **Sessions.** A session that ran in the macOS temp dir (`/var/folders/<xx>/<yyy>/T`, where `claude -p` from a script often runs) has the repo name `tmp` in `repo:` queries and the repo facet, in SQLite and the Postgres and ClickHouse stores, instead of `T`. [Query](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md).
- **Capture.** A vendor-shaped key whose value is plainly made up, such as AWS's sample, `sk-ant-test-…`, `csh_ik_aaaa…` or `SECRETSECRETSECRET`, is still redacted but counts as `placeholder-key` and no longer tags the session `secret:exposed`. Before, working on code with test fixtures, coding.sh's own included, flagged sessions as leaking credentials. Random-looking keys count as before. `coding.sh ingest --rebuild` re-counts sessions already stored. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md).
- **Improve.** A failed compound shell line counts against the part that failed: the one its error names, the tool whose error it is, the part `timeout` stopped, or the last part of a pipeline, and "compound command" when nothing singles one out. Before, it went under its first word, so `cat`, `for`, `sed`, `export` and even `--session` (from a line continuation) filled the list with errors from other tools, and a `cat` of a test log got the broken-setup verdict. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#see-what-to-fix-first).
- **Improve.** `improve`, `improve check` and the MCP `improve` and `harness_changes` tools redact secrets and email addresses in the prompts, error lines, commands and session titles they show. Before, an email address in a prompt or an error printed as is, because capture redaction covers secrets only. `capture.redact` takes `email` to keep addresses out of the store too. [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#see-what-to-fix-first).
- **Storage.** An ingest or transcript import that stores more than 10,000 events empties the index's write-ahead log before it returns, and so does every command when it closes the index. Before, with the viewer or another command holding the index open, a full import left `index.db-wal` as large as the data it wrote (3.8 GB next to a 1.5 GB index) until a later command happened to close it last. [Architecture](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/design/architecture.md#storage).
- **Proxy.** A model call that both `coding.sh proxy` and the agent's hooks or transcript logged shows once in `show`, `show --follow`, the viewer's timeline and the MCP `session_get` and `session_current` tools, as the proxy's copy with its call id, filled in with what only the other copy has. Before, each call printed twice, and MCP `latency` counted it twice. [Capture with the proxy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md#3-read-a-call).
- **Proxy.** Storing a call no longer slows down as the session grows. The proxy folds only the new events into the session row, instead of every event in the session, and writes the call's blobs in one batch. Late in a 1,000-call session a call now takes 9 ms to store instead of 26 ms. A call stored as message blocks reads back in about 2 ms instead of 9 ms. `bench.mjs proxy-session` measures the write against an older build. [Capture with the proxy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy.md).
- **Evals.** `exp show <id>` takes the experiment id that `eval run` prints for a tasks suite and lists its runs per task with the agent's cost at API prices. Before, it said the experiment was not found. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#the-report).
- **Refine.** When a `refine run --suite` climb holds a cheaper or faster candidate whose quality is even on average but whose interval is too wide to confirm within `--margin`, the round now says the hold is a sample-size problem ("the quality interval is too wide to adopt it within the margin at N tasks: add tasks or trials") instead of implying the gain cost quality, and carries a warning that names the quality lower bound against the margin and the fix. The verdict is unchanged; only the explanation is clearer. [How good is the hill-climb, really?](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/research/hillclimb-quality.md).
- **Cost.** `usage` `TOK/TURN` (`tokensPerTurn`) divides only top-level sessions' tokens by their turns. Before, subagents' tokens were divided by their parents' turns, so a row with heavy subagents, or a model only subagents ran, showed an inflated or impossible tokens per turn. [Expensive sessions](https://coding.sh/docs/cost).

### Performance

- **Ingest.** The SQLite index gives each connection a 16 MB page cache (`cache_size`) and in-memory temp b-trees (`temp_store`), both bounded RAM freed on close, and ingest skips the duplicate `session_facets` inserts a session repeats across its events (the facet table is unchanged). Measured the paired way with `scripts/dogfood/bench.mjs ingest --baseline <dist>`. [Store ingest](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md#store-ingest).

## [0.1.2] - 2026-11-08

coding.sh runs on Windows, and CI tests it there.

### Added

- **Sessions.** A new `text-tool-call` flag marks a session whose model wrote a tool call out as text, so the call never ran. In Claude Code this is usually a subagent whose `tools:` frontmatter is a map (`edit: false`), which leaves it with no tools; `improve` names the fix. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Sessions.** A session whose main thread moved to another model mid-session, such as Claude Code's silent fallback to an older model after a refusal, gets the `model:switch` tag and `stats.modelSwitch` with both models, when, and the trigger (`refusal`, `error` or `/model`). `stats.models` counts calls per model (both show in a Models row in `show` and the MCP digest), a refused call adds the `refusal` flag, and a session's model is now the one most of its main-thread calls ran on instead of the last one seen. Helper models (Haiku titles) and subagents are not a switch. [Read API](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md#get-apisessionsid).
- **Evals.** `eval validate` takes several tasks, or `--tag <tag>` for every task with that tag (`suite:<name>` for a suite), prints one line per task and exits 1 if any did not validate. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#session-to-eval).
- **Evals.** `eval init --sessions --repo <DIR|all>` drafts tasks from sessions in another checkout or worktree of the project, not only the current one. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md).
- **Docs.** Use-case pages for tracking Claude Code and Codex cost, replaying a session, testing an AGENTS.md or skill change, building evals from failures, self-hosting, and logging model calls through the proxy. Comparison pages for Langfuse, LangSmith, Braintrust, AgentOps, Laminar and ccusage, each with a feature table, where the other tool is the better choice, and its sources. [Compare](https://coding.sh/docs/compare).
- **Site.** Every docs page has its own link preview image, the home page and FAQ carry FAQ markup, and the site build fails on a missing or too-long title or description, a wrong canonical URL, or a link to a page that doesn't exist.
- **Evals.** `eval validate` checks verifiers that grade the agent's answer or trace: a task dir's `reference/` (`output.md`, `stdout.jsonl`) is a correct run that must pass, and `near-misses/<label>/` hold wrong runs that must fail. The verifier reads them through `CODING_SH_AGENT_OUTPUT`, `CODING_SH_AGENT_STDOUT` and `CODING_SH_AGENT_DIFF`, as in a real run. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#hand-written-tasks).
- **Evals.** A hand-written task can start from a commit with `"start": {"commit": "<sha or ref>", "repo": "<path>"}` in `task.json`, so a suite can live in the repo it tests. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#hand-written-tasks).
- **Capture.** `coding.sh init` imports the sessions each agent already keeps on this machine, so the viewer the installer opens shows your own history. It shows progress, stops after 8 seconds and imports the rest in the background; the viewer says "Importing past sessions…" until it is done. `--no-import` skips it. An empty viewer shows the import command, `coding.sh ingest --backfill`, and what each imported session shows. [What init changes](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#what-init-changes).
- **Examples.** `examples/plugin-ab` A/B tests a Claude Code plugin against plain Claude Code on ten small tasks in a container, with `coding.sh compare`: pass rate, tokens, cost split by token type, wall time, reply length and a blind clarity judge, paired by task with bootstrap intervals. It takes any plugin directory and ships a small demo plugin. [The example](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/tree/main/examples/plugin-ab).
- **Improve.** `coding.sh improve` lists what goes wrong most in this repo's sessions (failure flags, and any command failing the same way in two or more sessions), each with an example session, the AGENTS.md line that fixes it and the next commands. `improve fix N` adds the line, uncommitted. `improve check` compares the sessions before and after each committed AGENTS.md, rule or skill change, with how often the agent follows each new line, says Helped, Regressed, No clear change or Too early, and exits 1 on a regression; `--watch` re-checks as sessions arrive. No API key needed. [Improve your agent every week](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md).
- **MCP.** `improve` and `harness_changes` tools, and `improve` and `did-it-help` prompts, run the same loop from inside your agent: what to fix first, and whether an AGENTS.md change helped, without a shell. [Improve your agent every week](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#from-inside-your-agent).
- **Harness.** `coding.sh harness audit` lists each memory and instruction file (Claude auto-memory, AGENTS.md and the other instructions files, rules, skills) with the sessions that loaded it, read it and wrote it, its size and its load cost in tokens, and flags files never read, written but never read back, and skills listed but never used. `--agent`, `--since` (default 30d) and `--json`. [Memory and instruction files](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#memory-and-instruction-files).
- **Sessions.** Text that Claude Code hooks add to the context (a plugin's SessionStart notes, a UserPromptSubmit reminder, PostToolUse additionalContext) is stored as `hook.context` events with its hook event, source and size. `stats.hookContext` counts the tokens by source, each source tags the session (`hook:plugin:terse`, `hook:settings`), `sessions --where hook:plugin:<name>` or `hook.tokens>500` filters on it, `--facets hook` counts sessions per source, and `show` lists it. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Sessions.** Headless sessions (`claude -p`, an Agent SDK, `codex exec`) are marked `stats.launch: "headless"`, and terminal or IDE sessions `"interactive"`, from Claude Code's entrypoint and Codex's originator. `sessions --where launch:headless` filters on it and `--facets launch` counts it. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Improve.** `improve check` lists, under each change, the sessions still flagged after it with their flags and start time (five, or all with `--sessions`; every flagged session before and after in `--json`), and the MCP `harness_changes` tool returns them as `after.flaggedSessions`, so your agent can answer "which sessions still failed after it?". [Did it help?](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#did-your-last-change-help)
- **Sessions.** `show` has a `Flags` row, and `sessions` ends with how many listed sessions are flagged and a pointer to `coding.sh improve`.
- **Evals.** `coding.sh eval init` writes an eval suite to `evals/<name>/` from failed or flagged sessions in the repo (`--sessions`, `--session latest`), a skill's should and should-not prompts (`--skill`), or cases for an app (`--cases`, `--describe` with `--command` or `--url`). `--draft` has a model write the prompts or cases, marked draft. It picks a code grader when it can and a judge to calibrate when it can't, marks the suite capability or regression, and ends with the run command and, for cases and skills, a GitHub Actions workflow. On a terminal with no flags it asks. [Your first eval in 5 minutes](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#your-first-eval-in-5-minutes).
- **Evals.** `coding.sh eval run [suite…]` runs suites k times per task and reports pass@k, pass^k, flaky tasks, the session of each failed trial, small suites and saturation. A regression suite exits 1 when a task fails most of its trials or nothing could be graded; a capability suite never fails on its score. [The report](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#the-report).
- **MCP.** An `eval_init` tool lets your agent build an eval suite for you, with the same sources as `eval init`; it writes files only with `confirm: true`. [MCP server](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp).
- **Evals.** `eval import` warns when a verifier names a `tests/` path but the hidden files land at the work-tree root (`tests/check.js` is copied to `check.js`). Such a verifier finds no file, or runs zero tests and passes. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#hand-written-tasks).
- **Judges.** LLM graders take `"provider": "claude-code"`, which grades through `claude -p` on a Claude subscription (`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` from `claude setup-token`) instead of an API key. The judge runs with no tools, setting sources, plugins, MCP servers or saved session, so the hooks of the arm under test never reach it. [Evaluate an Agent Skill](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/evaluate-a-skill#4-grade-in-two-steps).
- **Stores.** The Postgres and ClickHouse store plugins answer `--where` in the database, with every value bound: session and event keys, `has:`, `is:`, bare words, `sort:` and `--facets`, for `sessions`, `usage`, `export`, the viewer API and MCP. `sessions --where` and facets no longer stop at the newest 20,000 sessions there, and event keys and bare words no longer fail with `unsupported_store`. [Query language](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md).
- **Harness A/B.** Each run keeps the end of its verifier's output (the last 4096 characters of stdout and stderr, colour codes removed, redacted with `capture.redact`), each repair round too. `compare` and `eval run` print the last lines of failed runs under the report, and `--json` lists them in `failedRuns`. [Verifier output](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#verifier-output).
- **Harness A/B.** Harness bundles carry Claude Code plugins (hooks, skills, agents, commands, MCP servers: the whole plugin directory) with `harness snapshot --plugin <name|dir>` or `plugins = [...]` in a harness TOML, and `compare A --with plugin:<name>` runs "with the plugin vs without" in one command (`--without plugin:<name>` drops one). Each run loads the plugin with `--plugin-dir` from its own home; your Claude config is never changed. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#claude-code-plugins), [Does this plugin help me?](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/does-this-plugin-help).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare --repair N` reports Seq@1..N+1 per arm: the share of tasks solved by the k-th attempt with verifier feedback, with intervals, where the gain lands (EGS, LGS), rounds that changed no file, and the agent's spend per solved run. With `--k 2` or more it adds a cost front of k attempts with feedback against k independent attempts (unbiased pass@k), and a note when the two arms rank one way on the first attempt and the other way after the rounds (Seq@K, arXiv 2610.37859). [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#repair-rounds).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare --repair N` tells the agent "Verification failed." in its own session (Claude Code `--resume`, `codex exec resume`) after a failed verifier, up to N rounds, with the hidden tests out of its tree. The report adds the repair rate, mean rounds and the rounds' cost per arm. `--repair-signal names` adds the failing test names from the verifier output; a round that edits, deletes or skips tests is not a repair; `--false-alarm P` tells a seeded share of passing runs they failed; `--repair-verdict` decides on the outcome after repair. Repair settings are part of the `--reuse` key (BOBA, arXiv 2611.02207). [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#repair-rounds).
- **Skills.** `eval trigger <skill-dir>` checks that the agent loads a skill when it should and not when it shouldn't. It runs the `should` and `should_not` prompts in `triggers.toml` k times each with the skill and its siblings installed, reads each load from the captured session, and reports precision, recall and F1 with Wilson intervals, the missed and flaky prompts, and near misses that went to the expected sibling. `--description` or `--candidate` A/Bs a new description with a paired bootstrap CI and the prompts that flipped. `--min-f1` and `--fail-on regression` set the exit code. CI checks it with real Claude Code and Codex against a stub model. [Evaluate an Agent Skill](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/evaluate-a-skill#does-the-skill-load).
- **Examples.** The skill eval tests an edit to the skill: `run.sh <out-dir> --candidate <skill-dir>` runs the pinned skill as arm A and the edited copy as arm B, graded the same way, with the `--promote` line in the report. `TASKS="01 06"` runs some of the tasks. [Skill eval example](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/tree/main/examples/skill-eval#run-it).
- **Self-host.** `docker run -p 4318:4318 -v coding-sh:/data ghcr.io/jackulau/coding.sh` starts a team server with no configuration: with no keys set, the first start makes a read key and an ingest key, keeps them in the volume and prints them with the connect command. Each tagged release publishes the image for amd64 and arm64, starting with this one. `deploy/compose/` has SQLite, Postgres and ClickHouse stacks that need no edits. The viewer's sign-in page takes the read key in a form. serve waits up to 30 s for its database to accept connections. [Self-host for a team](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/self-host-team).
- **Self-host.** `coding.sh init --remote <url> --key csh_ik_…` connects a machine to a team server in one command: it checks the key, installs capture hooks and adds the `team` sink. `--remote-mode full` also sends prompts and output. [Self-host for a team](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/self-host-team#4-connect-each-machine).
- **Replay.** `coding.sh replay <session>` and a Replay mode on the viewer's session page step through a session with the files the agent touched rebuilt as of each step: each step's diff, a file's content at that step, what a range of steps changed, and shell commands with exit code and output. Where content can't be rebuilt, it says which step and why. The viewer plays at 1×, 4× or 16× with a scrubber and `?step=N` links; the MCP `session_replay` tool returns the same. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#replay-a-session).
