- **Capture.** `coding.sh proxy` is a local pass-through for the Anthropic and OpenAI APIs. Each model call is logged with its redacted request (system prompt, tool definitions, messages) and response, usage with cache reads and writes, status, time to first byte and token, and rate limits, inside the Claude Code or Codex session its hooks capture. Auth headers are forwarded and never stored. It adds 0.34 ms per streamed chunk and 1.96 ms to first byte (CI fails over 2 ms and 5 ms). [Capture with the proxy](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy).
- **Install.** `init --proxy` points Claude Code (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`) and Codex (a `coding-sh` provider) at the proxy and starts it; the hook restarts it when a session starts. `uninstall --proxy` undoes only that, and `doctor` shows whether it runs. CI checks it with real Claude Code and Codex. [Capture with the proxy](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy#2-point-your-client-at-it).
- **Install.** `init --proxy` keeps a base URL the agent already used (a gateway or CLIProxyAPI in Claude Code's `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, your own Codex provider, `openai_base_url` or `OPENAI_BASE_URL`) as the proxy's upstream and chains through it, instead of asking for `--force`. `uninstall --proxy` puts the old value back. [Already using a gateway or CLIProxyAPI?](https://coding.sh/docs/ai-gateway)
- **Capture.** Proxy calls record reasoning effort (including Anthropic's `output_config.effort`), service tier and speed from the request when the response has none, and `compaction: true` on calls that compacted the conversation, including Codex's `/responses/compact`, which is now logged. [What is stored](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy#3-read-a-call)
- **Capture.** The proxy stores a call's messages in content-addressed blocks shared with the session's earlier calls, instead of the whole conversation again on every call, and reads the body back exactly. A synthetic 500-call session took 5× fewer bytes (828 KiB instead of 4,116 KiB); reading a call back takes about 10 ms. Works on SQLite, Postgres and ClickHouse stores; calls stored whole still read, and `gc` and `purge` follow the blocks. [What is stored](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy#3-read-a-call)
- **Capture.** Proxy calls from a Claude Code subagent land in the subagent's own session instead of the parent's. Calls record the parent session, Claude Code's agent id and Codex's parent thread, fork and subagent kind when the client sends them, the account as a 16-character hash (the account id is not stored, in the event or the request body), and behind CLIProxyAPI the credential index it used. A session seen only through the proxy links to its parent. [What is stored](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy#3-read-a-call)
- **Serve.** `coding.sh serve` mounts the proxy at `/proxy/`: send an ingest key in `x-coding-sh-key` and the agent's own API key goes upstream unchanged. [REST API](https://coding.sh/docs/api#post-proxypath).
- **Sessions.** `show <session>#n` (or a call id) prints one model call's system prompt, tools, settings, cache use and cost, and `diff-calls` compares two calls in prompt-cache order and names what broke the cache. The viewer shows both on a proxy call. [Capture with the proxy](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/capture-with-proxy#4-see-why-the-prompt-cache-missed).
- **Sessions.** `replay <session> --step N --fork` sends the model call behind step N again with a changed AGENTS.md, rule, skill, system prompt or model (`--with`, `--harness`, `--model`, `--system`, `-k`) and prints the recorded next action beside the forked one. `--run` carries on with Claude Code in a copy of the repo, the stored history spliced into its requests, and `--verify` runs a check there. It needs a session captured through the proxy. The viewer's Replay pane has a "Fork from here" tab, and the MCP `session_replay` tool takes `fork`. [Explore sessions](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#fork-from-a-step).
- **Evals.** `replay --fork --save-case <suite>` keeps the step as a case in `evals/<suite>`: a `request` task that sends the stored request with the project's current context files, graded by a `next-action` grader that fails when the model repeats the recorded action. `eval run` runs it like any suite. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#fork-a-failing-step-into-a-case).
- **Replay.** `exp replay` and `replay --fork` use `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (from `claude setup-token`) for Anthropic calls when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is not set. [Log model calls](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/replay-llm-calls).
- **Experiments.** `exp replay <call> --vary model=a,b --vary system=@file.md --n 5` resends a logged model call with your own API key, grades whether each reply takes the agent's first action (or with a judge), and reports the paired comparison of `exp compare`. `--dry-run` prints the cost estimate. [Experiments](https://coding.sh/docs/experiments#exp-replay).
- **Install.** Windows: run the installer from Git Bash. Hook commands use `C:/…` paths that cmd.exe, PowerShell and Git Bash all run. CI runs the tests and the installer on Windows. [Install guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows).
- **Capture.** CI runs real OpenCode, Pi, Goose, Factory Droid, GitHub Copilot CLI and Aider sessions on Linux and Windows against a stub model, checks the recorded prompt, tool call, file change, model and reply, and checks that `ingest --backfill` adds no duplicates. [Install guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md#windows).
- **Gateways.** OpenRouter, LiteLLM, Cloudflare AI Gateway, Portkey, Helicone and Ramp Router (router.com) join Vercel: sessions get a `gateway:<name>` tag, `gateway enrich` fetches billed cost where the gateway has a lookup API, `gateway status` shows one row per gateway, and `compare --gateway <name>` routes runs through it. [AI gateways](https://coding.sh/docs/ai-gateway).
- **SDKs.** The TypeScript and Python SDKs detect all twelve gateways, not only Vercel: by base URL, a self-hosted gateway's URL env var, a response header (`cf-aig-log-id`, `helicone-id`, `x-litellm-call-id`, `x-portkey-trace-id`, …) or the response id. Each model call records the gateway and its request id, so SDK sessions get the `gateway:<name>` tag and `gateway enrich` can fetch their billed cost. The SDK tables are generated from the server's gateway list. [AI gateways](https://coding.sh/docs/ai-gateway#sdk).
- **Experiments.** `compare` records the agent release each run started on (`agentVersion`, checked at most once a minute, so an auto-update mid-run shows). When the arms ran on different releases, the report adds `agent claude-code 2.1.316 ×12  2.1.319 ×12  release differs` and a note that the delta mixes the change with the release, `--gate` repeats it, and `--promote` holds with `release_confound`. `--json` carries `report.agentVersions` (arXiv 2610.37332). [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#agent-release).
- **Experiments.** `exp compare` and `compare` give the exact McNemar power next to the normal MDE: `power   MDE 15.6pp · no 80% power at this n (exact McNemar ceiling 62%)` or `· exact MDE 10.6pp`. The exact test needs 6 flipped cases to reject at α 0.05, so a small suite can have no 80% power for any gap; a `no-power` warning says so, and `--json` carries `power.exact` (arXiv 2610.04433). [Experiments](https://coding.sh/docs/experiments#compare).
- **Experiments.** `exp run --series NAME` tags each round of an iteration loop and prints whether to stop. `exp plateau` runs a restart e-detector over the series and names the version to ship. It exits 3 to stop, 0 to continue and 2 with too few experiments (Yin et al., arXiv 2610.04756). [Experiments](https://coding.sh/docs/experiments#plateau).
- **Experiments.** `exp metrics` A/Bs two commands that print numbers (ms, bytes) in interleaved pairs, with a 95% paired bootstrap CI and a sign test per metric. `--fail-on-worse` exits 1 for CI. [Experiments](https://coding.sh/docs/experiments#metrics).
- **Experiments.** `compare` notes the runs whose model calls another model answered: after a refusal Claude Code retries on an older model, so a run can measure a model its arm did not ask for. The note counts those runs per arm and names the model; `--json` carries `report.modelMix`. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#agent-release).
- **Capture.** Sessions stored before an adapter's parsing changed are re-parsed from their transcripts and spool in the background, so this release's dedupe fixes reach data already captured. `coding.sh ingest --rebuild` does it now. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#old-sessions-show-doubled-counts).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare --gate` answers "ship B or not": `Ship it: B wins 9 tasks, loses 0`, `Hold: not enough evidence`, or `Don't ship: B breaks 3 tasks A solved` with the broken tasks listed. It exits 0 only for "Ship it" (SAGE, arXiv 2609.36043). `--by suite:` gates each tagged suite too, and one broken suite blocks a pooled "Ship it". Each arm's failed runs are split by the agent's own last test run: `A fails 12: own tests green 7 (58%), red 3, stale 1, none 1`, where green fails are silent misses (BOBA, arXiv 2611.02207). `compare --promote` also keeps a B that is cheaper at the same quality: `pareto: support 0.93 ≥ 0.70 → promote (cost-cut: …)` or `→ hold`, exit 0 only on promote (ParetoSaddler, arXiv 2611.02079). When B adds or removes one rule, skill or context file, the report adds a one-item gate scored only on tasks where the item reached the agent's context: `item gate k=3 τ=.34 m=2: KEEP · …`, or RECHECK, KILL, RERUN, and ACCEPT, REJECT, UNMEASURED for removals; `--item-gate` sets the exit code (Cortex, arXiv 2610.14216). CI checks the gate and the own-tests split on real Claude Code and Codex sessions against a stub model. The MCP `harness_compare` tool lists these A/B results and returns one with its gates and own-tests split. [Compare agents](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/compare-agents.md#4-read-the-result).
- **Experiments.** `exp compare --gate` gives the same ship-or-hold answer per case and exits 1 unless it is "Ship it". `exp sweep` ends with `adopt <winner>` or `keep <start>` and the reason. The MCP `experiment_compare` tool returns the gate too. With 2+ trials, `exp compare` also lists the cases B gained and lost (passed in any trial), counts cases that got more or less reliable, and warns when the two halves of the trials disagree; `--matrix` prints the success counts (EvoAudit, arXiv 2611.07624). [Experiments](https://coding.sh/docs/experiments#compare).
- **Durable workflows.** A Vercel Workflow, Temporal, Inngest, Restate, DBOS, LangGraph or Cloudflare Workflows run (Temporal, Restate and DBOS in both TypeScript and Python) is one session across processes, traces, crashes and deploys (keyed by run id). `show`, `workflows show` and the viewer list each step with its attempts, failed and lost ones included, show sleeps and LangGraph interrupts as waiting rather than work, mark where the run resumed after a crash, deploy or restart, and leave replayed model calls out of tokens. An attempt lost with its process counts as busy only while its process was still seen; `workflows stats` shows the rest as "lost to crashes" instead of agent time. A span with `workflow.wait.name` around a sleep is a wait in any engine, for those that export none (Temporal, Inngest, DBOS TS). New flags `step-retried` and `resumed`. Each engine is checked in CI against a real run with a failed step, a killed worker, a sleep and a restart. [Trace a durable workflow](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/trace-durable-workflow).
- **Evals.** Behaviour tasks can be written by hand and loaded with `eval import tasks/*/`; `compare --tag <tag>` runs them. Verifiers get the agent's final answer and full stdout (`CODING_SH_AGENT_OUTPUT`, `CODING_SH_AGENT_STDOUT`), and `judge grade --grader checklist.json` grades both from inside one. `compare` reports `agent spend` and `grader spend` rows, `--max-cost 15` stops starting runs at $15, per-task rows show task names, and a fresh store gets its hook script so runs are captured. [Evaluate an Agent Skill](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/evaluate-a-skill).
- **Viewer.** Cost shows in the session list, the session header and on each model call. Estimates read `~$0.04` (hover: "Estimated from list prices", and which price a call used); billed and reported costs have no mark; a call with no price shows `—` and says why.
- **Judges.** Judge prompts escape data tags in the judged text and say it is untrusted, so an output can't close `</output>` and order a pass. `judge calibrate --probe-injection judge.json` plants a "respond pass" order in human-fail cases and reports how many verdicts flip; any flip exits 1. Default-prompt judges get new version ids. [Evals](https://coding.sh/docs/judges#calibrate).
- **Judges.** Checklist items can grade rule compliance from the trace with no model call: `ran-command`, `read-path`, `wrote-identifier`, and `follows-rules`, which checks every imperative line that names a backticked command, flag, path or identifier (``Never use `git push --force` ``) against what the agent did. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#checklists-and-trace-checks).
- **Cost.** Estimates price each model call at the rates it ran at: long-context rates past the model's threshold (OpenAI over 272K, Gemini Pro and Claude Sonnet 4.5 1M over 200K, Haiku 5.5 over 100K), and batch, flex, priority, fast or Claude fast-mode rates from the call's recorded service tier. The call's `payload.priceTier` says which, and shows in `show` and the viewer's cost tooltip. `usage --json` adds `estimatedCostByTier`. `[prices]` takes `long_context` and tier tables. Claude 1-hour cache writes are priced at 2x input instead of 1.25x, from Claude Code's `cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` and OpenTelemetry `cache_creation_1h_tokens`, and Claude 4.6+ calls with `inference_geo: "us"` at 1.1x (`[prices]` takes `cache_write_1h` and a `geo` table). Stored estimates reprice by themselves. [Where cost comes from](https://coding.sh/docs/cost#where-cost-comes-from).
- **Judges.** `judge calibrate --grader tests` audits a test or command verifier: `--sample-passes 40` lists unlabelled passing cases to label, and the audit reports precision (how many passes a person would accept) with a Wilson interval and the false accepts. `exp show` and `exp compare` then print the precision and a pass rate corrected for it. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#auditing-a-test-verifier).
- **Workflows.** `workflows compare` gives each variant P(front), the share of bootstrap draws in which no other variant beats it on quality and cost (`front 0.93`, `dominated by base (front 0.41)`). Near-equal noisy variants no longer get a confident "dominated". `--select min-cost --q-drop 10`, `--select weighted --lambda 2` or `--select pareto` picks a variant to deploy and prints `deploy: cand (…)` with the reason. `--seed` fixes the bootstrap (ParetoSaddler, arXiv 2611.02079). [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/workflows.md#compare-them)
- **Workflows.** `workflows compare` checks replay experiments for a control fork. When the candidate resumes from recorded runs (`resumeFromRunId`) and no baseline run resumes from the same ones, it notes "no control fork", since the difference would mix the edit with the replay itself. With a control, it reports how often the control's outcome changed from its source run as the replay noise floor (`control.drift` in `--json`). [Workflows guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#workflows).
- **Production.** `coding.sh-store-clickhouse` runs serve, the CLI and MCP on ClickHouse: `--store 'clickhouse://user:pass@host:8123/db'` (`clickhouses://` for TLS). It has no runtime dependencies and passes the store conformance suite; blobs stay on `file://` or S3. The same page shows how to send sessions to an OpenTelemetry Collector that writes to ClickHouse, with SQL for cost, slow tools and failed sessions. [Self-host for a team](https://coding.sh/docs/stores#clickhouse).
- **Evals.** `eval validate` flags hidden tests that name a helper or file only the reference adds and the instruction never mentions, which fail valid fixes written differently (`--strict-coupling` makes it blocking). `eval crosscheck <task>` has a reviewer model judge stored runs' diffs against the instruction and lists the runs where it disagrees with the verifier, as false rejects or false accepts. `--solver <bundle>` solves the task once first. The counts are stored on the task in the same shape as a human verifier audit. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#session-to-eval)
- **Experiments.** Long runs survive interruptions. `exp run`, `exp aa`, `exp sweep`, `compare`, `bench run` and `workflows run` save each trial as it finishes, and `--resume [<id>]` re-runs only the unfinished ones, with the same seed, order and `--stop-early` decision as an uninterrupted run. A changed config refuses to resume and names what changed. The first Ctrl-C lets trials in flight finish. Rate limits, 5xx and network errors are retried with backoff (`--retries`, default 3), task failures never are. `--trial-timeout` caps a trial, and `exp status` shows progress, ETA and cost so far. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#long-runs-resume-retries-status).
- **Judges.** `follows-rules` reads rule-file frontmatter: a Claude Code rule with `paths:`, a Cursor rule with `globs:` (or neither glob nor `alwaysApply`), or a Copilot rule with `applyTo:` is graded only after the agent touches a matching file or opens the rule. Before that the agent never saw it, so it no longer counts as skipped. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#checklists-and-trace-checks)
- **Experiments.** `coding.sh exp ab` tests an AGENTS.md, rule or skill edit on your own recent sessions, with no eval tasks to write. It finds the moments your agent went wrong (said done without running anything, stopped with tests failing, retried a failed command unchanged), replays the next turn there with your committed files and with your edit, and has a judge score both. It prints the fixed and broken cases, the same ship gate as `compare --gate`, and the replay noise floor from the control arm. `--dry-run` shows the cost first, `--max-cost` caps it, reruns are free from the cache, and `--watch` reruns on every save. CI runs it on real Claude Code sessions against a stub model. [Test an AGENTS.md change in five minutes](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/test-agents-md-change).
- **Experiments.** The pieces under `exp ab` work on their own: a `continuation` task replays a frozen session's next turn with given context files, and a decision-point miner turns sessions into a dataset of those moments, each with its own rubric. A continuation experiment needs a control arm: run it as an A/B, or pass `--no-control` and get a warning that there is no noise floor to read the difference against. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#ab-a-harness-edit-on-your-own-sessions).
- **Refine.** `coding.sh refine propose|test|apply|rollback|log|run` improves AGENTS.md, rules and skills in tested rounds. `propose` asks a model for lines that address your sessions' most severe flags, with protected lines marked READ-ONLY. `test` checks a candidate before any model call (protected lines, harmful or duplicate added lines), A/Bs it against your current files on the decision points `exp ab` mines, and rejects it when it forgets what an earlier kept edit won (`guard: forgets kept edit r_…`). `apply` writes a kept edit with a backup, `rollback` restores it byte for byte, and `log` lists every round with its verdict, effect CI, guard result and reason. `run` loops until `--rounds`, `--max-cost` or the plateau detector says stop. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#refine-loop).
- **Refine.** `coding.sh refine score labels.jsonl --pred FILE` scores diagnoses against hand labels by code: occurrence, Signal and session precision, recall and F1, Signal coverage, recall per taxonomy family and, with 2+ `--pred` files, repeat agreement, each with its counts. Matching is maximum one-to-one on session, component and top-3 type ids. Without `--pred` it measures the precision of each session flag (Signals, arXiv 2611.04507). [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#scoring-diagnoses).
- **Refine.** `refine test|run --suite <name>` judges a harness edit on an eval suite of repo tasks, k trials per arm, and can make the harness cheaper and faster, not only better: `--objective cost,wall` keeps an edit whose pass rate holds within `--margin` (2pp by default) and whose cost, tokens, wall time, LLM time, turns or size drop significantly, and never keeps one that loses quality beyond the margin. With a cost or time objective the proposer cuts, shown what made the runs slow or expensive; `--proposer-command 'claude -p'` writes the edit with a command. `--until-plateau` climbs until the e-detector says it stopped gaining, `--resume` continues an interrupted round, a skill directory can be the `--target`, and `refine log` shows each climb's rounds and Pareto front. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#climbing-on-an-eval-suite).
- **Harness A/B.** `coding.sh refine check` exits 1 on an AGENTS.md, rule or skill edit that deletes or rewords a protected safety line, or adds an exception that relaxes one (``protected-line: AGENTS.md:3 "Never run `git push --force`." (auto)``), since a pass-rate A/B can't see that harm (Masked Erosion, arXiv 2610.38392). A line is protected by a `<!-- coding.sh:protect -->` block, a `[refine] protect` regex, or automatically when one sentence pairs a gate word with a safety topic; `refine protected` lists them. `exp ab` runs the same check before any model call and fails `--gate` on it. `--allow-protected-edit AGENTS.md:3` allows one on purpose. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#protected-safety-lines).
- **Docs.** A Workflow experiments page: capture and score multi-agent runs, `workflows compare`, replays, the control fork and the replay noise floor. [Workflow experiments](https://coding.sh/docs/workflows)
- **Long sessions.** A session that runs for hours or days reads as one timeline split into phases: the start, each `claude --resume`, each compaction and each long idle gap. `show` lists the phases with day lines, `show --all` lists every event, and `show --follow` prints new events while a session runs. Derails mark where a run went off the rails: `looping`, `failure-streak`, `churn`, `no-progress`, `stalled` and `context-thrash`, each with the event and time it started. They show in `show`, on the viewer timeline (with ‹ › to step through them), in `sessions --flag <name>`, and in the MCP `sessions_list` and `session_get` tools. `compare` reports derails and time to first derail per run, and a `progress` grader scores time on track for `exp compare`. The viewer loads a 40,000-event session in the background and scrolls it smoothly, and MCP `session_get` with `tail` reads a long session's latest events. Live hook ingest updates a long session from its new events only: 0.46 s per update on a 30,000-event session, down from 1.16 s. [Long sessions](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#long-sessions).
- **Experiments.** `exp compare --margin 0.03` checks that B is at most 3pp worse than A (paired non-inferiority on the 95% interval's lower bound) and exits 1 otherwise: `non-inf B − A ≥ -3.0pp: yes (95% CI lower bound -1.2pp > -3.0pp)`. It lists cases A passed on every trial that B now mostly fails. [Experiments](https://coding.sh/docs/experiments#non-inferiority).
- **Tool evals.** A `calls` check: tools the trial must call (`must`), must not call (`mustNot`), at most `max` calls, and an `order`, with partial argument matches (`~text` substrings, `<=N` bounds, dotted paths). It reads the trial's tap; the MCP tap now keeps each call's arguments, redacted. `exp run` takes the same fields as a `calls` grader over the task's trace. [Tool evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/TOOL-EVALS.md#3-task-format).
- **Experiments.** `exp run` and `toolevals run` take `--samples adaptive`: 2 trials per case, a 3rd when they disagree, and a case fails at half or more failing; the report gives case verdicts and no CI. `--stop-on-regression --baseline <experiment>` stops the run and exits 1 once a case that passed every baseline trial fails. [Gating runs](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#gating-runs-tie-breaks-and-the-regression-stop).
- **Tool evals.** `toolevals compare` prints `p (perm)` beside McNemar: a task-paired permutation test on the per-task differences, exact over all sign flips up to 20 tasks and seeded Monte Carlo above. It holds its level at the 5-11 tasks a suite often has, where bootstrap intervals are too narrow (AT-AT, arXiv 2610.34679). `--json` carries `report.permutation`; verdicts are unchanged. [Tool evals](https://coding.sh/docs/tool-evals#experiments).
- **Judges.** `judge calibrate` prints the judge's discrimination gap and label efficiency η, and warns when auditing outputs directly would need fewer human labels: `label efficiency η 0.21 · auditing outputs directly needs ~4.8x fewer human labels for the same precision`. `--judge-cost 0.001` adds the human label cost above which the judge pays off. The calibration state is unchanged; `--json` carries `calibration.efficiency` (Choi et al., arXiv 2610.35698). [Evals](https://coding.sh/docs/judges#calibrate).
- **Judges.** `exp compare --pairwise` and judge graders in `exp compare` print how often a resample of the cases picks the same winner, and n*, the smallest size where that holds 95% of the time: `stable  B better in 97% of resamples (n*=50)` or `unstable  B better in 61% of resamples (n* censored)`. An uncalibrated judge that never settles warns "pick or calibrate the judge before collecting more verdicts" (arXiv 2611.04090). `--json` carries `stability`. [Evals](https://coding.sh/docs/judges).
- **Judges.** `judge sample <exp> --budget 60 --out plan.json` says which cases to label next: a seeded random sample stratified by the judge's verdict and confidence, with labels in proportion to stratum size, so the labels don't favour the doubtful cases. `judge calibrate --plan plan.json` then prints the human-audited pass rate: `human-audited pass rate 0.71 [0.63, 0.79] (42/50 of plan labelled; worst case ±0.09)` (Choi et al., arXiv 2610.35698). [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#which-cases-to-label).
- **Site.** Pages are ready about 0.5 s sooner on a slow phone, with half the font bytes and less layout shift. A Lighthouse A/B with budgets runs on every site change. [Site speed](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md).
- **Site.** A blog at /blog: posts are Markdown files in `apps/site/src/content/blog`, each with its own page, title card and sitemap entry, and an Atom feed at /blog.xml. The first post explains why coding.sh keeps your sessions on your machine. [Blog](https://coding.sh/blog).
- **Capture.** Claude Code Bash commands that edit files (`sed -i`, a heredoc write, `tee`) record a `file.change` per file with its diff, taken from Claude Code's own edit diff, even when the command then failed. A nested instructions file or skills folder loaded mid-session, a Skill call's skill folder and the skills restored after `/compact` become `harness.loaded` events and show in `show` and the viewer timeline. Stored Claude Code sessions are re-parsed to pick them up. CI checks both on a real Claude Code 2.1.319 session. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around).
- **Sessions.** Each skill a session used, through the Skill tool or by reading or running a file in its folder, adds a `skill:<name>` tag, so `sessions --flag skill:pdf` finds those sessions. `show` lists them as `Skills  pdf (project, invoked, first, before any edit) · pdf-tools (personal, read)`, and `show --json` and MCP `session_get` return `skills`. A new `skill-copy-conflict` flag marks a session that used two copies of one skill (arXiv 2610.11647). [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Cost.** Two kinds of waste per session. Re-fetches are read-only calls (Read, Grep, `cat`, `git diff`, …) that returned the same result as an earlier call in the same context; three or more add the `duplicate-fetch` flag (arXiv 2611.07238). Cache breaks are model calls that found the prompt cache gone and paid for the prompt again, by cause (compaction, model switch, idle, other), priced as cache write minus cache read (arXiv 2610.35496). `show` prints `Cache breaks  2: 31k prompt tokens billed again, $0.1086 extra (other 1, compaction 1)`, `usage` totals both per group, and the viewer header, `--json` and MCP carry `stats.waste` and `stats.cacheBreaks`. [API reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/api.md).
- **Sessions.** New `test-tamper` flag: the agent added a skip marker to a test, deleted a test file while tests failed, or changed an assertion and that edit alone turned a failing run green. It reads Edit/Write/apply_patch changes and shell writes (`sed -i`, `>`, `tee`, `rm`, `mv`, `git checkout`), and covers test-runner configs and CI files. New tests and files the agent created itself are not flagged. On 3,957 local sessions it flags 13 (EPISTEME, arXiv 2611.06563). [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare` links each tamper flag to the tool call that made the change and splits tampered passes by whether the agent said it was changing the test: `tampered passes  B 3: disclosed 2, stealth 1`, then one line per pass with the run, file, tool, event id and the agent's words. `--json` adds `tamperAttribution` per run, and the MCP `harness_compare` tool a `tamper` field. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab).
- **Judges.** New `no-test-tamper` checklist check, with no model call: it answers no when a test change hid a failure (a skip marker, a test deleted while red, an assertion edit that alone turned the tests green), cites the change and the test runs around it, and quotes what the agent said: `weaken test/sum.test.js by Edit in e42 (turned failing tests green), disclosed: "…"`. Saying why never makes it pass. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#checklists-and-trace-checks).
- **Evals.** `eval audit <task> --experiment <id> --witness <file> --witness-cmd <cmd>` replays a witness test, one that checks a requirement the verifier skips, on every passing run: `claude-tdd: passes 6/10, refuted 3, audited pass rate 30% (3/10)`. The witness must pass the reference 3/3 first. A replay whose verifier no longer passes is `replay-mismatch`, never refuted, and a witness refuting over half the passes is ambiguous and not counted. No model calls (TestJack, arXiv 2610.10619). `--promote` adds it to the verifier, and `eval export`/`import` carry witnesses. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#witness-audits).
- **Harness A/B.** After `eval audit`, `compare` shows each audited arm's raw and audited pass rate, `audited pass  B 60% → 30% (3 refuted, coverage 100%)`. `--audited` counts refuted passes as fails and labels the verdict `verdict (audited)`, with a note when the audit changes it. It needs every pass in both arms replayed, or the raw verdict stands with a coverage note. `--json` and the MCP `harness_compare` tool add the per-arm counts. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare` adds a `surface` row: the bytes of instruction text each bundle puts in front of the agent (AGENTS.md and the other instructions files, skills, rules, system-prompt files, `~/` ones too). When B's is more than 20% bigger, a note asks for a success CI above 0 rather than a tie, since those bytes cost tokens on every turn. `--json` has the sizes per file in `report.surface`. [Compare agents](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/how-to/compare-agents.md#4-read-the-result).
- **Evals.** Experiment sessions: `exp session new --name <topic>` starts one in a folder, and each `exp run` there prints its delta against the previous run on the same dataset as it finishes (`pass 0.0% → 100.0%  Δ +100.0pp […]  mcnemar p=0.125`, the grader deltas, the cases fixed and broken, the files changed), with no `exp compare`. `exp session note` records hypotheses and readings, `exp session summary [--json]` prints the timeline. `exp run --watch` reruns on every save of the spec, the agent script, grader files or `watch` globs, aborts a run made stale mid-way without storing it, and regrades the stored outputs with 0 agent calls when only the graders changed. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#sessions-deltas-notes-watch).
- **MCP.** `exp_session_note` adds a redacted note to the project's experiment session (the hypothesis before a run, the reading after it) and `exp_session_summary` returns its timeline, paged, with the same blocks as `exp session summary --json`, so an agent can pick an investigation back up after a context reset. Without a session both return a `not_found` error naming `coding.sh exp session new --name <topic>`. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#ask-your-agent).
- **Experiments.** `exp regrade <experiment> spec.json` grades a stored experiment's outputs again with new graders, without calling the task, and prints the pass rate before and after, each grader's version change and every run that flipped (`flips 1 of 4 runs: 1 pass → fail`). `exp compare` pins grader versions: a grader that changed version or graded one side only gets a warning above the verdict, and cases left ungraded on one side are listed. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#regrade).
- **Experiments.** `exp run matrix.json` runs a spec with `"matrix"` (JSON Pointer axes, every combination) or `"variants"` (named cells plus `"vary"`) against a baseline cell: the cells run interleaved, each is stored as its own experiment with `metadata.matrix`, each is compared with the baseline, and the McNemar p-values are Holm-adjusted over the cells. A cell that differs from the baseline outside `vary`, or touches graders or the dataset, is refused before any call; `--plan` prints the cells and runs nothing, `--cell` runs a subset, `--resume` continues an interrupted matrix. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#matrix).
- **Graders.** The `agent-slop` grader flags placeholder code, `as any` and `@ts-ignore`, empty catches, leftover debugging, focused or skipped tests and narrating comments in the lines an agent's diff adds, and `coding.sh judge slop <session|run|experiment>` scores captured work with it, per arm for a compare experiment. Verifiers get the agent's diff in `CODING_SH_AGENT_DIFF`. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#agent-slop).
- **Query.** `coding.sh sql "select …"` runs one read-only SQL query against the SQLite index: one `SELECT` or `WITH` statement on a read-only connection with `query_only` on, with a timeout (`--timeout`, 10 s) and a row cap (`--limit`, 1000). Writes, `ATTACH`, `PRAGMA` and a second statement exit 2; Postgres and ClickHouse stores get an error that points to `export`. `coding.sh export sessions|events|labels` streams rows as JSONL or CSV with the same filters as `sessions`, on every store. [Query and export](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md).
- **Privacy.** Sessions count what redaction replaced, by kind, in `stats.redactions` (counts only, never values; blobs and metadata mode included), and a session in which a vendor token was redacted gets the `secret:exposed` tag, since the agent and its model provider still saw it. `coding.sh sessions --flag secret:exposed` lists the credentials to rotate, and `show` prints a `Redacted` row. Sessions stored earlier are counted from their redaction markers. [Storage, sinks and privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md).
- **Privacy.** `coding.sh purge --literal <file>` removes a leaked secret from the SQLite store after the fact: event payloads and the search index, session titles and stats, labels, eval docs, settings, blobs (rewritten under a new hash, with references repointed) and the spool, then VACUUMs the index. Values come from a file or stdin and are never printed; `--dry-run` counts the copies. [Storage, sinks and privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md).
- **Cost.** `coding.sh usage` marks a cost that counts only some of a row's sessions as a lower bound (`≥$1.50`, replacing the `+` suffix), prints how many sessions have tokens but no cost, and names up to five unpriced models with the `[prices."<model>"]` fix. `--json` is unchanged. [Find expensive sessions](https://coding.sh/docs/cost).
- **Experiments.** `compare A B --reuse baseline` copies A's finished runs from earlier comparisons instead of running them again (`--reuse both` copies B's too), so several candidates against one baseline pay for it once. A run is reused only when its task, verifier and hidden files, bundle, agent version, attempt, timeout and route hash the same, it finished without an error, and it is newer than `--reuse-max-age` (default 7d). `--dry-run` prints how many runs each arm would reuse and run, and the report notes that reused runs did not run interleaved. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#reuse-finished-runs).
- **Evals.** An `executability` grader scores a run by how far it got before its first invalid tool call, one the harness refused to run (a bad argument, an unknown tool, an edit of a file not read yet, a string to replace that is not there, a missing file or command, a denied call). A test that exits 1 ran and is not invalid. The score is the share of steps before that call (its mean is AUC-PEC), and it passes with at most `maxInvalid` invalid calls (its pass rate is TER); `kinds` counts only some kinds (Prefix Executability, arXiv 2610.40444). [Explore guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#executability).
- **Query.** `sessions`, `search`, `usage` and `export` take `--where`, one filter language over any session or event field: `agent:codex cost>0.5 tool:Bash ok:false -flag:compaction`, with `OR`, `( )`, wildcards, ranges, `sort:` and `limit:`. `sessions --facets model,tool` counts sessions per value. The viewer API (`?where=`, `/api/facets`) and the MCP tools `sessions_list`, `search` and `usage` (`where`, `facets`) take the same queries. On SQLite a query is one parameterized SQL statement; a typo is a usage error that points at the character. [Query and export](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md#filter-with---where)
- **Query.** `--where` on tools, MCP servers, event kinds and `ok` reads indexes instead of scanning events, and their facet counts come from a small per-session table. On a 3.3 GB, 1.4M-event store, `tool:Bash ok:false` takes 5 ms instead of 26 s and the tool, MCP and kind facets 31 ms instead of 8.5 s. The store migrates once on open (about a minute at that size, +4% on disk). `GET /api/events` returns matching events across sessions, newest first and keyset-paged, and `/api/facets` takes `prefix` for autocomplete. [Query and export](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/query.md#filter-with---where)
- **Viewer.** The session list has one filter bar for the `--where` language: it completes keys and values (with session counts), runs as you type, and on Enter says what is wrong with a bad query and where. Clauses show as removable chips. The sidebar counts sessions per agent, model, repository, branch, flag, tool, MCP server and event kind; a click adds a value, Alt-click excludes it, and groups fold shut. The query is in the URL, and old `?agent=` and `?q=` links still open. Full-text hits moved out of the list; the list matches sessions by their text. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer)
- **Viewer.** An Events tab next to Sessions lists the events that match the query, newest first, with the session each belongs to and the matched words marked. Enter opens the event's step in its session, and `n` and `N` step through that session's other matches. `GET /api/events` takes `scope=top`. [Explore](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#open-the-viewer)
- **Privacy.** `coding.sh purge --rule <regex>` removes every match of a secret's shape (`sk-ant-api03-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{80,}`) the way `--literal` removes a value, without printing the rule or a match. Purge also scrubs eval run dirs (`~/.coding.sh/runs`), where a kept run holds the agent's transcripts. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md)
- **Experiments.** `exp run matrix.json --reuse baseline` copies the baseline cell's finished case-trials from earlier experiments instead of running them again, so several matrices against one baseline pay for it once (`--reuse all` copies any cell's). A case-trial is copied only when its case, task spec and files, graders, trial number and timeout all match, within `--reuse-max-age` (default 7d). `--plan` prints how many each cell would copy, and the comparisons warn that the copies did not run interleaved. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#matrix).
- **Compare.** `compare A B --experiment <id> --retry-errors` runs again, in place, only the runs of a stored comparison that errored or failed on infrastructure, and each task, arm and attempt counts once. `--failing <id>[@bundle]` runs only the tasks A failed in that comparison, with a note that the pass rates are conditioned on that failure. `--sample N --seed S` runs a seeded random sample of the tasks. `--json` adds `planned`, `skipped`, `retried` and `selection`, and the consent prompt counts only the runs still to go. [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#iterate-on-a-comparison).
- **Docs.** A grader field reference: every grader kind, its fields and defaults, and when it passes, with the provider fields judges share. [Grader fields](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#grader-fields)
- **Experiments.** `exp ab` and `refine` run on a Claude or ChatGPT subscription with `--provider claude-code` (`claude -p`) or `--provider codex` (`codex exec`), and pick one on their own when no API key is set and the CLI is logged in, saying so in one line. Each call runs without your hooks, plugins, MCP servers or settings, in a throwaway config dir; with `claude -p` the model gets the session's own Claude Code tools and a hook of ours refuses every call, so none runs. Calls run 2 at a time, rate limits back off and retry, and a spent usage limit stops the run with the finished trials kept. Costs show as API-equivalent, with no cap unless you pass `--max-cost`; `improve` and `improve fix` suggest the test on a subscription too. [Replaying on a subscription](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#replaying-on-a-subscription).
- **Judges.** LLM graders take `"provider": "codex"`, which grades through `codex exec` on a ChatGPT subscription. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#judges).
- **Experiments.** `exp ab` with no uncommitted harness edits tests your last commit that changed AGENTS.md, a rule or a skill against the version before it, `--dry-run` included, instead of stopping with "No uncommitted changes". [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#ab-a-harness-edit-on-your-own-sessions).

### Changed

- **Harness A/B.** The `harness changes` block of a `compare` report collapses a file list longer than 20 lines into directories with file counts (`+ .claude/skills/ (412 files)`). Before, comparing a full `~/.claude` against plain Claude Code printed one line per file, 1,500 of them. `--json` still lists every path in `report.bundleDiff`. [Harness A/B](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab).
- **Docs.** Long docs are split into pages that load faster: the CLI reference has a page per command, a long changelog release continues over numbered pages, Evals continues on [Experiments](https://coding.sh/docs/experiments) and [Harness evals](https://coding.sh/docs/harness-evals), and the Read API has its own two pages. Old links to a section that moved still land on it. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli)
- **Docs.** The vercel-security-audit case study has the live result: six read-only tasks against a real Vercel team, three runs per arm. Counting the 27 sessions that stayed on the requested model, graded with the fixed site check, 0/14 runs passed without the skill and 9/13 with it (+69.2 points, 95% CI +23.1 to +100, McNemar p = 0.25, gate Hold), at 6 times the cost per run. A Corrections section says what changed from the first 4/18 and 14/18: the `$LIVE_SITE` check, task 06's wording, and 9 sessions that Claude Code moved to an older model after a refusal. Aggregate numbers only. [Case study](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/case-studies/vercel-security-audit.md#live-run).
- **SDK.** The TypeScript SDK is published as `@codingdotsh/sdk` (npm org names can't contain a dot). Install it with `npm i @codingdotsh/sdk`. See [the SDK reference](docs/reference/sdk.md).
- **Experiments.** The item gate no longer accepts removing a skill or rule when the agent fails the exposed tasks without it; such items read UNCOVERED (neither arm solves them) or UNMEASURED (pass rate without the item under 0.8). The gate line shows `U=` and `A=` and a count of internalized, works, uncovered, hurts and between tasks (Wang et al., arXiv 2610.32322). [Evals guide](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab).
- **Experiments.** `exp run`, `exp aa` and `exp regrade` add each `--grader` to the spec file's graders instead of replacing them, so `exp run spec.json -g judge.json` grades with both; `--replace-graders` keeps the old behavior. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#datasets-and-experiments)

### Fixed

- **Sessions.** Replay knows that a file a step created did not exist before that step, instead of calling it unknown, so `replay --fork --run` removes it from the fork's copy. [Replay a session](https://coding.sh/docs/use-cases/replay-agent-session#how-files-are-rebuilt).
- **Evals.** `eval validate` applies a reference or near-miss patch after setup when it only applies to what setup creates, as an agent's run sees it. Before, a task whose setup made the mess the agent cleans up could not validate: its reference "did not apply". [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#hand-written-tasks).
- **Improve.** `improve check --repo <DIR|all>` counts the sessions that ran in other checkouts and worktrees of the repo. Before, it ignored `--repo` and counted only sessions whose repo root was the current checkout, so a repo whose agents work in worktrees showed every change as "No sessions yet". [Improve](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md).
- **Harness.** `harness audit` skips a file or directory it can't stat (a symlink loop, or a Windows junction Node can't follow) instead of failing with "unknown error". [Memory and instruction files](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/explore.md#memory-and-instruction-files).
- **Harness A/B.** `harness snapshot` follows linked skill, command and agent directories (`npx skills`, a dotfiles repo) and stores the files they point to. Before, a `~/.claude/skills` made of links snapshotted no skills, so the arm ran without them. [Harness A/B](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare` and `eval run` say why a failed run's agent failed: its exit code or timeout and the last lines of its stderr, redacted, under the report and in `failedRuns[].agent`. Before, an agent that couldn't start (Claude Code refusing bypass permissions as root, a bad flag) failed every run in seconds with only the verifier's output shown. [Verifier output](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#verifier-output).
- **Capture.** Backfill follows symlinked and junctioned transcript directories and files, and skips dangling links. Before, a `~/.claude/projects` made of links ingested nothing. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#known-breakage-to-design-around).
- **Harness A/B.** Two tasks from the same repo no longer error when they start at once: the first run of a repo set up its snapshot store while the second was reading it. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md).
- **Harness A/B.** The tamper check no longer flags a run that only added new test cases to an existing test file, so a regression test written next to the fix keeps the task in the gate's evidence. Changed, removed or skipped tests, early returns, hooks, mocks and shadowing definitions still flag. The run lists those files in `tamper.addedTests`. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab).
- **Sessions.** `premature-completion` no longer flags an agent that stopped on a failing test run and said so ("2 tests still fail", "I couldn't get test_x to pass", or a question about the failing test). It still flags one that says it is done, fixed or passing, or says nothing about the failure. Stored sessions get their flags recomputed once. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Site.** A page that shows "±" no longer downloads a second font file for it. [Site speed](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/perf.md)
- **Harness A/B.** `compare --gate` no longer says "Don't ship: B breaks a task A solved" when both arms passed it by editing tests. Tamper-flagged passes now count as fails for the gate in both arms, not only in B. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#harness-ab).
- **Experiments.** `exp ab` with no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `OPENAI_API_KEY` stops before the first trial with `missing_key` and points at `coding.sh improve check`, instead of running every case into an error and reporting "Hold: not enough evidence". `refine propose` without a key names the proposer, not the judge. [Improve your agent every week](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/improve.md#test-it-before-you-commit).
- **Rules.** An AGENTS.md line like "Run `npm test` before you say you are done, and never claim…" is read as a requirement to run `npm test`: the clause that holds the command decides, not a "never" later in the line.
- **Judges.** The Anthropic and OpenAI judge providers read `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, like the vendors' SDKs, so judges and drafts reach a proxy, gateway or local stub without `--base-url`. [Judges](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#judges).
- **Experiments.** When no attempt in either arm solves a task, `compare` suggests `coding.sh eval validate <task>` instead of an `eval curate` command that does not exist.
- **Refine.** `refine check`, `refine test` and `exp ab` judge only the new sentences of an edited line when looking for an edit that relaxes a protected line. Editing a one-line paragraph that already held a conditional ("use the CLI only if …") no longer rejects the edit. [Protected safety lines](https://coding.sh/docs/harness-evals#protected).
- **Examples.** The skill eval's live site check counts a network command that uses `$LIVE_SITE` as touching the site; it missed those, and passed runs that had probed the site. Scenario 06 no longer tells the agent to record open questions instead of waiting, since its pass is to stop and ask. [Skill eval example](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/tree/main/examples/skill-eval#live-run-a-real-vercel-team-read-only).
- **Privacy.** `coding.sh purge` never repeats what was typed on its command line: a secret pasted as an argument, a flag value, an unknown option or a `--literal` path that does not exist is refused with exit 2 and `(value not shown)`, instead of being printed to the terminal and logs. Other commands still name a stray argument. [Purge](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-purge).
- **Compare.** The `agent spend` and `grader spend` rows no longer count runs copied by `--reuse`; a `reused` row shows what those runs cost when they first ran, marked not spent. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#reuse-finished-runs).
- **Sessions.** Claude Code subagents get a title and a turn: the title is the Agent tool's `description`, else the first line of the prompt, and the prompt is recorded as the subagent's first message. Claude Code's internal forks (the compaction summarizer, prompt suggestions) no longer leave empty subagent sessions with no model, tools or title. Stored sessions are parsed again on the next ingest. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Capture.** Claude Code subagents that fire no SubagentStart or SubagentStop (`web-fetch`) are linked to their parent from the parent's `Agent` tool result, with their prompt, result, model and end, instead of showing as a top-level session with no prompt. [Adapters](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/ADAPTERS.md#subagents).
- **Sessions.** `skill:<name>` tags and `skill-copy-conflict` count only installed skills: a `skills/` folder under an agent's dot folder or in a plugin. A `skills/` folder in source code (`skills/mod.rs`), a reference checkout, text inside a heredoc and a write to a skill file no longer tag a skill. Codex's `.system/` and `synced/<id>/` folders are skipped, so `skill:.system` and `skill:synced` become the skill's own name, and a plugin named for its one skill is tagged once (`skill:frontend-design`). [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Sessions.** `test-tamper` no longer flags adding tests: a skip guard inside a new test, fixing a test the agent added earlier, updating an expectation its own source edit broke, or a green run of fewer tests than the failing one. A file a shell command's diff picked up without the command naming it (a test run, a `git rebase`, another agent in the checkout) is never flagged, and BSD `sed -i ''` no longer counts `''` as a file. A skip marker inside a string literal (a diff or `sed` command a test holds as data) is not a skip. Stored sessions are re-checked in the background after the upgrade. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md).
- **Sessions.** `coding.sh sessions` leaves out sessions with no turn, tool call or model call, such as a process that started and ended a session and did nothing; `--empty` lists them too, and `--limit` still fills with sessions that have work. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md).
- **Cost.** `usage` `TOK/TURN` (`tokensPerTurn`) is input, output and cache-write tokens per user turn. Cache reads are left out, because each model call re-reads the whole context, and `TURNS` counts only top-level sessions' turns, because a subagent's prompt comes from its parent's turn. [Expensive sessions](https://coding.sh/docs/cost).
- **Capture.** The background re-parse after an upgrade no longer blocks reads: it holds the ingest lock at most a second at a time and ingests new hook lines between holds, so `sessions` and the viewer answer in about a second instead of waiting 8 to 24 seconds. It writes blobs in batches, resumes where a cut-short run stopped, and works out stats and flags again for every stored session when an upgrade changes them. Listing a large spool is faster too. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md#old-sessions-show-doubled-counts).
- **Doctor.** Hooks in a project's config (`.claude/settings.local.json`, `.codex/hooks.json`, `.gemini/settings.json`, `.cursor/hooks.json`, …) show as `hooked in <file> (project)`, found in the current directory and in the directories the agent's recent sessions ran in. An agent with events in the last 5 minutes and no hook found reads `events arriving, hook not in user config`, never `not hooked`. A stale hook bin now says `run coding.sh update --refresh-bin` instead of `init`, and `coding.sh ingest` (which the hook starts at every session end) refreshes the bin itself after `npm i -g` of a newer version. [Troubleshooting](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/troubleshooting.md).
- **Viewer.** API sessions with a failed request are red in the session list again, with the reason: `1 failed request (429)`, the first error on hover. Sessions carry `stats.llmErrors`, counted at ingest (existing indexes are filled in on upgrade), and a list page costs the same store queries at any length.
- **Viewer.** The top bar fits from 320px up, in any font (on Linux without Geist it ran 2px past a 320px screen; a tab label now ends in an ellipsis before the page scrolls sideways): between 641 and 820px search folds to its icon instead of squeezing the theme button, and every top bar control has a 44px hit area.
- **Viewer.** The session title in the list keeps at least 12rem on any desktop width (at 1024px, next to the filter sidebar, it was 20px): tokens, then duration, then repo, then activity give way first.
- **Windows.** `coding.sh update` and the MCP launcher run npm and `coding.sh` without a shell, so paths with spaces or `&` arrive intact.
- **Windows.** Goose's config is found under `%APPDATA%\Block\goose\config`, where Goose keeps it on Windows. Eval runs find agent binaries on PATH (Windows spells it `Path`), and Amp and Pi hooks use the `node` on PATH, as elsewhere, instead of the one that ran `init`.
- **Aider.** Eval runs pass `--no-show-release-notes`: on a new version's first run, `--yes-always` made aider open its release notes in a browser.
- **Codex.** Code mode: a command run inside an `exec` cell now points at the cell, the cell fails when a command in it fails, and session stats count each command once instead of twice (hooks and backfill agree).
- **Viewer.** A big ingest no longer blanks reads: `coding.sh ui` opens at once (0.3 s, was 41 s on a 25k-line spool) and indexes in a background worker with an "Indexing N files…" pill, and `status`, `sessions` and `search` answer within 1.5 s, noting what is still pending (`--fresh` waits). [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md).
- **Capture.** A full ingest of a 25k-line spool takes 4 s instead of 294 s on macOS, and 39 s instead of 153 s on Linux: blobs are made durable once per checkpoint instead of with two fsyncs per line, and each checkpoint commits its events, session rows and spool cursors in one transaction, so a crash mid-drain neither loses nor duplicates events.
- **Cost.** `coding.sh collector` prices model calls with tokens and no cost, as `serve` and `ingest` do, using your `[prices]`.
- **Viewer.** The top bar fits a 360px phone.
- **MCP.** Tool calls no longer wait for a big ingest: they answer within 1.5 s from what is indexed, with `pending: {files, indexing}` in the result. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-mcp).
- **Harness A/B.** `compare` and `eval crosscheck` runs keep the agent's text from mid-turn: the run dir, which holds the agent's own transcript, was removed before it was ingested, so the session kept only the final reply.
- **Examples.** The skill eval's live mode grades fairly: `--help` and `help` calls never count as writes, a refused `env ls` reads "refused secret read", live task copies name the team, project and site only through `$VERCEL_SCOPE`, `$VERCEL_PROJECT` and `$LIVE_SITE`, the site checks match the team's real domains (kept from the agent), and `out/checks` records each run's task and arm. `run.sh <out-dir> --regrade` grades stored runs again without calling the agent. [Evaluate an Agent Skill](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/evaluate-a-skill#2-stub-what-the-skill-touches).
- **CLI.** `status`, `sessions` and `search` with `--json` carry `"pending": {"files", "indexing"}` while captures are still waiting, instead of dropping the note. [CLI reference](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/reference/cli.md#codingsh-status).
- **Evals.** A command task, grader or source that crashes names the error and its cause, such as `exited 1: TypeError: fetch failed (cause: connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:8803)`, instead of the last three lines of stderr (for Node, the closing braces of the error object). A refused connection now reads as an infrastructure error, so the trial is retried and counted as infra, not as a failure. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Evals.** A task drafted from a session names the files its setup was guessed from, such as `setup guessed from package.json (no lockfile): npm install`, instead of saying "from lockfiles" when there was none.
- **Experiments.** A grader error leaves a run ungraded only when no other grader failed it. With `exact` and a judge that is down, the runs `exact` failed count as failures and only the runs it passed are left out, instead of every run reading ungraded and the pass rate going blank. `exp regrade` follows the same rule. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Evals.** `eval run` reports on a cases suite say "case" and on a triggers suite "prompt" (the header, the table, never-passed, saturation and the regression lines), and `--json` carries the report's `unit`.
- **Evals.** `eval validate` shows the last 12 lines of the verifier's output when a check goes the wrong way (the reference fails, the no-op or a near-miss passes, or setup fails), once per kind of check, and `--json` carries every step with its output in `steps`. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Evals.** `eval init --force` no longer deletes your work: it stops when the suite holds a file `eval init` did not write or that you changed since (a `reference.patch` you added, a prompt you fixed) and lists them. `--force-all` deletes those too. `evals/<name>/.eval-init.json` records what `eval init` wrote. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#your-agents-failed-sessions)
- **Evals.** `eval init --skill <dir>` uses the skill's own `triggers.toml` when it has one and you give no prompts and no `--draft`: `suite.json` points at it instead of a TODO template. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#a-skill)
- **Evals.** `eval init --sessions` and `--session latest` include a session that just ended: they wait up to 30 seconds for the ingest its hook started, then ingest the rest, instead of skipping the ingest when its lock is busy after 1.5 seconds. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#your-agents-failed-sessions)
- **Graders.** When a command grader, command task or bench source crashes on Node 24.22, its reason names the error again (`TypeError: fetch failed (cause: connect ECONNREFUSED …)`) instead of the crash's closing braces. Node 24.22 prints an uncaught fetch failure as `[TypeError: fetch failed] {`. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md)
- **Store.** A SQLite index that fails to open (a newer schema, a failed migration) no longer stays open in the process that tried, so on Windows the file can be replaced or removed right away. [Concepts](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/concepts.md)
- **Evals.** On Windows, `eval init` and `eval run` show suite paths with forward slashes, like the file list under them: `Wrote … to evals/triage`, `evals/pdf/triggers.toml still say TODO` and the `--skill` path in the `--draft --force` hint read the same on every OS. [Evals](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/evals.md#a-skill)

## [0.1.1] - 2026-11-07

Daily benchmarks for any AI agent: `coding.sh bench` runs every contender on fresh cases each day,
and experiments add cluster bootstrap, an A/A noise floor, interleaved runs, early stopping and `compare --without`.
Seven more agents are verified on a real install, the viewer and the site work on phones, and the installer uses npm once the package is there.

### Added

- **Benchmarks.** `coding.sh bench` samples fresh cases each day from a command, failed sessions, GitHub issues, a sitemap or a dataset, freezes them as a dataset version and runs every contender on that set in random order. [Benchmarks](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/benchmarks.md).
- **Benchmarks.** Shared timeout and retries, a budget cap, quota and rate-limit cases dropped for every contender, and `bench run --resume` to finish a stopped run with the same seed and cases.
- **Benchmarks.** `bench report` writes a static HTML page and JSON: trend, gaps to a reference pooled over a fixed window, head-to-head stats, difficulty and failures. `bench regrade` re-scores stored outputs and `bench schedule` prints a cron line and a GitHub Actions workflow.
- **Benchmarks.** An `[anchor]` set of fixed cases runs every day next to the fresh ones and is scored apart, so its trend has no day-to-day difficulty in it.
- **Benchmarks.** `seed = "secret"` publishes the hash of each next day's salt and `bench show` checks the chain. Content hashes keep the same case under a new key from counting as fresh.
- **Benchmarks.** Input and output tokens are kept apart, and each cost says where it came from: reported, `costPerRun`, the shared price table, or unknown.
- **MCP.** A `benchmarks` tool lists benchmarks and reads results and history.
- **Experiments.** `exp compare --cluster <key>` resamples by repo or session and warns under 10 clusters. [Evals](https://coding.sh/docs/evals).
- **Experiments.** `exp aa` measures the run-to-run noise floor, and power planning uses it.
- **Experiments.** Compare flags uneven attrition between arms (Fisher exact) and splits infra errors from agent errors.
- **Experiments.** Under 100 paired cases, compare also reports P(B>A) from a paired posterior.
- **Experiments.** `exp run --arm a=<task> --arm b=<task>` interleaves both arms case by case in a seeded random order.
- **Experiments.** `--stop-early` checks at planned looks with O'Brien-Fleming alpha spending and reports a repeated confidence interval.
- **Experiments.** Pairwise judges see both outputs in both orders. `exp compare --pairwise` reports win rate, ties and position bias.
- **Experiments.** `exp sweep` races task configs on a train split and reports the winner's optimism gap on held-out cases.
- **Compare.** `compare <bundle> --without <path>` measures what one skill, AGENTS.md or MCP config is worth. [Compare agents](https://coding.sh/docs/how-to/compare-agents).
- **Compare.** With 2+ attempts per task, failed runs split into bad runs, arm limits and tasks no arm solves, with variance shares.
- **Compare.** Cost rows show how many runs had a known cost, and "cheaper" needs a known cost for 80% of each arm.
- **Agents.** Verified on a real install against a local stub model: opencode, copilot-cli, droid, goose, pi, t3code and aider. [Agent matrix](https://coding.sh/docs/agents).
- **Agents.** Antigravity backfill reads agy transcripts and conversation DBs for model, tokens, cache reads and latency per call. Tool steps missing from the transcript count as failures, and hooks reply `{}`.
- **Viewer.** Phone layouts: 44px targets, filters in a bottom sheet, a compact session header and safe-area insets.
- **Viewer.** The Benchmarks tab shows anchors, pooled gaps, difficulty, cost sources and tokens, and ranks follow the pooled gaps.
- **Site.** Mobile pass: touch targets, safe areas, stacked agent cards and 16px body text on phones.
- **Site.** A new landing page, a changelog page and docs pages.
- **Install.** `install.sh` installs from npm when the registry has the version, else from the GitHub release. `CODING_SH_SOURCE=npm|github` picks one.
- **Release.** A tag creates its GitHub release as soon as the checks pass, so the installer gets it right away.
- **Release.** The release workflow can publish all five packages to npm and PyPI with trusted publishing. Nothing is on npm or PyPI yet.
- **Docs.** A new README, reference pages, how-to guides and a code of conduct.

### Fixed

- **Fixes.** Safer installs: symlinked and JSONC agent configs survive, `init --dry-run` prints a short plan, and blockers show before the Y/n.
- **Fixes.** Each SQLite migration runs once when the hook and the CLI open a new index at the same time.
- **Fixes.** Experiments stop cleanly on Ctrl-C, kill their child processes and keep every finished run.
- **Fixes.** Capture, ingest and storage state bugs from an audit of each state machine. Sessions are priced locally at ingest.
- **Fixes.** Adapter fixes for opencode, goose, copilot-cli, droid, aider and t3code, found running each on a real install.
- **Fixes.** `coding.sh run --agent <name>` records sessions for names with no adapter.
- **Fixes.** `exp aa` refuses to compare a run with itself.
- **Fixes.** `coding.sh ui` prints the viewer URL once in a terminal.
- **Fixes.** Muted text in the viewer passes WCAG AA in both themes.
- **Fixes.** Timing tests compare against a baseline, so a loaded machine no longer fails them.

## [0.1.0] - 2026-11-06

The first public release, still pre-1.0. coding.sh records what your AI agents do, with coding
agents first-class, on your own machine, shows it in a local viewer, and turns those sessions into evals and A/B experiments.
It also runs as a server on SQLite or Postgres, with SDKs for apps that call model APIs.

### Added

- **Install.** `curl -fsSL https://coding.sh/install | sh` checks for Node 22.15+, installs with npm, runs `init` and opens the viewer.
- **Install.** Or `npm install -g coding.sh && coding.sh init`. `init` shows one plan and asks one Y/n. [Install guide](https://coding.sh/docs/install).
- **Install.** `coding.sh` with no command opens the viewer, and sets up capture on its first run.
- **Install.** Commands print one dim line when a release is out, checked in the background at most once a day.
- **Install.** `coding.sh update` installs the new release. The check is off in CI and [can be turned off](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/install.md).
- **Install.** `coding.sh uninstall` restores each agent config byte for byte if nothing else has edited it since.
- **Capture.** Hooks record prompts, tool calls, file changes, turns and errors to a local spool, then ingest them into sessions.
- **Capture.** Secrets are redacted before anything is stored. [Privacy](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/blob/main/docs/guides/privacy.md).
- **Capture.** `run` spools secret flag values (`--openai-api-key X`, `--api-key=X`) redacted, and ingest redacts them in any argv.
- **Capture.** Pi, Goose, Copilot CLI, Droid, Gemini CLI and Antigravity sessions seen by hooks and by `ingest --backfill` store each call, reply and turn once, and count tokens once.
- **Capture.** A session seen by hooks and by `ingest --backfill` keeps one session start and one end, for every agent.
- **Capture.** A Claude Code or Codex compaction seen by hooks and by `ingest --backfill` is stored once; Codex transcripts no longer record each compaction twice.
- **Capture.** Cursor prompts and replies seen by hooks and by `ingest --backfill` are stored once, though Cursor transcripts carry no timestamps.
- **Capture.** `coding.sh run -- <cmd>` wraps any agent and snapshots the tree before and after in shadow git, not your repo.
- **Capture.** Every model call becomes an `llm.call` event with model, tokens, duration and time to first token.
- **Capture.** Subagent starts and ends are captured and linked to their child sessions.
- **Capture.** No ingest step: reads, the viewer and MCP ingest new events first; a session end starts a locked background ingest.
- **Capture.** `coding.sh collector` takes OTLP/HTTP traces and logs, protobuf or JSON, on 127.0.0.1:4318. [OTLP setup](https://coding.sh/docs/otel).
- **Capture.** The receiver reads OTel GenAI semconv, OpenInference, the Vercel AI SDK, and Claude Code, Codex and Gemini CLI logs.
- **Agents.** Verified on real sessions: Claude Code, Codex and Antigravity. [Agent matrix](https://coding.sh/docs/agents).
- **Agents.** Checked in CI against recorded payloads, not run live yet: opencode, cursor, gemini-cli, copilot-cli, amp, pi, droid, goose, t3code and aider.
- **Agents.** Every adapter has fixture tests, and an `ingest --backfill` path where the agent keeps history.
- **Agents.** T3 Code threads import from its local store; Codex and Claude Code runs in T3 count once, agent in `provider`.
- **Agents.** `coding.sh usage --by agent-model` compares tokens per turn and cache-read share across harnesses.
- **Cost.** Calls with tokens and no cost get one from a dated price table (Claude, OpenAI, Gemini, open models), marked estimated; billed and reported costs win. [Where cost comes from](https://coding.sh/docs/cost#where-cost-comes-from).
- **Cost.** `[prices."<model>"]` in config.toml sets your own rates; `usage --reprice` recomputes stored estimates.
- **Cost.** A model call nested in another, or a repeated response id, counts once in session totals.
- **Storage.** One SQLite index (node:sqlite, no native deps) and a content-addressed, zstd-compressed blob store. [Storage](https://coding.sh/docs/storage).
- **Storage.** OTLP and HTTP sinks send metadata only by default.
- **Viewer.** `coding.sh ui` serves a local viewer: sessions, search, a timeline of turns, tools and subagents, and outcome labels.
- **MCP.** `coding.sh mcp` lets agents query sessions, failures, latency, usage, subagents and experiments over stdio. [MCP](https://coding.sh/docs/mcp).
- **MCP.** `init` registers it with Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, OpenCode and Gemini CLI. `--no-mcp` skips that.
- **CLI.** 27 commands, from `init` and `sessions` to `eval`, `exp` and `serve`, all with `--json` output. [CLI reference](https://coding.sh/docs/cli).
- **Evals.** Repo-replay tasks start from the snapshot taken before the agent ran. [Evals](https://coding.sh/docs/evals).
- **Evals.** `eval validate` checks verifiers: a do-nothing run fails, the reference fix passes k of k, near-misses fail, nothing leaks.
- **Evals.** Datasets with deterministic graders, command graders and LLM judges.
- **Evals.** Judges are calibrated on human labels with Cohen's kappa on a held-out split. Reports flag uncalibrated judges.
- **Evals.** Experiments run two arms on the same cases: a paired bootstrap CI of the difference and exact McNemar on flips.
- **Evals.** Harness compare adds per-arm Wilson intervals and the first step where the traces diverge.
- **Evals.** [Tool evals](https://coding.sh/docs/tool-evals) for CLI and MCP surfaces: description size, schema checks, per-task tool-call metrics in a sandbox.
- **Production.** `coding.sh serve` runs ingest (OTLP/HTTP and `POST /v1/ingest`), the viewer and its API on one port. [Production](https://coding.sh/docs/production).
- **Production.** Set the store with `CODING_SH_STORE_URL` or `DATABASE_URL`, and blobs with `CODING_SH_BLOB_URL` (file, postgres, s3).
- **Production.** Writes are acked after commit, a full queue answers 429 with `Retry-After`, and retries never duplicate.
- **Production.** Ingest keys pick the project and read keys open the viewer. `serve keygen` and `serve healthcheck` help.
- **Production.** `/healthz`, `/readyz`, Prometheus `/metrics`, and one writer lock with `--role viewer` replicas.
- **Production.** `CODING_SH_RETENTION_DAYS` sets retention and `CODING_SH_CAPTURE_MODE=metadata` drops message text and tool I/O.
- **Production.** `coding.sh-store-postgres` keeps the index, and blobs by default, in Postgres. Passes the store conformance suite.
- **Production.** Store URLs take `sqlite://`, `postgres://` or `postgresql://`.
- **Production.** `coding.sh-store-s3` keeps blobs in S3, R2 or MinIO. SigV4, no AWS SDK, credentials only from `AWS_*` env vars.
- **Production.** A Dockerfile with both store plugins, a Compose stack (serve, Postgres, MinIO), and examples in examples/production.
- **Production.** Deploy recipes for Fly, Railway and Render. [Deploy guides](https://github.com/jackulau/coding.sh/tree/main/docs/production).
- **Production.** `--store <url>` or `CODING_SH_STORE_URL` points `sessions`, `show`, `search`, `mcp`, `dataset` and `exp` at the serve database.
- **Production.** `sessions` and `search` take `--project`.
- **SDKs.** `@codingdotsh/sdk` for TypeScript: `observe(client)` for OpenAI and Anthropic, `observeFetch()`, `aiSdkTracer()`, `withSession`.
- **SDKs.** `coding-sh` for Python: `observe(client)` and OTel setup.
- **SDKs.** Both batch, retry on 429 and never throw into your app. Not on npm or PyPI yet.
- **Integrations.** Sessions sent through the Vercel AI Gateway are tagged `gateway:vercel`. [AI Gateway](https://coding.sh/docs/ai-gateway).
- **Integrations.** `coding.sh gateway status|enrich` adds billed cost, serving provider, client session join and the `vmc/` model per call.
- **Integrations.** The receiver maps gateway trace drains and AI SDK `gateway` spans.
- **Integrations.** `compare --gateway` and the `gateway` grader provider run arms across models with one key.
- **Integrations.** eve: span enrichment, a read-only importer for its local traces, and an experiment task that drives a local agent.
- **Docs.** README with a quickstart, the agent matrix, OTLP setup, storage, privacy and uninstall.
- **Docs.** CONTRIBUTING, SECURITY and a release checklist in docs/RELEASE.md.
